<a id="srs-1"></a>
## 1. Purpose and key decisions

Sketch2Life retains two product flows, Parent and Teacher, now placed on a shared lesson plan foundation layer:

1. **Parent:** an Android application; a self-entered/selected topic and a public/shared lesson are optional. If a lesson is selected, the Parent may view the knowledge presentation and the guide of the lesson; a general guide may also be enabled by topic before drawing. A drawing image of exactly one child is mandatory; afterwards the VLM recognizes the drawing and checks its suitability against the topic/lesson, the adult confirms the subject, selects one main activity before approving the script, then a new video is created from the child's own drawing. After the video, the pinned lesson displays all the activities of the lesson; without a pinned lesson, the selected main activity is displayed.
2. **Teacher:** a web application; a school is an organization managing classes and profiles. The Teacher creates a versioned lesson/presentation, filters uploaded content, then reviews it before use; may edit content and slide order, add uploaded images, videos embedded from YouTube/Vimeo, SVG catalog items, or AI-generated SVG drawings in draft status. The guide draws progressively from basic strokes to a recognizable figure. During a session, the Teacher controls the presentation/guide; the flow of selecting a child, confirming context and creating a sample video from one child's drawing still follows current policy.

Each story run is bound to exactly one child. Parent and Teacher accounts remain separate; a child profile may be shared through invitations and scoped, revocable permissions, without merging the two accounts or making the Parent a member of an organization. Each school is an organization/tenant. There is no classroom collaboration runtime, shared drawing canvas, QR/lobby/admission, batch processing of multiple children, or drawing animation using PixiJS.

The current AI direction is clearly classified in §§6.3 and 9.3: Qwen3.5-VL for drawing understanding, story/storyboard and structured vector drafts; the SVG catalog together with a deterministic engine is responsible for parsing, validating, storing canonical vector data and rendering SVG for line-sketches/illustrations; FLUX is not part of the target direction and must not be used as a fallback; the only exception is the optional drawing-guide picture a Teacher requests for the Sketch Deck (Rev75, §31.21). Whisper is the ASR direction; SAM 2.1 is an optional segmentation; Modal is the inference hosting direction during the development phase. These names do not confirm checkpoint/runtime/production approval or end-to-end integration. The LTX-2.5/Modal L40S video pipeline per Q144 remains unchanged.

The only approval gate before calling video creation is the adult confirming the final revision of the script/storyboard. The transcript, subject, activity, lesson plan and profile context each have a separate review/confirmation step before the story is created. The completed video is displayed after passing technical checks and the automatic safety gate; there is no final video approval step. If the adult marks the video as unsuitable, the old video is hidden from playback as soon as the regeneration request is accepted; the run returns to the script approval step. The old version is retained internally until the run is deleted.

### 1.1 Target table counts

The historical design baseline v2.3 is **89 active tables + 48 legacy = 137 expected tables**. Before Rev31, the repository source inventory recorded **86 mappings**; Rev31 adds three Lesson support mappings, raising the current source crosswalk to **89 mappings** (38 retained/refactored + 48 legacy/read-only + 3 Lesson support). This is a count of source mappings, not the current number of active tables or of physical databases. The current target is **92 active** (38 retained/refactored + 54 new) and **48 legacy/read-only**, for a total of **140 expected physical mappings**. Four helper/snapshot tables are excluded from the active graph; 13 AI Policy provider-copy/resource receipt mappings remain legacy/read-only. Five fixtures are excluded. The 92/140 figure is a source-declared model, not the final DDL or an inventory of the production database.

| Active tables in the current target | 92 | 38 retained/refactored + 54 new; source-derived target, not an inventory of the running database |
|---|---:|---|
| Active tables in the current target | 92 | 38 retained/refactored + 54 new; source-derived target, not an inventory of the running database |
| Fixture tables used for testing only | 5 | Not production tables; excluded from all calculations |
| Source mappings in the current crosswalk after Rev31 | 89 | 86 pre-Rev31 + 3 Lesson support; including 38 retained/refactored, 48 legacy and 3 Lesson support |
| Existing tables retained/standardized | 38 | Current tables still needed for the product and the target platform |
| New tables in the current target model | 54 | Parent/profile data, lesson library/sharing/feedback, Sketch Deck, Story, quota/billing, notification, upload, asset and AI job |
| Total currently active tables | **92** | 38 + 54; synchronized with the Data Table Register |
| Read-only legacy tables | 48 | Including 35 old workflow tables and 13 deferred AI Policy tables; kept in place |
| Expected physical production tables under the current target | **140** | 92 active + 48 legacy; not a DDL/prod inventory |

Rev31 implementation crosswalk adds three Lesson-owned support tables beyond the historical v2.3 design (89/137). The current companion target and source-derived crosswalk are both 92 active + 48 legacy/read-only = 140 projected physical mappings. Neither count is a live database observation or final physical schema; see Data Table Register §§1 and 14.4. The local 41-table snapshot in §32 remains environment-specific.

DATA_TABLE_REGISTER.md records the source disposition, target table name, data owner and constraints. This design does not drop tables and does not copy data to a second archive store.

### 1.2 Decision status

The historical v2.3 design baseline is 89 active + 48 legacy = 137; the current source crosswalk is 89 mappings (86 pre-Rev31 + 3 Lesson support); the current target is 92 active + 48 legacy = 140 expected mappings per the Data Table Register. Rev43 performed a read-only check of the local development database and recorded 41 application tables; this snapshot is not production and does not complete the migration inventory. The four numbers have different scopes, explained in §32 and DATA_TABLE_REGISTER.md.

<a id="srs-1-document"></a>
### 1.3 Scope and document structure

This is a consolidated Markdown package of the SRS, target architecture (SAD-level), operations and verification strategy, at the owner's request to approve a complete baseline together with the data register. Normative requirements reside in BR/FR/NFR; the technology, entity, API, job/ops content in the corresponding sections must be read according to `proposed`/`gate` status, not as implementation fact. After the owner approves the content, the architecture, runbook and test strategy chapters may be split into separate files while preserving IDs/traceability; the document is not yet split to avoid divergence among multiple source copies.

Requirements reference material is cross-checked against ISO/IEC/IEEE 29148:2018; IEEE 830-1998 is a legacy practice that has been superseded by 29148. This version uses the structure and requirement attributes as guidance and does not claim full standard compliance certification. The visual diagrams section is not yet in the Markdown because the owner requested that it be created only after the SRS and register are approved.

<a id="srs-2"></a>
## 2. Scope

### 2.1 In scope

- Restore/migrate the legacy Parent flow to the current Android platform.
- Retain the target requirements for Parent Android, Teacher web and the image/video generation pipeline; the lesson, content and authorization platform is an architectural addition. This does not assert that the client/API/provider flows have been completed in the current code.
- Separate Parent and Teacher accounts; support sharing a child profile via invitation, minimum permission scope, consent, audit and revoke.
- Each school is one organization/tenant; separate the platform Admin, school Manager, Teacher and Parent permissions.
- A reusable lesson library in preset form, with versions and private/school/selected/public visibility; lesson content is independent of the actual teaching occurrence.
- A standardized Teacher authoring form and template upload; keep original files private, filter/extract via an allowlist before AI, and the Teacher views/edits the structured version before use.
- Teachers/Managers may create or review lesson drafts according to the permissions that are finalized; Parents use public/shared lessons and may edit a personal copy but must not edit the published original.
- Parents may view lessons already taught for a shared profile within the configured history period, download lessons they have been granted access to, and send feedback; there is no chat, completion toggle or drawing upload to feedback.
- The child age range is 3–10 (36–120 months), divided into three versioned age bands: 36–71, 72–107 and 108–120 months. The profile/run keeps the band version used; the exact date of birth is not stored.
- One lesson occurrence per teaching session and at most one successful sample video for that occurrence; each run selects exactly one child.
- The Teacher guide and the Parent line-sketch guide are optional; when turned off, no guide-generation job is created. The Teacher lesson/presentation is editable content, while the source SVG/guide keeps its own provenance and revision.
- The Parent guide uses the SVG catalog first; when no suitable guide is available, an AI draft may be requested if the capability is enabled. A guide has 4–8 adaptive steps, going from basic strokes to a recognizable shape; adults may skip it. The guide is not the child's finished drawing and does not accept the child's profile, images, voice or identifying data.
- The Teacher lesson presentation supports editing slide content and order, image upload, embedded video from YouTube/Vimeo, the SVG catalog and SVG AI drafts. Slide/media placement belongs to the draft version; the Teacher reviews before submitting, the Manager approves at school scope, and the Platform Admin approves platform-wide public release according to permissions.
- Storytelling video from a child's drawing follows the pipeline: VLM image understanding → LLM creates a structured story → storyboard → adult reviews/confirms the script → prepare Character Bible and reference images → LTX-2.5 on Modal per scene (target per Q144; WAN is only an experimental candidate) → consistency check → FFmpeg scene stitching → generate/synchronize TTS and subtitles → automated safety → separate playback within the run. The development direction for VLM/story is Qwen3.5-VL; ASR is Whisper; the TTS model/checkpoint and the quality/runtime gates remain open.
- New AI line-sketches/illustrations use structured vector output that is validated and serialized by the SVG engine; FLUX.3 is not used, except for the optional drawing-guide picture a Teacher requests for the Sketch Deck (§31.21). Qwen3.5-VL is the direction for creating drafts when the catalog has no suitable source, subject to POC/quality gates. SAM 2.1 remains an optional segmentation choice; this is a target direction, not evidence that the guide/deck has been wired to the API/runtime.
- Camera capture/image upload, ideas by text/voice, viewing and editing the Whisper transcript, subject recognition/adjustment, personalized story creation, script approval, creating a new video from the drawing, and then displaying the outdoor activities configured in the lesson.
- Profile revision, the approved context snapshot for each run, provenance, deletion lifecycle, and the constraint of using only synthetic data for the demo.
- An inventory of source tables across the whole repository and a target data design that does not destroy existing data.
- Clean architecture, versioned contracts, standardized error codes, monitoring, rate limits, local Docker operations, and model inference via Modal.
- Organization capacity includes 10 class slots in the paid basic plan; additional slots may be purchased at a per-slot unit price via MoMo/VNPAY in the initial adapter.
- Video quota has two separate owner scopes: the school organization and the Parent account. A Parent buys quota for the Parent account; a School Manager who is assigned buys for the organization and allocates internal limits to Teachers who currently belong to the school. Teacher usage counts against the school quota per occurrence; a Teacher has no right to purchase and no entitlement owner of their own, and there is no cross-scope fallback. The Platform Admin manages plan/price versions. The monthly plan allows manual payment or automatic renewal after the purchaser authorizes it; a top-up is a one-time transaction, does not auto-renew, and does not expire until used up. An unused Teacher allocation is automatically returned to the School balance when the Teacher leaves the assignment with the organization; usage already consumed remains with the School. Quota is not refunded after a successful payment. The temporary review levels are Parent 2 uses/month and top-up 5 uses/₫249.000; School 30 uses/month and top-up 50 uses/₫1.990.000. These figures/prices are review assumptions only, not commercial configuration or production prices. The failed-renewal rules and the behavior on role change other than the Teacher-leaves-organization event remain open.

### 2.2 Out of scope

- Classroom collaboration sessions, QR, lobby/admission, multiple children in one run, group canvas, shared drawing, or collaborative stage-based progress.
- Drawing animation with PixiJS.
- Teacher AI Sketch Deck in the Parent flow; pre-selecting the subject of the child's drawing before capture. The Parent AI line-sketch guide is a separate option, does not take child data as input, and does not block capture.
- Exporting the presentation to PPTX/PDF or using slides as a collaborative canvas on devices; the Teacher presentation is created/edited and presented within the application/web.
- Merging Parent/Teacher identities into one account; sharing profiles within a family outside the defined grant flow; sharing a Parent/Teacher account.
- OCR of scanned lesson plan pages, extracting images into lesson content, Parents submitting their own activities, or directly editing each AI-generated scene.
- Final video review, downloading/exporting files from the product, sharing videos, public gallery, ranking/comparing children.
- Finalizing the production host/region, production email provider, production SLO/alert addresses, specific commercial price levels, specific AI model/weights revisions, or live payment information.
- Destructive schema cleanup, running migrations, deploying code, running product build/tests, using real child data, live external payments, or calling real providers/Modal.

<a id="srs-2-context"></a>
### 2.3 Stakeholders, system context and users

| Stakeholder | Concerns/responsibilities | Information to be provided |
|---|---|---|
| Product owner | Scope, experience, the one-child/one-run rule, approved content | Parent/Teacher flows, quota, states, acceptance criteria |
| Platform admin | Platform-wide roles, public lesson library, moderation/hide and quota policy | Audit, provenance, review/publish state and clear access permissions |
| School Manager | One organization/school, member groups, quota, public lesson review and feedback within the school | Tenant-based permissions, review history and feedback; media not granted by default |
| Parent/guardian | Child profile, sharing consent, lessons used with the child, personal video and feedback | Grant status/revoke, quota, limited lesson history and notifications |
| Responsible teacher | Reusable lesson, occurrence, one sample child, optional sketch and Parent feedback | Filtered upload, shared profile scope and current class permissions |
| Platform Admin with content-review permission | Lesson library and shared safety policy | Review/publish/moderate permission, revision, source and revocation history; not a separate product role |
| Operations/engineering | Service health, queue, model/provider, cost and recovery | Redacted metrics, correlation ID, runbook, limits/circuit breaker |
| Privacy/legal/commercial reviewer | Child data, controller/processor roles, transfer, retention, payment/tax | Evidence and production approval decisions; not yet considered complete |

**Target users and environment:** A Parent is an adult using an Android phone who may be unfamiliar with AI terminology; a preschool Teacher works on a desktop/laptop via a browser while preparing for a lesson; a School Manager uses the web with billing/roster permissions per organization. V1 uses Vietnamese. If the camera/microphone is denied or not available, an alternative of image upload and text input must be provided. The repo currently declares `minSdk 29` (Android 10), `targetSdk 36`, `compileSdk 37`; the browser target is the two most recent stable versions of desktop Chrome/Edge and must be re-verified in the release gate.

**Integration context in text form** (the diagram is still deferred until after the Markdown is approved):

| External component/system | Communication | Data and boundary | Conditions |
|---|---|---|---|
| Parent Android and Teacher Web | HTTPS to the versioned API `/v1`; Firebase ID token | Only commands/asset upload/status; no DB, MinIO, payment or Modal credentials | The backend verifies the token and the permission for each resource |
| Firebase Authentication | Client/backend identity authentication | Issuer/UID; does not store profile, media, notification or product data | The repository permits only Firebase Authentication |
| Modal | Backend worker calls asynchronously; small references and files via isolated scratch | Input/output limited per job, checksum, scratch deleted after processing | Synthetic-only; review region/transfer before real data |
| MoMo/VNPAY | Provider callback to the backend; client redirect only displays status | Signed payload, provider event ID, invoice/payment refs | Signature, replay protection, dedupe and reconciliation via sandbox |
| Notifications | Inbox, email, Web Push, Android adapter | Minimal payload/opaque reference; no child name/media/story | Android delivery is a POC gate; Firebase Messaging is not used |
| PostgreSQL/Redis-RQ/MinIO | Internal Docker network | DB as the canonical source; Redis dispatch/rate-limit; private object storage | Local synthetic; manual backup/restore |
| OTel/Prometheus/Grafana/Mailpit | Internal telemetry/development | Redacted logs/metrics; local Mailpit | Production destinations and retention not yet chosen |

| User group | Assumed device/skills | Needs and accessibility |
|---|---|---|
| Parent | Android, touch, basic digital skills | Choose a lesson, turn the guide on/off, draw/capture, confirm subject/script, watch the video then the activity; errors come with a next action |
| Teacher | Chrome/Edge desktop, keyboard/mouse, fast operation | Import/review lesson, choose profile/activity, capture or receive an uploaded image, show the sample after confirmation |
| School Manager | Web desktop, organizational/quota skills | Manage groups, assignment, public review and quota; does not view media by default |
| Platform Admin | Web desktop, platform administration permission | Author public lessons; another Platform Admin with Content Admin permission reviews before publishing; manage roles/quota; child-media access is deny by default |

<a id="srs-3"></a>
## 3. Actors and authorization

| Actor | Permissions | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| Platform Admin | Manage platform-wide roles/permissions; author public lessons; publish only after independent review by another Platform Admin with Content Admin permission; view/reply to school-scoped feedback; hide or revoke content when needed | The Platform Admin role by itself does not grant permission to read child media. Media access is denied by default; no permission is opened until the owner approves a separate process. Every future process must satisfy permission, reason code, privacy/lifecycle and audit. |
| School Manager | Manage one organization (one school), Teacher/Parent groups, school quota, view and approve Teacher lessons before public release; view and respond to feedback belonging to the school | Must not see data/media of another organization; a role that manages the roster does not implicitly grant access to child media. |
| Parent | Owns the Parent account and profile; creates runs; uses public/shared lessons; views lessons already taught for linked child profiles; submits feedback/replies in the feedback thread; manages and revokes grants; views videos | Must not access the organization/roster; must not view the Teacher profile beyond the shared fields; has no chat, completion toggle, drawing upload for feedback, or direct editing of AI scenes. |
| Teacher in charge (Teacher) | Operates the assigned class; authors/edits lessons and uses optional presets; manages assigned profiles; uploads/reviews filtered lesson plans; prepares occurrences; turns line-sketch on/off; views and confirms the representative child randomly selected by the system under the approved policy, then creates a new sample video; handles class feedback | Run/profile permissions follow the assignment and the currently effective grant; upon revoke/reassignment, permissions are revoked immediately. Must not process multiple children in one run. |
| Platform Admin (Content Admin permission) | Content review/publish/moderation is a permission capability within the Platform Admin policy, not an independent principal/role. Use the actor label Platform Admin (Content Admin permission) in UC/screen when an action requires this permission. | Affects only lessons/catalog according to the permission; does not grant child profile/media permissions. |
| System operations | Operates the local service, monitors health, restores backups, and configures the secret provider | Operational telemetry must not expose children's content. Access to production services requires a separately reviewed policy. |
| Adapter AI provider | Receives the permitted input of exactly one run and returns typed/versioned results | Runs only on the backend; does not receive the list of children, hidden profile fields, account credentials, or unrestricted storage access. |

Authentication uses Firebase Authentication only. The backend verifies the ID token and derives the actor identity; each resource read/write must further check role, permission, organization, class assignment, profile ownership, and the run's actor. Content review is a Platform Admin permission, not a separate login role. Firebase Storage, Firestore, or Realtime Database must not be used.

Parent and Teacher accounts must be separate, even when one adult holds both roles. A Parent may invite a responsible Teacher/school to share selected profile fields; the grant records the granting/receiving party, scope, purpose, status, duration, and revoke, and does not change the profile owner. Each school is a tenant; every lesson, feedback, and group member is checked by organization. Each Teacher profile has at most one active class enrollment; cross-role profile resolution occurs only through a versioned contract/grant.



**Target permission resolver contract (TO-BE / PROPOSED):** The API/application must authorize on the server side according to the authenticated principal, the effective role/permission assignment, organization/class scope, resource/action, and the currently effective grant revision. The resolver returns allow or deny together with an internal reason and the revision used for the decision; if an assignment, scope, or resource mapping is missing, or the version cannot be checked, it denies. Revoke invalidates cached decisions based on the old revision and blocks new reads/tokens as well as jobs that have not yet been accepted. This is a logical contract for design, not a table name, endpoint, or verified implementation. A Parent does not read the school roster; a School Manager has only the assigned tenant scope; a Platform Admin does not read child profile/media by virtue of the role or Content Admin permission. Force-close only preserves the rule in BR-41: the School Manager of the organization that owns the class/run may send an explicit emergency command, accompanied by a reason and audit metadata, without reading media and without deleting quota/sample claims. The assignment method, resolver, specific delegated persons, and audit fields/retention remain OPEN at Q-13 and §31.10(14),(17).

<a id="srs-4"></a>
## 4. Business Rules

BR-01 to BR-46 record the product/safety baseline; BR-47 to BR-60 add the platform v2.3 rules and the overrides requested by the owner. Conflicting historical rules are retained for traceability but yield to the newer content in §31.

| ID | Business Rule |
|---|---|
| BR-01 | A story run must be tied to exactly one child profile. The request and job contract store only a single scalar profile ref; multiple children, batches, and group contexts are rejected. |
| BR-02 | Each adult account has at most one unfinished story run. The lock is tied to the current owner (`active_owner_parent_account_id` or `active_owner_teacher_id`), separate from the immutable `created_by_actor_ref`. A Parent is locked by the Parent account; a Teacher is locked by the currently responsible teacher. |
| BR-03 | Parent and Teacher have separate account scope and profile history; a Parent may invite a Teacher/school to share a profile through a grant with purpose/scope/revoke. Principals and profiles are not merged automatically; role claims from the client do not grant permissions. |
| BR-04 | Classes and Teacher child profiles belong to an organization. Each organization has 10 class slots in the paid basic package. Additional capacity is purchased at a single configurable unit price per slot, monthly or yearly; there are no multiple tiers. The specific price belongs to commercial configuration. |
| BR-05 | The organization's School Manager assigns one responsible Teacher to each class. A Teacher may be responsible for multiple classes. Each child profile has at most one active class enrollment. |
| BR-06 | BE1 is the authoritative source of the current assignment. Reassignment uses a durable two-owner BE1/Story workflow: put the class into transfer-pending/read-only, fence old permissions and tokens immediately, transfer the active-run owner/lock through the Story port, and only then finalize the new Teacher; while pending, content is not opened to any Teacher. `created_by_actor_ref` is provenance only. If the new Teacher has an active run, Story rejects the lock transfer and the saga compensates BE1 back to the old assignment; if compensation is ambiguous, the transfer stays pending/read-only for reconciliation; a valid AI job is not canceled merely because the Teacher changed. Output is accepted only if the input/script/hash/occurrence and assignment version remain valid; only the current Teacher may view it. Admin does not view media by default. The responsible Teacher confirms with an audit before showing the sample to the class; viewers have no account, reusable playback capability, or export permission. |
| BR-07 | A Teacher creates/edits child profiles using a structured quick-entry form, and may re-enter answers from the parent survey form. V1 has no OCR or import of survey form files. |
| BR-08 | A lesson version may configure one or more outdoor activities with materials/method/notes. After the VLM and subject confirmation, the Parent chooses one main activity before the script is created. The backend builds a catalog-first candidate set from the PUBLIC/SHARED lesson versions the Parent currently has permission to view, filtered by the correct age, topic, the drawing's subject/features, and valid context; if the run pins a lesson, only the activities of that exact version/hash are used. Qwen3.5 receives the candidate set together with the profile summary/tags, topic, subject/features, and the narration/transcript confirmed by the adult (if any), then returns IDs/order/reasons; the backend hydrates the canonical content only from the source. No images, raw audio, or child identifiers are sent. If there is no suitable candidate, the AI may create exactly 3 draft activities through the quality gate; the adult chooses one activity. The adult may request another set of suggestions; activities already displayed/rejected must be excluded from the candidate output to avoid duplicates. A draft must not be written into the catalog or replace the lesson pin. The main activity is included in the script context and takes place after the video. After the video, a lesson pin displays all activities of the exact version/hash; if no lesson is pinned, the selected main activity is displayed. The Teacher occurrence keeps its own flow according to the approved plan. |
| BR-09 | A reusable lesson/version is independent of the occurrence. A Parent may skip the topic and lesson preset or choose a lesson currently permitted for viewing from the Gallery; choosing a lesson is optional and not a condition for taking a photo/creating a video. The run pins the exact lesson/version/hash validated by the backend. A Teacher occurrence pins an optional lesson/version; a Teacher occurrence that does not use a preset still requires an approved plan. The sample video uses exactly one child/drawing. |
| BR-10 | Each Teacher occurrence creates at most one playable sample video that has passed safety checks, for exactly one child. The slot is claimed atomically at the first playable video. A run with a technical failure or safety block before the first claim does not occupy the slot. After the claim, the slot is not reopened when the video is marked inappropriate, hidden, regeneration fails/runs out of attempts, the run is deleted, canceled, or the teacher changes; there is no second recovery run for that occurrence. |
| BR-11 | A run has one initial full video-generation request and at most two quality regeneration requests. Each accepted full video request consumes one limit slot of the run and one quota unit, even if it later fails technically or is safety-blocked. Automatic scene retries do not consume additional requests/units. Each regeneration remains in the same run and must return to confirming the script revision. |
| BR-12 | A completed Teacher video, including quality regenerations, remains the sole sample video of the occurrence; regeneration does not grant the right to choose another child or open a second independent sample run. |
| BR-13 | Each story has an initial version and at most three AI rewrites (four story revisions per run in total). A Parent submits suggestions; a Teacher may add narrative direction from the lesson plan/class plan. Adults do not directly edit AI-generated scenes. If the first story revision fails with the determined quality error `STORY_OUTPUT_INVALID`, the Parent may submit one new rewrite command on the same run, once only; the failed revision counts toward the four revisions and Teacher runs have no such recovery; the failed revision is kept immutable and the command must use a new idempotency key. State-transition conditions and the owner lock are specified in §6.1.1; this does not apply to unknown outcomes, safety/consent failures, or other errors. |
| BR-14 | Each new story revision must be viewed and confirmed again before creating the video. The UI displays the current revision; old revisions are kept internally until the run is deleted. |
| BR-15 | The transcript, subject, lesson/plan, and profile context snapshot must be confirmed at the corresponding precondition step. A Parent must choose one main activity before the script is created; the activity suggestion may be changed with a new request that excludes choices already seen/rejected. A Teacher sample still pins one approved activity. The final gate before video is script/storyboard confirmation; there is no separate final video approval. |
| BR-16 | The video is displayed immediately when it is created and has passed the technical check and the automatic safety gate. If an adult marks the video as inappropriate and then requests regeneration, the old video is hidden from playback immediately; the media is kept internally until the run is deleted. If the replacement generation fails, the old video still must not be played. The run returns to the script-viewing step and the script must be confirmed before the next generation. |
| BR-17 | Each scene has one initial attempt and at most two automatic technical retries. When retries are exhausted, the run stops and returns a safe error code. A new Teacher run may be opened for the occurrence only if no sample video has passed the safety gate and become playable. If the first video has claimed the slot, is later hidden, and both quality regenerations fail/run out of attempts, the run ends in error; the slot remains used and there is no new recovery run within the occurrence (Q127). |
| BR-18 | Parent and Teacher profiles are for children aged 36–120 months (3–10 years), divided into versioned age bands of 36–71, 72–107, and 108–120 months; age is stored in months and the band version used is pinned; the exact date of birth is not stored. Names/nicknames are not sent to the model. This replaces the historical limit of 36–155 months. |
| BR-19 | Personalization information is entered by adults and is revisioned. It may include interests, learning observations, in-app run history, off-app experiences/events entered by adults, achievements, sources, and an optional approximate date. In-app history and off-app experiences are two separate sources. |
| BR-20 | Health/psychological information represents only support needs entered by adults, not a diagnosis. This field has restricted access, is excluded from the AI context by default, and the system does not infer it automatically. |
| BR-21 | The adult reviews a minimal context snapshot, tied to a specific version for each run. Only explicitly permitted fields may be sent into the AI process. |
| BR-22 | Teacher lesson plans accept PDF/PPTX/DOCX with text, up to 25 MB and 100 pages/slides. Encrypted/password-protected files are rejected. OCR of scanned pages is not supported. |
| BR-23 | Audio ideas are up to 2 minutes and 20 MB. Whisper prioritizes Vietnamese. The original audio, raw transcript, and corrected transcript are kept until the run is deleted. |
| BR-24 | The final video is MP4, 720p, 16:9 aspect ratio, 30–90 seconds long as decided by the system, with a single neutral Vietnamese adult voice, Vietnamese subtitles, and background music always off. The recognizable subject, main shape, colors, and style of the drawing are the reference anchors for the scene. |
| BR-25 | The product has no download/export function or public video URL. Playback requires a short-lived token authorized by the server. |
| BR-26 | When a user deletes a run in COMPLETED, FAILED, or ABANDONED, or cancels a run that is not yet complete, the lifecycle deletes the drawing, audio, transcript, the run's profile snapshot, story revisions, scene/video media, and linked content. A cancel command releases the active lock immediately; delete/cancel commands retain only minimal audit metadata. A FAILED or ABANDONED run is not purged automatically; its content is retained until an authorized adult requests deletion. For a Teacher occurrence, the used sample slot state and the claim time are retained; the pointer to the run/media is deleted but the slot is not reopened. There is no separate video delete operation. Consumed quota units are not refunded (Q119, Q123). |
| BR-27 | All demo flows use only synthetic profiles, drawings, and audio. Real child data is outside the demo and requires a separate policy on purpose, rights, authorization, data transfer to providers, and lifecycle. |
| BR-28 | Class subscription capacity is calculated monthly/yearly and in VND. MoMo and VNPAY are the V1 payment adapters. V1 does not use VietQR. |
| BR-29 | Additional purchased class slots are activated after payment verification and are prorated for the remaining time of the current period; the next renewal date does not change. |
| BR-30 | Auto-renew may be turned off. Paid capacity remains in effect until the end of the period. V1 does not automatically calculate mid-period prorated refunds; exceptions are handled manually outside the system. |
| BR-31 | A failed renewal starts a 7-day grace period. When grace ends, creation of new classes and new story runs is restricted; access to existing classes, rosters, history, and payment management is still allowed. |
| BR-32 | Reducing capacity requires the School Manager to choose the classes to be suspended before confirming. One append-only event is recorded for each selected class, with a shared change-group ID and effective time. Suspended classes/rosters/history are retained in read-only mode; no data is deleted. |
| BR-33 | Billing notifications are sent to the payment admin in-app and by email: reminders 7 days and 1 day before the due date, when a payment succeeds/fails, and when grace expires. |
| BR-34 | System audit records only metadata of actor, action, resource, and result. Media, transcripts, prompts, tokens, signed URLs, or children's names must not be recorded in audit/log/metric labels. |
| BR-35 | Deleting a Parent or Teacher child profile starts a lifecycle by owner scope: revoke access, block new runs, delete the profile context and related run content; retain only necessary audit/enrollment metadata. Profiles belonging to other scopes are not affected. |
| BR-36 | Uploaded lesson plans, adult suggestions, and model output are all untrusted content. A versioned child-safety policy is applied before displaying the script or generating the video. Before playback, an automated gate checks sampled frames, the entire narration transcript, and all subtitles of the composed video. Blocked output cannot be played and returns to the script review step; this gate does not add a human final-video approval step. The content must not diagnose children, give medical/psychological advice, disclose profile data, or steer children toward dangerous activities. |
| BR-37 | A Teacher may show a sample video to the class only after the responsible Teacher records a separate confirmation with audit for the occurrence. This is a confirmation of the audience/purpose of presentation, not a final video approval. Processing real child data still requires a privacy policy that is reviewed separately. |
| BR-38 | Video quota is by calendar month in `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`, kept separate: Parent usage is counted against the Parent account; Teacher usage is counted against the organization that owns the occurrence. A Parent may purchase quota for their own account; an assigned School Manager may purchase quota for the organization and grant internal limits to Teachers belonging to the organization. An allocation does not create additional quota and must not exceed the school's remaining available balance; the School may reclaim the unused portion to reallocate; when a Teacher leaves the assignment with the organization, the unused allocation automatically returns to the School balance, while consumed usage remains attributed to the School. A Teacher has no independent purchase/owner pool and no cross-scope fallback. The Platform Admin manages offer/price versions. A monthly plan may be paid manually or auto-renewed after the customer gives explicit authorization; cancelling renewal must be allowed. A top-up is a one-time payment, does not auto-renew, and does not expire until used up. MoMo/VNPAY are payment methods; a grant is issued only after a verified settlement event processed idempotently. There is no refund for quota purchases after successful settlement. A full-video request is considered accepted only after AI Policy confirms the compute reservation and Billing confirms quota admission with the same idempotency key; Billing records exactly one usage unit in its own transaction before dispatch. This is a multi-owner saga, not a SQL transaction spanning modules; an ambiguous receipt is reconciled before dispatch. Each accepted request still consumes one unit even if a provider/technical error or safety block occurs afterward; usage quota is not refunded. A scene retry is not a new request and does not consume an additional unit. A rejected budget/quota admission creates no accepted generation/job/usage, does not occupy the run request cap, and does not dispatch. When quota is exhausted, new video requests are rejected with VIDEO_QUOTA_EXCEEDED; viewing old videos/data is still allowed. A new request may be resubmitted only when the scope has a valid grant. Billing owns the grant and the immutable usage ledger; AI Policy only accounts for/reserves platform resources (GPU/compute) and does not own product quota. No mutable remaining balance is stored. The included amount is configured separately for school and Parent, with the figures finalized after a synthetic benchmark (Q115). The current included grant is used first, then top-ups FIFO; included expires at the end of the month, top-ups do not expire until used up (Q117–Q118, Q124–Q126, Rev71). Starting mid-month receives the full included amount (Q121); deleting a run does not refund a unit (Q119); top-up is one-time (Q120, Q128, Q132). A Teacher allocation is a hard cap (owner 2026-11-26): a Teacher with no allocation (`QUOTA_ALLOCATION_NOT_FOUND`) or an exhausted one (`TEACHER_QUOTA_INSUFFICIENT`) cannot create a video even when the school still has balance; an empty school balance returns `SCHOOL_QUOTA_INSUFFICIENT`. |
| BR-39 | All states awaiting adult action, including `DRAFT`, begin adult-wait when no AI job remains and an adult command is needed. At +144 hours, exactly one inbox reminder is created per dedupe key; a best-effort push and a best-effort email are attempted only to the verified address of the current owner. The inbox is the source of truth; an email/push delivery failure does not change the run state. At +168 hours, `VIDEO_READY` becomes `COMPLETED`, and any other adult-wait state becomes `ABANDONED`, releasing the owner lock but retaining content until the user deletes it. `VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING` is system work with no idle timer; if admission is rejected, the StoryRun returns to `SCRIPT_REVIEW` and a new adult-wait begins (Q133, Q136). |
| BR-40 | An adult may cancel a run that is not yet complete to release the lock immediately and delete content per the lifecycle; deleting a run in COMPLETED, FAILED, or ABANDONED deletes linked media/content. An accepted video request is not refunded quota; a successful Teacher sample claim is still retained. (Q123/Q128/Q133/Q134) |
| BR-41 | The School Manager of the organization that owns the class/run may force-close a conflicting run only in an emergency, via an explicit command with a reason and audit metadata. The command does not allow viewing media; the quota claim and sample claim are retained unchanged. How permission is assigned/stored and the actor resolver remain open at §31.10. (Q134) |
| BR-42 | Q137/Q141/Q146/Q167/Rev66: The Parent line guide and Teacher drawing guide are optional; a lesson presentation may provide knowledge/guide before drawing. A Parent does not preselect the subject of the drawing; the subject is reviewed after capture. The Teacher guide/deck follows the approved lesson/plan, progressive adaptive 4–8 steps; the Teacher presentation supports text/image/video/SVG per slide. |
| BR-43 | The Teacher guide covers the approved lesson/plan and guides drawing progressively from basic strokes to a sufficiently recognizable shape, with adaptive 4–8 steps; the presentation supports content/image/video/SVG by arranged slide position. The Teacher controls the presentation; when the plan or source content changes, dependent assets/guides must be marked stale by revision. (Q138–Q140/Q142/Q167; Rev66) |
| BR-44 | The SVG catalog, SVG engine, and AI guide/visual do not consume video quota. They use a separate request limit and compute cap; specific values must be benchmarked/configured. The AI guide/visual does not receive profile, photo/drawing, voice, or child identity. (Q143/Q146; Rev66) |
| BR-45 | The video follows the pipeline VLM image understanding → structured LLM story → storyboard of 5–15 scenes → adult script confirmation → scene reference/Character Bible → LTX-2.5 image-to-video on Modal L40S → consistency/technical validation → FFmpeg composition → Vietnamese TTS/subtitles, no music → automated safety gate → private playback. Each run has one child; there is no collaboration, public gallery/download, or final human video approval. An unsuitable video returns to script review, with at most two full regenerations after the initial request; each scene has at most two technical retries after the first attempt. (Q144 and the finalized pipeline decisions) |
| BR-46 | Q146/Rev66 (supersedes text-only Q145): A Parent may request or skip a progressive 4–8 step SVG guide for a general topic; the approved catalog takes priority, with an AI draft when missing and the capability enabled. No child data is sent; output is private with provenance/cleanup and has a separate cap; no error blocks the mandatory capture and the post-capture subject review. |

<a id="srs-5"></a>
## 5. User flows

### 5.1 Parents

A Parent may open the public library, lessons shared by name/group, or lessons that have been taught to a child profile that is currently shared. A lesson is used as a preset; the personalized version does not modify the source version. Lesson history has a 30-day time limit counted from the first delivery/open; revoke/unpublish retains neutral metadata but does not reopen the lesson content.

1. Log in with a separate Parent account, choose a child profile belonging to that account, and confirm that the data-processing permission is still valid.
2. A self-entered/selected topic and a lesson from the Gallery are both optional. If the Parent opens the Gallery, they can only read public/shared lessons they have permission for; a lesson pin must validate current access and the exact version/hash.
3. If a lesson is chosen, the Parent may view the lesson's knowledge presentation before drawing. The lesson's guide or a topic-based guide is optional: the approved SVG guide from the catalog is taken first; if missing, the adult may request an AI draft if the capability is enabled, preview it, then accept/discard. The guide has 4–8 adaptive steps and receives no child data; a missing guide/error/disabled/timeout/rejection does not block capture.
4. The child draws on paper. The adult photographs the drawing of exactly one child (camera is the primary action; photo library is an alternative). The photo is mandatory; without a photo, no StoryRun is created and no VLM is called.
5. The VLM analyzes the photo and, if a topic/lesson is chosen, checks suitability. If there is a topic mismatch, the system notifies the adult to change the topic or retake the photo at most once. If retaken, the subject recognized by AI from the new photo is used; the chosen lesson remains the source of knowledge/presentation.
6. If there is a recording, run Whisper; display the transcript and require the adult to edit/confirm. Display the subject/features/uncertainty of the photo; the adult confirms or edits the subject by revision.
7. The adult reviews the minimal context snapshot (profile revision, age band, interests allowlist, topic/idea, consent version, and lesson pin if any). The backend validates profile/consent/lesson access and binds the run to the exact lesson version/hash.
8. After the subject is confirmed, the backend builds a catalog candidate set from PUBLIC/SHARED lesson versions the Parent is permitted to view, filtered by age/topic/subject/features/context; a lesson pin restricts it to the exact version/hash. Qwen3.5 selects IDs and explains based on the profile summary/tags, topic, subject/features, and confirmed narration; if no suitable activity exists, it may create exactly 3 drafts via the quality gate. No photo/raw audio/identifier is sent.
9. The Parent selects one main activity before creating the script. The adult may request a replacement suggestion set; the system excludes options that have already been displayed/rejected to avoid repetition. The selected activity is included in the story context and will be done after the video. An error/timeout/disabled/rejection state must have an understandable status; the Parent may change the selection or retry according to the recovery policy; no script is created until the main activity is selected (BR-15), with no automatic AI retry.
10. The AI generates a personalized story/storyboard from the drawing, the approved context, the subject and the main activity. Lesson script direction is applied only if the version allows `script_following_allowed`. The Parent may suggest up to three rewrites but cannot edit scenes directly.
11. The adult reviews and confirms the final script/storyboard revision before the video.
12. The backend generates a new video for the correct child from that drawing image according to the pipeline in §6.2.2; it must not reuse another child's video. The video may differ by artwork/profile but follows the script direction when permitted/enabled.
13. Playback is opened only after technical and safety checks. The Parent may finalize or request regeneration up to two times; the old video is hidden immediately once the replacement request is received. An accepted video request consumes quota under the Billing rules, including technical/provider/safety failures.
14. After the video, if a lesson pin exists, all outdoor activities from the exact pinned version/hash are displayed in full; if there is no pin, the main activity selected before the script is displayed. Keep the 144h reminder, the 168h idle close, the cancel/delete lifecycle, private playback and feedback according to the shared profile; there is no chat, no completion toggle and no sending of drawings via feedback.

The mandatory sequence is profile/consent → (optional) topic or lesson presentation → (optional) SVG guide → mandatory photo capture → VLM and topic/lesson match check → adult confirms subject/context → select one main activity → generate and approve story → generate a new video from the drawing → after the video, display the selected activity and all activities of the exact lesson pin, if any. Topic, lesson and guide are optional; the photo and the confirmation of the main activity before the script are mandatory.

### 5.2 Teacher

A Teacher may create a reusable school lesson or use a public/shared version as a preset; using a preset does not modify the source version. An occurrence may use a preset or a self-created approved plan; if a preset is used, the occurrence pins the exact selected lesson/version/plan.

1. Log in with a separate Teacher web account and open the organization's assigned class.
2. Create or select a managed child profile. Each profile belongs to only one active class at a time. Answers from the parent questionnaire may be entered quickly.
3. Create an occurrence and choose whether or not to use a lesson preset; documents may be uploaded using a standard template/form.
4. The parser extracts text within the current limits, with no OCR of scans. The system filters/maps allowlisted fields into a structured draft; the original file and the raw plan are not sent to the model. The Teacher previews/corrects the extracted draft before AI assists in creating the lesson/plan.
5. An AI-assisted lesson draft is always in draft status. The Teacher reviews/edits, saves a version and submits it to the School Manager for review if they want it public; the Admin approves if they want it public platform-wide. A lesson has an activity list and one editable presentation: the Teacher edits slide content/order, uploads images, adds YouTube/Vimeo video links, selects from the SVG catalog or requests an AI SVG draft if the capability is enabled, then places media on the appropriate slide. Images/videos/AI drafts must pass checks and Teacher review before publication.
6. The drawing guide is optional. The Teacher may take a guide from the SVG catalog or a guide attached to the lesson; when none exists, the Teacher may request a draft for the exact subject/knowledge of the lesson. The guide uses 4–8 adaptive steps and progresses from basic strokes to a sufficiently recognizable shape.
7. During the session, the Teacher opens the presentation/guide and controls Back/Next/skip/regenerate according to permissions. Children draw with paper and pen; there is no shared canvas or multiple children in one run.
8. The sample video remains a separate flow. The system selects one child with equal probability from the eligible set; the Teacher confirms the child/context. A stale candidate may be reselected at most once; if there is no candidate, it stops. Before confirmation, no StoryRun is created/accepted, and no AI job, quota usage or sample claim occurs. One occurrence has at most one successful sample video.
9. Pin the approved plan to the occurrence; if a preset is used, pin the lesson/version and the Teacher selects exactly one approved activity for the sample run. A plan-only occurrence does not require a lesson preset.
10. The Teacher photographs that child's paper drawing with the browser camera or uploads a photo taken with a phone. Enter the idea as text or a voice recording; Whisper prioritizes Vietnamese, and the Teacher reviews/edits/confirms the transcript and the recognized subject. Editing the subject creates a new VLM revision. Do not use another child's image in the same run.
11. The VLM analyzes the drawing and creates the recognition anchor; the LLM generates a story/storyboard of 5–15 scenes personalized to the profile. The Teacher may set a lesson-based narrative direction and suggest up to three rewrites.
12. The Teacher reviews and confirms the final revision of the script/storyboard. A lesson may enable/disable script-following; if enabled, the video follows the script direction, but each video is still new and may differ by drawing/profile. The pipeline in §6.2.2 runs only after confirmation.
13. The backend prepares reference images/the Character Bible solely from the selected drawing, generates scene clips with LTX-2.5 on Modal L40S, checks consistency/technical quality, assembles them with FFmpeg, generates Vietnamese narration and subtitles, turns off background music, then runs automatic safety validation on the assembled output. Only a video that passes the gate may be played; a ready video is shown within the run/occurrence, and there is no final video approval step.
14. If the video is unsuitable, the Teacher requests regeneration; the old output is hidden from playback immediately once the replacement request is received and the run returns to script review. There are at most two whole-video regeneration requests after the first request; each scene has at most two technical retries after the first attempt. Each accepted video request counts toward quota even with technical/safety failure; a failure before the first playable sample video does not claim the occurrence slot. After the first claim, the slot is not reopened, even if regenerations fail (Q127/Q128).
15. The Teacher is responsible for separately confirming the showing of the video to the whole class at that occurrence. This is an audience/use confirmation, not a video approval. When no AI job remains and the video is ready, the Teacher may finalize or regenerate; adult-wait/144h reminder/168h close, run cancellation, media retention until deletion, and non-refundable quota follow the general rules.

After the session, the linked Parent may view the lesson taught for 30 days under §31.6, download content they still have access to, and send separate feedback within that tenant.


#### Decision table UC-05 — sample child selection (TO-BE)

The system computes the candidate set according to the policy version, consent, enrollment and assignment that are still in effect, then selects exactly one child with equal probability; the Teacher reviews and confirms the child/context. A stale candidate may be reselected at most once; an empty set stops. Before confirmation, no StoryRun is created/accepted, and no AI job, quota usage or sample claim occurs. The table below keeps technical guards/responses that have no contract yet in the open state. "State unchanged" means no new occurrence/run state is invented; specific transitions must use the states that already exist in §§6/22–23.

| Case | Guard/input | Action | Response and target state | Evidence / decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Eligible set has at least 1 child | The candidate must belong to the current roster/enrollment and Teacher assignment, and satisfy the consent/exclusion policy currently in effect. | The server selects one typed child/recipient reference with equal probability; returns exactly one choice to the assigned Teacher. | Displays the filtered child/context for the Teacher to review; only after confirmation is a StoryRun created/accepted and admission proceeds. The occurrence keeps its current state before confirmation. | The owner has chosen equal-probability selection and confirmation by the Teacher. The selection evidence/snapshot schema remains OPEN; a separate run-input context snapshot after confirmation is still required. |
| Candidate stale before confirmation | The candidate or assignment/grant/consent revision is no longer current. | Revalidate; allow reselection at most once. | If a new candidate is still valid, return one new choice; otherwise stop safely. No run/job/quota/claim is created before confirmation. | The owner has chosen the one-time reselect limit for a stale candidate; the error/expiry/idempotency contract remains OPEN. |
| Empty set | No candidate satisfies the approved policy. | No fallback to another profile; the Teacher may not pick outside the set. | Stop selection; no sample run, AI job, quota usage or claim is created; keep the occurrence in its current state and display a message that there is no valid choice, without revealing the reason for exclusion per child. | The owner has chosen stop/no-fallback. The error code, retry control, audit event and selection-attempt evidence remain OPEN. |
| Grant/consent no longer valid before returning a choice or confirm | The server rechecks the version/validity before each read/command; revoke must block reads/new tokens and jobs not yet accepted. | Deny the command and do not include child media/context in the response; do not accept the job. | A denied/refresh screen per the error handling in §21.6; the outcome for a pending selection (clear, retain hidden or require a new selection) is OPEN. | Record allow/deny metadata per the current policy; retention/field schema OPEN §31.10(17). |
| Teacher does not confirm or closes the screen | There is no confirm command tied to the correct occurrence/selection/input revision. | Do not create or accept a StoryRun. | Keep the current state; timeout, selection expiry and resume behavior are OPEN. | Events/audit for confirm/decline/timeout and the selection retention time are OPEN. |
| Teacher requests reselection | The previous candidate became stale before confirmation; the one-time limit is unused. | Re-run selection once per the current policy; arbitrary reselection is not allowed. | Return a new choice or stop if there is no candidate; no run/job/quota/claim is accepted before confirmation. | The owner has chosen at most one reselect only for a stale candidate; attempt correlation/evidence OPEN. |
| Two concurrent requests claim the same occurrence | A compare-and-set/serialized command must check the occurrence revision and the one-successful-sample invariant. The physical mechanism is not approved. | At most one command may record a successful sample for the occurrence; a duplicate request is idempotent or a conflict per the approved contract. | The winner continues the flow; the loser cannot create a second successful sample. The run/quota of an already accepted request is handled per the current receipt/quota rules; the reconciliation contract still needs review. | Q-01 + D-06; exact claim timing, key/index and audit evidence OPEN; no cross-owner transaction is created. |
| Revoke occurs while the Teacher is waiting to confirm | The current grant revision differs from the revision used to create the view, or consent has expired under policy. | Re-authorize on confirm; deny if the permission no longer exists. | Do not accept a job that is not yet accepted. For an already accepted job, do not retarget or auto-cancel; apply the approved grant/job policy, which is still OPEN for this case. | Q-04 / D-09; the permission revision and correlation must be traced; retention OPEN. |

The mandatory run-input context snapshot may be created only from the approved minimum data after confirmation. Metadata-only selection evidence may be used for reconciliation, but the field list, retention and handling of concurrent confirmation have not been finalized. Do not store re-identifiable seeds or child media in audit by inference.

### 5.3 Organization and payment

1. The School Manager of the organization creates a class while capacity remains (10 class slots included in the basic plan plus purchased additional slots).
2. The School Manager assigns one Teacher in charge of each class and manages the roster/class placement of profiles.
3. The School Manager purchases additional class slots via the MoMo/VNPAY adapter monthly/yearly. Only the provider reference is stored; payment credentials are not stored.
4. When payment verification succeeds, update the capacity period; slots purchased mid-period are prorated.
5. Send in-app/email reminders 7 days and 1 day before the due date; send notifications of payment success/failure and when the grace period expires.
6. A failed renewal has a 7-day grace period. When the grace period ends, creation of new classes and story runs is restricted, while the right to view existing classes/rosters/history and to manage payment is retained.
7. When capacity is reduced, the School Manager designates the classes to be suspended. Data of a suspended class remains and is read-only.

<a id="srs-6"></a>
## 6. Story and video lifecycle

### 6.1 Logical stages

| Stage | Required outcome | User action or system rule |
|---|---|---|
| Draft | One child and the source drawing are attached to the run | The backend must enforce only one profile ref |
| View input | The adult's idea exists as text or a transcript for review | Whisper converts speech to text, and the adult confirms |
| View subject | The VLM returns a structure of subject/entity/uncertainty level | The adult confirms or edits; an edit re-runs the VLM |
| View context | Minimal profile snapshot and lesson/script direction, if the adult chose them, have been approved | The Parent must select one primary activity before the story. After the video, no-pin returns the activity saved in the run; lesson pin returns all activities of the exact version and marks the primary activity. A Teacher run confirms the approved activity in the plan before generation. |
| Story draft | Creates a versioned script with 5–15 scenes | The LLM output must match the schema and safety policy |
| Rewrite | Creates a new immutable story revision | At most three rewrite requests after the first version; each revision is reviewed |
| Confirm script | A hash of the confirmed script/storyboard revision exists | Mandatory before each video generation request |
| Generate video | Per-scene clips, narration, subtitles and the merged MP4 exist | LTX-2.5 via Modal L40S; each scene has at most two automatic technical retries |
| Ready to play | An artifact that passes technical and safety checks exists | Displayed immediately; the run remains active until the adult presses Complete or Regenerate; there is no video approval |
| Quality regeneration | The run returns to the script review step | The adult marks the video as inappropriate; the old video's playback is hidden from the moment the regeneration request is accepted; the script must be confirmed; at most two regenerations |
| Complete, fail, abandon or cancel | The run leaves the active set | Completion is by the adult or idle closeout from VIDEO_READY; other waiting runs become ABANDONED after 168 adult-wait hours. FAILED/ABANDONED retain content until deletion; cancel releases the lock and then deletes according to the lifecycle. A run that fails before the first playable claim does not occupy a Teacher sample slot |
| Deleted | The lifecycle has deleted all linked content | Only minimal audit metadata is kept; consumed quota and successful sample claims are still retained |

Asynchronous AI results must match the expected run revision, input hash, profile context snapshot hash, lesson version if pinned, selected primary activity, Teacher activity/plan ref if applicable, and contract version. The Parent's primary activity is the story context; additional activities from the lesson are only for performing after the video and do not change the approved script. Old results are recorded as stale and must not overwrite newer data.

### 6.1.1 Proposed State Model

The state names below are target values in the domain. UI labels must be localized into Vietnamese, and internal/provider names must not be displayed to users.

| Aggregate | Proposed states | Summary of permitted state transitions |
|---|---|---|
| StoryRun | DRAFT, INPUT_PROCESSING, INPUT_REVIEW, SUBJECT_REVIEW, PREPARATION_REVIEW, STORY_GENERATING, SCRIPT_REVIEW, VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING, VIDEO_QUEUED, VIDEO_GENERATING, VIDEO_SAFETY_VALIDATING, VIDEO_READY, SAFETY_BLOCKED, COMPLETED, FAILED, ABANDONED, DELETE_PENDING, DELETED | `run_state` describes the visible business step; `AiJob.job_state` + `job_stage` describe detailed ASR/VLM/story/video processing. DRAFT is adult-wait when the adult needs to enter/confirm data; DRAFT → INPUT_PROCESSING when the input has been received and the ASR/VLM job is enqueued; input job success or a recoverable error → INPUT_REVIEW; the adult confirms the transcript → SUBJECT_REVIEW; editing the subject creates a new VLM job and returns INPUT_PROCESSING → SUBJECT_REVIEW. AI processing in progress is not adult-wait even if the screen displays the stage. SUBJECT_REVIEW/PREPARATION_REVIEW → STORY_GENERATING only when the subject, context and one primary activity have been selected/confirmed; Teacher uses the approved activity, Parent must select one activity before the script. STORY_GENERATING → SCRIPT_REVIEW; SCRIPT_REVIEW → STORY_GENERATING on a valid rewrite or VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING when the script revision has been confirmed; only an accepted admission moves StoryRun/VideoGeneration to VIDEO_QUEUED. Queue → generation → safety validation → VIDEO_READY when passed. Only states with no job being processed and requiring an adult command are adult-wait: DRAFT, INPUT_REVIEW, SUBJECT_REVIEW, PREPARATION_REVIEW, SCRIPT_REVIEW, SAFETY_BLOCKED (when an adult is needed) and VIDEO_READY. VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING is system work and does not arm the idle timer; a budget/quota rejection returns the run to SCRIPT_REVIEW and starts a new adult-wait; an ambiguous receipt keeps it pending until reconcile. `last_meaningful_activity_at` is written by the server in UTC when an adult submits an effective command/review/confirmation; polling/push receipt/app heartbeat do not reset idle. `adult_wait_started_at` begins when the AI job ends/fails and the run actually needs adult action. After 144 hours, an inbox reminder is created, plus best-effort push and email if the current owner's email is verified; after 168 hours VIDEO_READY → COMPLETED, and any other adult-wait state, including DRAFT → ABANDONED; both release the lock and keep content until the user deletes it. An AI job has an independent 6-hour deadline and is not subject to idle-closeout while it is QUEUED/RUNNING/RETRY_WAIT/AWAITING_RECONCILIATION. Cancelling an unfinished run → DELETE_PENDING, releases the lock immediately and starts the purge. COMPLETED, FAILED, ABANDONED, DELETE_PENDING, DELETED do not occupy the lock. Reassignment does not change state: permission moves to the currently assigned Teacher, the old permission/token is revoked, and audit metadata is recorded. SCRIPT_GENERATING (the script is being generated) and SCRIPT_GENERATION_UNKNOWN (the outcome is unknown) are official StoryRun states (owner 2026-11-26): they are stored, returned in responses and may be cancelled under UC-11. |
| VideoGeneration | ADMISSION_PENDING, ADMISSION_REJECTED, ACCEPTED, QUEUED, GENERATING, TECHNICAL_VALIDATING, SAFETY_VALIDATING, READY, SAFETY_BLOCKED, FAILED, REJECTED_BY_ADULT, SUPERSEDED | A durable generation row is first an intent with an idempotency key. It moves from ADMISSION_PENDING to ACCEPTED only after the AI Policy budget reservation and the Billing quota receipt both confirm; only ACCEPTED receives generation_no, occupies one of the three requests/run, creates the AI job/outbox and consumes quota. A budget/quota failure moves the intent to ADMISSION_REJECTED and does not count toward request/video quota. An ambiguous result keeps the intent pending so the reconciler looks up the receipt by the same key before dispatch. When a StoryRun in SCRIPT_REVIEW receives a video generation request, it moves to VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING. This is system work, not adult-wait; `ADMISSION_REJECTED` returns the StoryRun to SCRIPT_REVIEW and sets a new timer, without consuming quota/cap. `ACCEPTED` sets `deadline_at = accepted_at + 6h`, covering the queue, retry wait and every stage. READY requires passing technical and safety checks. When the adult rejects the video and requests regeneration, the old video becomes REJECTED_BY_ADULT and is hidden from playback immediately; the content is kept internally until the run is deleted. If the replacement video fails, the old video still does not play again. The new script must be confirmed before the next request. |
| LessonOccurrence | DRAFT, DOCUMENT_REVIEW, PLAN_REVIEW, APPROVED, SAMPLE_AVAILABLE, SAMPLE_USED, ARCHIVED | Import/review the document before approving the plan; an approved occurrence allows creating a sample video; the single output that passes safety and is playable atomically claims the slot. A run that fails before playback keeps the slot unused. Deleting a run deletes the run pointer but keeps the claim state/time. The occurrence also records the confirmation of the responsible Teacher when the video is shown to the class. |
| SketchDeckRevision | GENERATING, READY, FAILED, STALE, SUPERSEDED | Created from one approved plan revision/hash; READY only when it covers every activity, each guide has 4–8 ordered progressive drawing steps under the adaptive policy, and content/layout/media validation passes. Every output must have an asset/hash/lineage. A plan/source change makes dependent guides STALE; regenerate creates a new revision. There is no profile/child subject and it does not consume video quota. |
| AiJob | QUEUED, RUNNING, RETRY_WAIT, SUCCEEDED, FAILED, CANCEL_REQUESTED, CANCELLED, STALE, AWAITING_RECONCILIATION | Queue/worker state is stored durably; bounded technical retry uses RETRY_WAIT; a valid result means success; a hash/revision mismatch means stale; exhausted retries mean failure; an unclear provider outcome stops for reconciliation before calling the costly GPU again. |
| OrganizationSubscription | PAYMENT_PENDING, ACTIVE, GRACE, RESTRICTED, CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END, CANCELED | Only verified provider events change payment state; a failed renewal enters a 7-day grace and then is restricted; cancelling the subscription stops the next period but paid entitlements remain until the end of the period; capacity is tied to the effective period. |
| PaymentAttempt | CREATED, PENDING, SUCCEEDED, FAILED, EXPIRED | A browser redirect is informational only; only a callback/provider event with a verified signature, or one that has been reconciled, records payment as made. A duplicate event does not apply capacity a second time. |

Transition rules:

- Bounded story recovery exception: when `STORY_GENERATING` ends with the determined quality error `STORY_OUTPUT_INVALID`, the run is in `FAILED` and the last revision is in `FAILED`; only a new rewrite command initiated by the Parent, and only when the failed revision is the first one, is allowed to go `FAILED → STORY_GENERATING`. The backend must verify the same owner, the latest revision failed, `job_state=FAILED`, no unknown outcome, a non-empty instruction, the revision count still within the limit, and the context/subject/understanding/drawing/activity selection and hashes unchanged. The command uses a new idempotency key; the failed revision and old receipt are not modified or replayed. In the same transaction owned by Story, lock the run, check that the Parent has no other active run, create the revision and a new command receipt, then reset `active_owner_parent_account_id`; the partial unique index is the final gate for concurrent contention, and a conflict returns 409 before dispatch. When the rewrite succeeds the run returns to `SCRIPT_REVIEW`; if it fails again, the new revision stays `FAILED`, the run goes to `FAILED` and releases the active-owner lock. The limit remains one first version and at most three rewrites; the UI only opens the rewrite action for exactly this error. Every other `FAILED` error, unknown outcome/reconciliation, safety/consent failure, deleted/abandoned run or stale data remains terminal. Deleting a run and a recovery command on the same `FAILED` run are serialized by the run lock: the command accepted first wins and the later one is refused. A `FAILED` run that is still eligible for recovery is not treated as a closed run under FR-C-11 (owner 2026-11-26).
- Each application command changes state and writes the event/outbox in a transaction owned by that aggregate's owner. The client does not set state itself; a business operation that passes through multiple owners uses a contract, an idempotency receipt and a saga, not a SQL transaction across modules.
- The story rewrite counter and the video request counter are separate. Each run has the first story and at most three rewrites; one initial video request and at most two quality regeneration requests. Admission uses the same business command/idempotency key but separate owning transactions: AI Policy creates the compute reservation; Billing checks the quota scope and writes the usage unit/receipt; only after both receipts are confirmed does Story record the accepted state, occupy the request slot and write the outbox in its own separate Story transaction. If Billing rejects, the reservation is released; an ambiguous receipt is looked up/reconciled with the same key before dispatch. Each accepted request still consumes one quota unit even if the request later fails technically/at the provider or is safety-blocked; quota is not refunded. Automatic scene retry has its own limit and does not create new product-quota usage.
- Quality video regeneration must return to reviewing/confirming the current script revision. The adult may keep the current story or ask the AI to rewrite it within the limit before confirming.
- A technical scene retry does not count as a story rewrite or a video regeneration request. A run that fails before there is a safe/playable output does not occupy the sample video slot of the Teacher occurrence.
- VIDEO_READY requires passing the technical checks and the versioned output-safety gate. If the gate does not check the media sufficiently, fails or returns an unknown result, playback is blocked and the run is put in a state waiting for the adult to fix/confirm the script. A video that passes may be played immediately; the adult may complete/regenerate. VIDEO_READY automatically becomes COMPLETED after 168 hours of adult-wait idle; other adult-wait states automatically become ABANDONED at the same deadline, after a reminder at hour 144. Both outcomes close the run, release the lock and keep the content. Quota for accepted requests is not refunded and a used Teacher sample claim is not reopened.

### 6.2 AI Process

1. **Image understanding:** The VLM identifies candidate subjects, characters, objects, setting, visual style anchors and uncertainty level from the original drawing.
2. **Idea understanding:** Whisper produces a transcript prioritizing Vietnamese. Content edited by the adult is stored as a separate revision.
3. **Context filtering:** the backend creates a bounded context snapshot, per schema version and approved by the adult. The child's name and supporting notes are removed.
4. **Story creation:** The LLM receives one drawing, a snapshot of one child, the approved idea, either one main activity chosen by the Parent or one activity approved by the Teacher, and (for Teachers only) the approved lesson plan/narration direction. The output is a structured script with 5–15 scenes.
5. **Script confirmation gate:** an adult confirms the final version. Each AI rewrite, including after a video is flagged as inappropriate, creates a new revision and requires confirmation again.
6. **Scene reference image preparation:** uses the original drawing/approved reference frame and the versioned character anchor to keep the subject, shape, colors, and style recognizable. The source artwork must not be silently replaced or modified.
7. **Budget and quota approval and video creation:** the use case has one idempotency boundary at the application level but no SQL transaction spanning modules. AI Policy first creates a GPU/compute budget reservation with an idempotency key, lease/expiry, and a worst-case amount for the request; Billing then checks the scope/grant and writes exactly one `billing.video_quota_usage` atomically in its own transaction. If Billing rejects the quota, the application releases the reservation; a reservation orphaned by a stopped process is cleaned up/reconciled after the lease. If the admission response is lost or unclear, the application looks up the same idempotency key at both owners before dispatching, releasing, or returning an error; it must not create a second request/usage. Only when both receipts are confirmed does it write/dispatch the accepted generation via the outbox. Story keeps only an opaque reference to the receipts and does not own the budget/quota. An accepted request still consumes a unit if the provider, technical, or safety stage fails; a scene retry does not create a new unit. The Modal L40S GPU is used for LTX; Modal L4 is a candidate for smaller model stages. Model/runtime/license and quality evidence need separate benchmarking.
8. **Technical checks and assembly:** verify per-scene decoding, frame size, aspect ratio, scene order, and duration; FFmpeg assembles them into a 720p MP4 with a fixed adult voice and Vietnamese subtitles, with background music turned off.
9. **Automated safety on the assembled output:** check all narration/subtitles and sample frames from the final video at least one frame per second, and also take the first/middle/last frame of each scene.
