Use a versioned policy/model profile, taxonomy, model revision, thresholds, calibration evidence, and false-positive/false-negative evaluation as the gate for promoting a model. If the check fails, lacks coverage, or is indeterminate, set SAFETY_BLOCKED, do not issue a playback token, and return the adult to the script view. Record only the policy version, result, and safe finding codes; do not guess thresholds from the prompt. Thresholds must be a deployment gate until evaluated on the synthetic set.
10. **Playback:** the API issues a short-lived token only after both the technical and safety checks pass. Media is stored in private object storage; the application cannot obtain an unrestricted public URL.

The live demo is expected to run on local Docker with synthetic data and live inference through Modal. Local MinIO cannot be accessed directly by Modal; the file transfer contract is described in §24.3. The backend SDK transfers bytes within the allowlist into a separate per-job scratch prefix on a Modal Volume, sends opaque path refs/hashes to the remote function, downloads the result via the SDK, verifies the output before saving it to MinIO, and deletes the scratch. Raw media must not be passed in function arguments, and Modal must not be granted broad access to local storage. According to Modal's documentation on large/asynchronous payloads and storage regions, this flow uses synthetic data only until a separate review of cross-region transfer/processing of real data is approved. [Modal local data](https://modal.com/docs/guide/local-data), [Modal data residency](https://modal.com/docs/guide/data-residency), [Modal Volumes](https://modal.com/docs/guide/volumes).

### 6.2.1 SVG drawing guide and Teacher presentation

SVG is the output format for vector guides/illustrations in the product; FLUX is not part of the target path and must not be used as a fallback; the optional FLUX picture of the Teacher Sketch Deck is an additional exception and does not replace SVG (§31.21). The approved SVG catalog is the preferred source. If no suitable guide exists for a topic/asset, a Parent or Teacher may request that AI create a draft when the corresponding capability is enabled. The AI returns structured vector data according to a schema; the backend validates path/coordinate/dependency/provenance limits, and then a deterministic SVG engine serializes and renders it. Free-form SVG/HTML/JavaScript from the model or unsanitized uploaded sources is not accepted.

A guide is a sequence of 4–8 steps, adaptive to the complexity of the subject, from basic strokes to a sufficiently recognizable shape. Strokes from previous steps are displayed solid; new strokes of the current step are displayed dashed/highlighted; future strokes are not yet shown. The guide must be detailed enough for a child to recognize and draw the animal/object step by step; a simple fixed number of steps does not replace checking recognizability/teachability. The thresholds for structural metrics, the reviewer rubric, adjudication, and the gates for enabling each AI capability remain technical/product gates to be finalized.

A Teacher lesson is a versioned presentation, not just an automatically generated Sketch Deck. The Teacher may edit text, add/delete/reorder slides, upload original images, add valid YouTube/Vimeo links for embedding, select SVGs from the catalog, request an AI SVG draft when enabled, and place each media item on an appropriate slide. Media must pass type/sanitization/safety checks; external video is allowed only as HTTPS YouTube/Vimeo embeds, and the client does not fetch arbitrary content from the backend. The Teacher reviews the draft; the School Manager approves school-level scope, and the Platform Admin approves platform-wide public scope according to permissions. A published revision is not edited directly; changes create a new draft/version and preserve provenance/lineage.

The Teacher guide/presentation may be opened as prior knowledge before the child draws. A guide per lesson or topic is optional; errors, a missing catalog, a disabled capability, a timeout, or quality rejection do not block Parent capture or the Teacher continuing the lesson. A change to a lesson/guide revision makes dependent content stale according to policy; the exact dependency granularity and persistence mapping await the API/DTR contract.

The Parent has a separate general guide capability: a topic from the catalog or text entered by the adult; the drawing/image, profile, exact age, voice, name, and child ID must not be sent to line-sketch generation. The Parent may skip the guide. The final drawing image is still mandatory and may be analyzed only after capture. A guide is temporary/private if it is a draft, has provenance/expiry/cleanup, is not attached to a profile/StoryRun, and does not consume video quota.

Sketch/vector generation does not use or deduct `video_quota_usage`; it has its own rate/cost controls. Engine/asset output must be checked for schema, source/license, sanitizer, bounds, path references, safety, and lineage. The DTR mapping, API version, job persistence, review state, stage render/cache lifecycle, and exact asset ownership have not been finalized by Rev66; do not infer them from existing code/APIs.

### 6.2.2 Story-video pipeline per the Sketch2Life proposal

The pipeline applies to both Parent and Teacher, and accepts only one drawing of one child at a time. The steps from the pipeline set provided by the owner are adjusted according to the current rules: no collaborative/multi-child drawing; the script/storyboard is the only human approval gate; the final output passes through an automated safety gate and is then played immediately, without waiting for final video approval; playback is private within the run, with no public gallery/download.

| # | Stage | Input/output and controls | Technology/deployment boundary |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Drawing and idea | One immutable drawing image of exactly one child; the adult enters a text idea and/or confirms a transcript using the Whisper ASR direction. The activity list belongs to the lesson and is displayed after the video; the Parent must select one main activity before the script is created (BR-15; Rev66). | Android camera/library or Teacher browser camera/upload; resumable upload API; Whisper is a development direction, and the checkpoint/runtime/Vietnamese quality still need to be pinned and benchmarked. |
| 2 | Drawing understanding | The VLM identifies candidate characters/objects/settings; stores color, shape, stroke style, composition, visual anchors, and uncertainty tied to the drawing hash. The anchors describe only the selected child's drawing. | Qwen3.5-VL is the development model per owner guidance; runs via Modal. The pinned checkpoint, serving profile, and structured-output conformance must be confirmed in the deployment manifest/POC. |
| 3 | Input review | The adult reviews/confirms the transcript and subject; editing the subject creates a new VLM revision on the same image hash. The adult approves the minimal context and the lesson direction if any. The Teacher confirms the approved lesson/activity; the Parent chooses one main activity before the story. The main activity is the story context; the remaining activities of the lesson are displayed after the video and do not modify the script. | Parent/Teacher client + FastAPI application; `story.review_decisions`. |
| 4 | Story creation | The Qwen3.5-VL model direction creates/orchestrates structured story content comprising beginning-development-ending, characters/actions, and Vietnamese narration based on the drawing, the confirmed idea, the minimal personalization snapshot, and the lesson/script direction if any. The Parent chooses one main activity before the story; that activity is the story context and will take place after the video. The Teacher uses the approved activity from the plan. | Story Planner port, structured JSON output, activity/version snapshot, pinned prompt/policy version; model/checkpoint, safety, license, latency, and quality need a POC before promotion. In the development environment, text workloads (story, storyboard, activity suggestions, topic and drawing-subject suggestions, sketch-set drafts, and lesson plan suggestions) and drawing understanding run by default on a separately pinned checkpoint, Qwen3.8-27B-FP8, per the owner's decision of 2026-11-25; a code-level fallback option pinning Qwen3.5 still exists, but running it again on Modal requires reloading the weights; cost, safety, license, latency, and quality remain open, and this is not a production approval. This is only the development-environment default; the owner-selected product direction remains Qwen3.5-VL. Falling back to Qwen3.5 is a manual operation, never automatic (owner 2026-11-26). |
| 5 | Storyboard creation | The Qwen3.5-VL model direction assists in splitting the content into 5–15 scenes, with a total duration chosen by the system within 30–90 seconds; the target is about 6 seconds/scene but the planner may adjust according to the total duration. Each scene includes an action, a controlled image/video prompt, a narration segment, timing, and a character anchor. | Structured output + versioned prompt template; scenes are stored as rows, not kept as an unbounded list in a single JSON field. |
| 6 | Script/storyboard approval | The adult views the current story version and confirms the final hash/revision before generation. There is one initial version and at most three rewrites. Manual editing of individual scenes/order is not allowed in V1. | Parent/Teacher web/mobile review + FastAPI; `story.review_decisions`. The Teacher may give direction from the plan; the Parent submits suggestions. |
| 7 | Reference preparation | Create a Character Bible for the single selected drawing: identity anchors, color, shape, and style; prepare reference images/layout for each scene. Do not take images/profiles of other children. | Python + OpenCV/Pillow; segmentation such as SAM2 is only optional after a POC, not mandatory in V1. Derived images have their own asset ID/hash/lineage. |
| 8 | Per-scene video generation | Image-to-video creates a separate clip from the reference and the approved action. Independent scenes may run in parallel within GPU limits; scenes that need frame continuity run sequentially. Each scene has one initial attempt and at most two automatic retries; once attempts are exhausted the request fails, with no unlimited repeated calls. | LTX-2.5 official-compatible inference workflow, pinned PyTorch/workflow; Modal GPU L40S is the target per Q144. WAN is a separate dev candidate and does not replace the LTX target unless there is an updated owner decision/ADR. Modal L4 is not assumed sufficient for LTX by default. |
| 9 | Consistency check | Check the subject/character anchor, color, shape/style, distortion, unwanted details, and action fit. Retry scenes that fail within the limit of stage 8; if they still fail, stop the request and report a safety error. | OpenCV metrics + VLM/vision validation adapter; thresholds, model revision, and calibration are benchmark/promotion gates; do not assume a prompt guarantees consistency. |
| 10 | Scene assembly | Check decodability, frame size, aspect ratio, and order; assemble clips according to the storyboard, aligning duration/transitions as needed to create the overall video. | FFmpeg worker/container; scenes are ordered by `scene_index`, and the approved storyboard order is not changed. |
| 11 | Narration and audio creation | Create narration in a single neutral Vietnamese adult voice, aligned with the scenes; create synchronized Vietnamese subtitles. V1 always turns off background music and sound effects. | Vietnamese TTS adapter (VieNeu-TTS is only a candidate), subtitle timing checks, and FFmpeg to mux the narration/subtitles into the overall video. |
| 12 | Safety and output checking | After muxing/merging, check the entire narration/subtitles and the video frames against a versioned policy/model profile. Missing coverage, errors, or undetermined results must all block playback. This is an automated check, not a step in which an adult reviews the final video. | OpenCV + safety/VLM adapter; store the policy revision, results, and safety reason codes; do not write media/prompts to telemetry. |
| 13 | Video playback and regeneration | After both the technical check and the safety check pass, issue a short-lived playback token and play the video within the run. If an adult marks it as inappropriate, hide the old version when the replacement request is accepted, return to script review, and confirm before regenerating; at most two full-video regenerations after the first one. | Authorization-enforcing API + private object storage; no public gallery, download/export, or long-lived URL. The Teacher confirms the audience separately if showing it to a class. |
| 14 | Data lifecycle | Store private outputs together with provenance until an adult deletes the run; on deletion, delete the drawing, audio, transcript, story, scenes, and video according to the lifecycle; retain only the specified quota/sample claim and audit metadata. | Core private object storage + PostgreSQL; the quota/slot ledger is managed by the respective owning module. |

**Operational limits:** one full-video request has at most 15 scenes × 3 inference attempts = 45 scene attempts. Every accepted request has already consumed quota, including provider/technical/safety failures; the cost-cap/circuit-breaker closes admission for new requests and does not stop work already reserved normally midway. The GPU-second ceiling per request and the included-level quota must be benchmarked synthetically and configured before live demo is enabled. An AI job has a 6-hour deadline from accepted admission, including the queue. The target video output of 30–90 seconds is not guaranteed to be met in every quality case; the consistency gate must block faulty output rather than silently replacing the original drawing.

### 6.3 AI candidates and deployment gates

| Stage | Candidate | Proposed hosting | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vietnamese ASR | Whisper/faster-whisper per owner guidance | Modal; the dev dashboard has the function `transcribe-vietnamese-audio` on CPU | Model/checkpoint not confirmed from the dashboard; Vietnamese accuracy and latency must be measured, and the transcript must be confirmed by an adult. |
| VLM + story/storyboard | Qwen3.5-VL per owner guidance | Modal; GPU profile according to memory and benchmark | Do not use Qwen2.x for the new flow. Schema, safety, latency, license, and backend connection still need verification. The default in the development environment is Qwen3.8-27B-FP8 for all text and drawing-understanding workloads per the owner decision of 2026-11-25; the fallback option of pinning Qwen3.5 exists only at the code level (weights must be re-downloaded to run); cost, safety, license, latency, and quality conditions remain open. This is only the development-environment default; the owner-selected product direction remains Qwen3.5-VL. Falling back to Qwen3.5 is a manual operation, never automatic (owner 2026-11-26). |
| SVG guide/illustration generation | SVG catalog + deterministic engine; Qwen3.5-VL creates a structured vector draft when the catalog is lacking and the corresponding capability is enabled | Modal is the inference hosting direction; the local/backend engine parses, validates, canonicalizes, and serializes | FLUX is not part of the target and is not a fallback (exception, the optional Teacher Sketch Deck picture: §31.21). Topic-to-guide, grouping existing paths, and static illustration are separate capabilities that need a POC; schema, safety, license, quality, latency/cost, and dispatch are still gated. |
| Vietnamese TTS | A function that synthesizes Vietnamese narration in the Modal dev app | Modal CPU, as observed on the dashboard | Model/checkpoint, voice quality, and timing synchronization are not confirmed. |
| Image segmentation | SAM 2.1 optional, after deterministic image processing with OpenCV/Pillow | Modal is the inference target; the backend currently has a SAM 2.1 adapter from older slices | Not mandatory for V1; needs migration/verification on Modal, measurement of mask quality, and preservation of the original image/lineage. |
| Teacher SVG guide/visual draft | The SVG catalog is the default source; Qwen3.5-VL may create a vector draft when the capability is enabled | Modal is the development hosting direction; the deterministic SVG engine renders | Specific to story-video; does not accept child profiles/images and does not deduct video quota. POC, educator/curator review, quality/safety/license, stability, and cost remain gates. |
| Structured output | Pydantic v2 + versioned JSON Schema | Python API/worker | Mandatory for VLM, story/storyboard, Character Bible, sketch deck, and safety result |
| Reference/image preparation | Python + OpenCV + Pillow; SAM 2.1 optional | Modal worker when segmentation is enabled | Create derived assets with hash/lineage; segmentation is not mandatory for V1; end-to-end Modal integration is currently not confirmed. |
| Vietnamese narration voice | Candidate VieNeu-TTS | Modal L4 | Need to verify a neutral adult voice, subtitle synchronization, and license |
| Image-to-video | Official self-hosted pipeline LTX-2.5 per Q144 | Modal GPU L40S (48 GB) is the target requiring a POC | Not verified end-to-end; the dev dashboard has a separate WAN candidate worker. WAN is not a decision to replace LTX-2.5. If the owner wants to change the video model, a new decision/ADR is required, and the requirements, quality, cost, license, GPU, latency, safety, and recovery gates must be updated. |
| Scene consistency check | OpenCV metrics + versioned VLM/vision adapter | Modal L4 candidate or CPU worker for steps that do not need a GPU | Check identity/color/style/action anchors after scene generation; block if a threshold is violated or the result is undetermined |
| Safety on the completed video | Versioned safety policy/model + checks of frames, narration, and subtitles | Modal L4 candidate or CPU worker per POC | Runs after composition/TTS/subtitles; fail closed if coverage is missing, a violation exists, or the result is undetermined; do not issue a playback token |
| Video assembly | FFmpeg | Local container worker | Pin the binary and verify the output |

The official LTX documentation states a minimum requirement of 32 GB VRAM; Modal lists the L40S with 48 GB GPU RAM and the L4 as a smaller GPU option. This is the basis for choosing the L40S for LTX, but it does not prove that the LTX pipeline has been deployed/benchmarked successfully. [LTX requirements](https://docs.ltx.io/open-source-model/getting-started/system-requirements), [Modal GPU guide](https://modal.com/docs/guide/gpu).

<a id="srs-7"></a>
## 7. Functional requirements

Priority levels: **M** = mandatory in the product baseline; **S** = required to run the end-to-end demo; **G** = deployment gate not yet completed.

The catalog currently has 86 FRs, distinct by ID. AI Sketch for Teacher is a separate request/job flow from StoryRun; do not extend the story actor/subject to multiple children.

### 7.1 Shared identity and profiles

| ID | Priority | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| FR-C-01 | M | Authenticate Parent and Teacher with separate Firebase Authentication accounts; the backend verifies the ID token and determines an exclusive account scope by issuer/UID. Reject invalid role/setup requests with the generic response `ACCOUNT_SETUP_UNAVAILABLE`, without revealing in which scope a UID is registered; the client cannot grant or change a role claim itself. |
| FR-C-02 | M | Reject a run request that has no child profile or has more than one profile. Do not accept a list of profiles, a group, or a class as the subject of a run. |
| FR-C-03 | M | Enforce at most one active run per current owner using a database unique constraint/concurrency. The lock uses the Parent account or the current Teacher via `active_owner_parent_account_id`/`active_owner_teacher_id`; separate from creator provenance. `DRAFT` and `VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING` hold the lock; completed/cancelled/abandoned states release the lock. |
| FR-C-04 | M | Separate the owner scope of Parent profiles from that of organization-owned Teacher profiles. Do not create any cross-scope linking or lookup function. |
| FR-C-05 | M | Store profile changes as immutable revisions; retain age in months/age group, interests, observations, achievements, out-of-app experiences, and the information source if any. |
| FR-C-06 | M | Store separately the history of runs created by the app and out-of-app experiences entered by the adult. Allow the adult to review the minimum values put into each run's snapshot. |
| FR-C-07 | M | Restrict support-needs data according to profile permissions, excluded from the AI context by default; do not infer diagnoses. |
| FR-C-08 | M | Allow the adult to cancel an incomplete run or delete a run in COMPLETED, FAILED, or ABANDONED via the lifecycle. The command to cancel an incomplete run must release the active lock immediately; revoke upload/playback, cancel jobs on a best-effort basis, delete linked content, and report purge progress. Do not refund the quota of an accepted video request; retain the minimum quota metadata and the Teacher sample claim that succeeded. |
| FR-C-09 | S | Each asset has its own metadata and immutable source/hash/derived lineage; do not store binaries in relational rows or unbounded JSON. |
| FR-C-10 | M | Allow the Parent profile owner or the Teacher/actor authorized by the organization to request profile deletion; revoke profile/run access immediately and display the status until linked content is purged. The account deletion policy and the role for deleting Teacher profiles must be finalized before real data is used. |
| FR-C-11 | M | `DRAFT` and every state requiring an adult are adult-wait when there is no AI job; at +144 hours create an inbox reminder, attempt a best-effort push, and send a best-effort email if the current owner has a verified address; at +168 hours `VIDEO_READY` becomes COMPLETED and other waiting states become ABANDONED. Release the owner lock and retain content until the user deletes it; a send failure does not block run closeout, and a closed run does not resume. `VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING` is not adult-wait; an admission rejection returns the run to `SCRIPT_REVIEW` and starts a new wait. The generation deadline is six hours from `accepted_at`, including the queue and all stages (Q133, Q136). |
| FR-C-12 | M | Resumable uploads of images, audio, and lesson plan files store a durable session/receipt in `core.upload_sessions` and `core.upload_parts`, not stuffed into `core_asset_metadata`. Each app chunk of at most 4 MiB is a separate object via PutObject; the session is bound to principal/scope/purpose, expected media/size/hash, idempotency, and expiry. A chunk with the same number/hash is idempotent; the same number with a different hash returns a conflict. The backend verifies contiguous parts, total bytes, and SHA-256 of each part/the whole asset, assembles a bounded stream, then writes the final asset with a single PutObject; it does not call the S3 Multipart Upload API. Only a complete, media-validated, and AV-clean asset may be used. Rate limits are separated for control/status/chunk requests, concurrent sessions, and unique bytes/minute; retrying an already-received chunk does not recount the byte budget. The client does not receive MinIO credentials/endpoint. |

### 7.2 Parent flow

| ID | Priority | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| FR-P-01 | M | Restore the Parent Android flow on the current apps/mobile foundation; use apps/ui-mobile as the reference source for behavior/interface. |
| FR-P-02 | M | Allow the Parent to create/select a profile belonging to them; require one drawing image as a mandatory input before creating a StoryRun/analyzing the image (camera is the primary action, photo library is the alternate). Topic and lesson preset in the Parent Gallery are both optional. If the Parent selects a lesson, send the exact lesson/version pin so the backend can validate access permissions and the revision. |
| FR-P-03 | M | Accept an idea as text, as voice up to 2 minutes/20 MB, or both; use Whisper to convert voice to text and require the adult to edit/confirm it. |
| FR-P-04 | M | Analyze the drawing image and let the adult edit the subject; when edited, create a new VLM interpretation revision. |
| FR-P-05 | M | After the adult confirms the subject, the Parent must choose one main activity before creating the story. The backend filters catalog-first candidates from PUBLIC/SHARED lesson versions the Parent is permitted to view, by age, profile summary/tags, topic, and drawing context; a lesson pin restricts to the exact version/hash. Qwen3.5 selects IDs/reasons, and the backend returns the canonical content. If no suitable candidate exists, exactly 3 drafts may be created via a quality gate; a request for a new set must exclude choices already shown/rejected to avoid duplicates. Do not send the image/raw audio/child identifiers. The selected activity is the story context and takes place after the video. After the video, a lesson pin displays all activities of the exact version; with no pin, the selected main activity is displayed. An AI draft is not a catalog item/lesson draft. |
| FR-P-06 | M | Create a minimal run context from the selected profile revision and require the adult to confirm before the AI creates the story. |
| FR-P-07 | M | Allow at most three AI rewrites of the story after the first version, based on the parent's suggestions; there is no control for directly editing the scene/script. |
| FR-P-08 | M | Require the adult to confirm the final script/storyboard before creating the video. Display the video in VIDEO_READY immediately after it passes the checks; keep the run active until the Parent taps Complete/Regenerate or 168 hours of idle adult-wait elapse, at which point the run completes automatically; final video review is not required. |
| FR-P-09 | M | Allow an adult to mark a video as inappropriate and return to review the script; limit full-quality regeneration to two times after the first video. Hide the rejected video from playback as soon as the regeneration request is accepted; retain it internally until the run is deleted. |
| FR-P-10 | S | Display the stage and clear, recoverable errors; do not expose the prompt/model diagnostics in the UI. |
| FR-P-11 | S | In a drawing session, the Parent may enable or skip a progressive 4–8 step SVG guide for a generic topic. The approved catalog is preferred; when it has no match, the Parent may request an AI draft if the capability is enabled. Send only the topic/difficulty/guide contract; do not send the profile, specific age, voice, photo/drawing, or child identifier. Display output only after it passes schema/vector/safety/media checks; a private draft has provenance and expiry/cleanup. Do not attach it to a profile/StoryRun, do not consume video quota, and any skip/no-source/disabled/unavailable/timeout/quality rejection must not block the mandatory capture. The drawing subject may only be reviewed after capture. |
| FR-P-12 | M | When the Parent selects a topic/lesson before capture, image analysis must check topic compatibility. If it is not compatible, display a warning for the adult to change the topic or retake the photo at most once. After the retake, the subject recognized by the VLM from the new image is the subject of the story; the pinned lesson remains the source of knowledge/presentation and does not change automatically. |
| FR-P-13 | M | Topic/lesson is optional; the photo is mandatory. Lesson presentation/guide is shown only if selected or enabled. The primary activity is selected before the script; a replacement activity must not duplicate a choice that was already presented/rejected. After the video, display the selected activity and all activities of the exact pinned lesson version, if any. |


#### Parent branch without a Lesson Preset — contract decision (UC-02/FR-P-05)

A Parent who does not pin a lesson must still select one primary activity before the story. Catalog-first suggestion takes activities from published/shared lesson versions that the Parent is allowed to view; if a lesson is pinned, activities of the exact version are preferred. The adult selects one result. If there is no suitable result, AI may generate exactly 3 drafts through the quality gate. Requesting another set must exclude choices already displayed/rejected. Selection is mandatory; the exact recovery when there are no results or the suggestion fails remains OPEN-TECHNICAL. After the video, no-pin returns the activity pinned in the run snapshot; do not query the new catalog and do not create a fallback list. |

| Moment | Query/response rule | Permission/filter/version requirements | Empty, error and revoke | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Select one primary activity before the story | The backend filters currently renderable candidates from activities in PUBLIC/SHARED lesson versions that the Parent has permission to view; a lesson pin restricts them to the exact version/hash. Qwen3.5 selects request-scoped ID/order/reason and the backend returns the canonical content; if there is no suitable candidate, exactly 3 temporary drafts may be generated through the quality gate for the adult to choose from. | Filter by age and available metadata; use the context allowlist at §31.16/31.17, with no raw image/audio or identifiers. Requesting another set excludes IDs already displayed/rejected. | Only one choice is committed to the StoryRun. A candidate that is revoked/unpublished or no longer accessible must not be returned as current content; exact error/recovery/API remain OPEN-TECHNICAL. | Rev66 policy §31.17; the contract remains OPEN-TECHNICAL. |
| After playback, no lesson pin | Return the primary activity that the adult selected and pinned into the run context; do not query new `learning_content`, and do not automatically suggest more. | The activity ref/revision belongs to the run snapshot; do not hydrate from the current catalog if doing so would change approved content. | If the ref can no longer be read, keep the snapshot according to the retention/authorization rule; exact response/error remain OPEN-TECHNICAL. | Rev66 §31.17 rule; the route/snapshot contract remains OPEN-TECHNICAL. |

When a lesson pin exists, after the video return all activities from the exact lesson-version/hash after a current-access recheck, marking the selected primary activity; do not use latest in place of the pin.

### 7.3 Teacher and organization flows

| ID | Priority | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| FR-T-01 | M | Provide the Teacher flow on the existing web app apps/teacher-ui. |
| FR-T-02 | M | Allow an authorized organization School Manager to create classes within capacity, manage the roster, and assign exactly one responsible Teacher to each class. The permission storage/resolver approach remains OPEN at §31.10. |
| FR-T-03 | M | Provide 10 class slots in each organization's paid basic subscription, plus purchased slots; prevent concurrent class creation from exceeding the currently effective capacity. |
| FR-T-04 | M | Allow a Teacher to create a child profile using a structured quick form; allow duplicate display names and use opaque internal IDs. |
| FR-T-05 | M | Limit each Teacher profile to at most one active class enrollment; when moving classes, close and retain the previous enrollment. |
| FR-T-06 | M | Accept PDF/PPTX/DOCX lesson plans up to 25 MB and 100 pages/slides; reject encrypted/password-protected files; return OCR_NOT_SUPPORTED for scanned pages. |
| FR-T-07 | M | Retain the original lesson plan file and the provenance of extracted text; allow edits as a new immutable text revision; require the Teacher to review and approve before use. |
| FR-T-08 | M | Create an occurrence and plan for the teaching session, pinning an optional typed `LessonVersionRefV1` with exact version/hash when a reusable lesson is selected; the plan revision and Teacher StoryRun keep the same pinned ref/hash lineage and do not resolve `latest`. An occurrence may hold multiple activities; the Teacher sample pins one activity and one child/run as the baseline. The physical storage approach awaits approved DDL. |
| FR-T-09 | M | A Teacher's self-entered activity exists only within that occurrence and requires confirmation by the responsible Teacher through a safety checklist. |
| FR-T-10 | M | The system selects one child with equal probability from the valid eligibility/consent set; the Teacher views and confirms the child/context before creating the run; there is no branch where the Teacher arbitrarily chooses a child. A stale candidate is re-selected at most once; an empty set stops with no fallback; before confirmation, no run/job/quota/claim is created/accepted. Pin the approved activity if a preset exists and snapshot the minimal context into the run. Exact eligibility predicate, consent evidence, concurrency and response contract remain OPEN at Q-01. |
| FR-T-11 | M | Allow the Teacher to capture using the browser camera or upload a photo taken with a phone. |
| FR-T-12 | M | Allow the Teacher to set the story direction according to the approved lesson/class plan and to request up to three AI rewrites; each video generation must have the script confirmed. |
| FR-T-13 | M | Enforce at most one playable, safety-passed sample video per occurrence. A technical error or safety block before the first playback/claim does not occupy the slot. After the first claim, the slot is not reopened even if the video is rejected/hidden, regenerations fail/run out of attempts, the run is deleted/canceled, or the Teacher changes. |
| FR-T-14 | M | Run/media permissions follow the current responsible Teacher. Reassignment/offboarding is an idempotent saga: BE1 fences/revokes the old permissions and tokens immediately, and the class becomes read-only; Story moves `active_owner_teacher_id`/lock in a separate transaction; BE1 finalizes only after the Story receipt. While pending, no Teacher can view content. If the new Teacher has an active run, Story returns a conflict and the saga restores the old assignment/permissions; if compensation is ambiguous, keep pending/read-only for reconciliation. `created_by_actor_ref` is for traceability only. Do not cancel a valid AI job merely because the assignment changed. A result is accepted only if the run/input/script/activity/occurrence hashes are still valid, in the same org/class, not canceled/deleted, and the current transfer receipt proves the new owner; an old assignment version/creator does not grant permission. |
| FR-T-15 | S | Display the status and sample result of each occurrence; at VIDEO_READY allow the Teacher to present, Complete, or Regenerate; after 168 hours of adult-wait idle it completes automatically. There is no action that runs across the whole roster/batch. |
| FR-T-16 | M | Only the current responsible Teacher may perform the present action for the class. Record the occurrence confirmation with an audit; issue a short-lived playback authorization, and do not issue a reusable capability to class members. This is not final video approval. |
| FR-T-17 | M | Allow the School Manager of the organization that owns the class/run to force-close a conflicting Teacher run only through an explicit emergency command with a mandatory reason and audit metadata. Close the run as ABANDONED and release the active-owner lock, without deleting the run/media or letting the School Manager view content; accepted quota and a successful sample claim remain. Force-close is an explicit step before retrying reassignment, not automatic behavior of ordinary reassignment. The permission assignment/storage, scope and resolver remain OPEN at §31.10. |
| FR-T-18 | M | A Teacher may open a catalog/lesson SVG guide to present how to draw. A guide/AI draft is created only when the Teacher requests it; if not enabled, no AI is dispatched. The guide follows the subject/lesson knowledge, has 4–8 adaptive steps, and does not accept the roster/profile/child's drawing as input. |
| FR-T-19 | M | A Teacher lesson has a 16:9 in-app presentation, with per-slide text editing, adding/deleting/reordering slides, and placing an image/video/SVG on the selected slide. Support Teacher-uploaded images, HTTPS YouTube/Vimeo embed links, catalog SVG, and AI SVG drafts. The Teacher controls the slideshow; PPTX/PDF export is not required. The child's story video still follows the occurrence flow after the pipeline completes. |
| FR-T-20 | M | Presentation/guide pins the lesson/plan revision/hash. Editing the source creates a draft revision; dependent assets/guides must become stale according to dependency. AI SVG/media drafts must not be published before Teacher review; the School Manager approves school scope, and the Platform Admin approves platform-wide public scope, by role. Sketch/vector AI uses a separate rate/cost cap and does not write `billing.video_quota_usage`. |
| FR-T-21 | M | Preserve the original asset/provenance; image uploads go through type/size/malware/safety checks. External video may only be embedded from YouTube/Vimeo through an HTTPS allowlist; the backend does not fetch arbitrary URLs. Slide order/media refs belong to the presentation revision; a published revision must not be edited directly. |
| FR-T-22 | S | A Teacher may request AI to generate a draft SVG illustration when the capability is enabled; the backend checks schema, bounds, provenance, safety and license, then a deterministic SVG engine renders it. The Teacher previews/accepts/discards before attaching or publishing. Do not send raw lesson files or the child roster/profile to the model. |

### 7.4 AI story, video and playback

| ID | Priority | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| FR-AI-01 | M | Create typed/versioned jobs for lesson extraction/library-version draft/activity suggestion/visual asset/plan-to-deck/sketch-step regeneration, Parent line-sketch guide, and for story ASR, VLM image understanding, story/rewrite, storyboard, scene reference, video scene, consistency/safety validation, TTS, subtitle and composition. Each job is bound to exactly one target kind/ref/revision; a Story/video target resolves exactly one child from the canonical StoryRun, a Lesson Sketch target has no child/profile subject, and a Parent guide target has only a generic topic/difficulty and no profile/child/artwork refs. Do not store a duplicate subject ref in the AI Job. |
| FR-AI-02 | M | Validate every model response against a versioned schema before storing/displaying it. Reject malformed or stale output. |
| FR-AI-03 | M | Retain the original drawing/audio/lesson plan and record the source hash, model/profile revision, config hash, reason and actor for derived artifacts. |
| FR-AI-04 | M | Create a storyboard of 5–15 scenes and a target video of 30–90 seconds chosen by the system. |
| FR-AI-05 | M | Create an image-to-video story for one child, using the selected drawing as the reference; keep the subject recognizable together with shape/color/style anchors according to the quality target; do not use PixiJS drawing animation. |
| FR-AI-06 | M | Check decode/output of each scene and the configured character consistency criteria. A failed scene gets at most two automatic technical/validation retries after the first attempt. When retries are exhausted, report a safe error and stop the run. |
| FR-AI-07 | M | Limit to two full-video quality regeneration requests after the first request in the same run; each time, return to review the script and confirm. Each accepted request consumes a limit slot and one quota unit, including technical/safety failures; scene retries do not consume more. |
| FR-AI-08 | M | Output MP4, 720p, 16:9, one neutral Vietnamese adult narrator voice, Vietnamese subtitles, and no music. |
| FR-AI-09 | M | Open playback immediately once the composed video passes the technical checks and automatic safety gate; do not require a reviewer for the final video and provide no download/export. When a video is marked inappropriate and regeneration is accepted, hide the old output from playback. |
| FR-AI-10 | S | Issue a short-lived playback authorization and recheck run permissions on every playback. |
| FR-AI-11 | S | Track stage attempts, duration, errors, GPU seconds and artifact IDs, but must not log user content. |
| FR-AI-12 | M | Apply versioned safety checks to text/voice transcripts, extracted lesson-plan context, script/storyboard, scene prompts, composed video frames, the full narration transcript and subtitle text. Treat instructions as data, not as control authority. Block unsafe/unknown output from review or playback and return a safety reason code; must not expose internal prompts. |
| FR-AI-13 | M | Execute the story-video pipeline per BR-45: Character Bible/reference preparation from a single drawing, LTX-2.5 image-to-video via Modal L40S for each scene, consistency validation, FFmpeg composition, TTS/subtitle generation and automated safety validation before playback. Each scene and each full-video request must comply with the defined retry/quota limits; every stage is a durable, idempotent job and is checked for staleness by hash/revision. |
| FR-AI-14 | M | Generate/retrieve/render SVG guides according to a versioned structured contract from a catalog or a Teacher-selected source. A guide has 4–8 adaptive steps; validate coverage, path references, dependency/order, layout, media, age-safety, lineage and educator/curator approval before display/AI dispatch. Must not send roster/profile/children's drawings; must not generate activities on its own beyond the approved lesson. Teacher presentation and guide revisions must pin the version/hash; when the source changes, dependent content becomes stale. |
| FR-AI-15 | S | Parent guide uses the SVG catalog first; if none is available, an AI draft may be generated by topic/difficulty and capability flag. The guide is progressive with 4–8 steps; a deterministic engine sanitizes/validates/serializes. Must not send profile/exact age/photo/audio/artwork/identity; the private draft has provenance/lifecycle, is not attached to a profile/StoryRun, and does not use video quota. Skip/no-source/disabled/unavailable/timeout/quality rejection does not block mandatory capture. Exact job, asset and API persistence remain technical gates. |

### 7.5 Billing and notifications

| ID | Priority | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| FR-B-01 | M | Store versioned VND pricing for organization capacity by month/year, including a paid base plan with 10 slots and one unit price for each additional slot. The base plan price/additional slot price is commercial configuration, not yet finalized in the SRS. |
| FR-B-02 | M | Support the organization subscription lifecycle and a MoMo/VNPAY recurring payment adapter; exclude VietQR from V1. |
| FR-B-03 | M | Verify provider callback signatures, deduplicate provider events and reconcile invoice/subscription within a transaction. Must not treat a browser redirect as proof of payment. |
| FR-B-04 | M | Activate additionally purchased capacity after payment verification and charge prorated for the remainder of the period, without changing the renewal date. |
| FR-B-05 | M | Support turning off auto-renew, retaining access until the end of the paid period; V1 does not automatically calculate prorated refunds. |
| FR-B-06 | M | Apply a 7-day renewal grace period, after which creating new classes/runs is restricted; access to existing classes/roster/history and payment management is still allowed. |
| FR-B-07 | M | When reducing capacity, selecting classes is mandatory; record a versioned, timestamped suspension event for each selected class and project the state to BE1 at the correct effective period. A suspended class is read-only, and all data is retained. |
| FR-B-08 | M | Send reminders to the billing admin in-app/by email 7 days and 1 day before the due date; send notifications on payment success/failure and when grace ends. |
| FR-B-09 | S | Use a notification outbox and bounded retry; deduplicate by schedule/provider event. The in-app inbox is the canonical source; push/email are best-effort delivery channels. |
| FR-B-10 | M | Maintain monthly video quota for Parent accounts and school organizations; Teacher video uses the occurrence's organization, with no Teacher-account pool. Billing `video_quota_grants` and `video_quota_usage` are the canonical entitlement/consumption sources. Billing's quota-admission use case idempotently allocates each accepted request to a grant. AI Policy only owns platform resource metering/reservation. An accepted request consumes one unit even on provider/technical/safety failure; quota is not refunded; scene retry does not consume. No child identity or media is written to the ledger. |
| FR-B-11 | M | Before Billing admission, the application must reserve the configurable maximum GPU budget for the request via the AI Policy port using an idempotency key and lease/expiry. If the budget is insufficient, return `AI_COST_LIMIT_REACHED` before the request is accepted, without consuming quota. After budget reservation, Billing checks quota in a separate transaction; `VIDEO_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` does not dispatch, does not create a usage row and causes the application to release the reservation. If the process stops or the response is unclear, a reconciler looks up the receipt by idempotency key, expires reservations not attached to an accepted request and does not dispatch twice. Dispatch only after both the budget reservation and quota admission are confirmed. Running out of quota still allows playback/management of stored data. Compute remaining quota in Billing from valid grants minus the immutable usage ledger; do not store a mutable balance. Prioritize current-month included quota, then FIFO top-ups. |
| FR-B-12 | S | Provide an authenticated quota summary by backend-derived scope from the actor, including current-month included quota/expiry time, FIFO top-ups, granted/consumed/remaining and period boundaries; must not expose other accounts or runs/children. |
| FR-B-13 | S | Platform Admin configures/versions quota offers and prices. A Parent purchases for the Parent account; an assigned School Manager purchases for the organization. Billing supports MoMo/VNPAY and issues entitlement only after a verified/idempotent settlement event; quantity and price version are checked by the server, and invoices/grants are immutable. The monthly plan supports manual payment or auto-renew with clear authorization and the ability to cancel; a top-up is a one-time transaction and does not auto-renew. A quota purchase after successful settlement is not refundable. Existing renewals keep the accepted price (§31.20). The auto-renew cycle versus calendar-month grants, failed renewal handling per §31.20 remain open. A top-up does not expire until it is used up (BR-38; §31.20). |
| FR-B-14 | M | Send run-expiry, job-update and billing reminders via the in-app inbox, web/mobile push and email. A run reminder is sent by email only to the current owner's verified address (Q136); a billing notice is sent to the billing admin as configured. Web uses Web Push. The Android push provider has not been chosen; enable only one adapter after a synthetic POC proves the provider, distributor, app installation/distribution, opt-in, token revocation and background experience on target devices. Do not default to UnifiedPush or Firebase Messaging; Firebase Messaging is excluded by repository rule. The push payload contains only the notification type and an opaque route/ref; no child name, story, transcript, media or playback URL. Email/delivery records do not write the raw recipient address into notice content or logs. Push does not replace inbox/polling; the inbox always receives the notice even if push is unavailable. |

### 7.6 Platform and operations

| ID | Priority | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| FR-O-01 | M | Use a modular monolith with domain modules that have clear owners and dependencies following clean architecture. |
| FR-O-02 | M | Version the API, queue, event, asset and profile-scope contracts. Reject unsupported major versions. |
| FR-O-03 | M | Use PostgreSQL as the source of business/job data; Redis/RQ as the dispatch/backpressure/rate-limit channel; MinIO as local private object storage. |
| FR-O-04 | M | Configure provider credentials only in the backend worker/runtime secret. Mobile and the Teacher browser do not receive secrets/endpoints for Modal, payment, object storage or the database. |
| FR-O-05 | M | Return a safe, stable error envelope with a correlation ID, retryability and an optional retry-after time. |
| FR-O-06 | M | Apply server-side limits by account/IP/API, together with AI queue/GPU limits and finite provider deadlines for each stage/whole run. The first demo parameters are in §10.4 and need benchmarking. |
| FR-O-07 | S | Provide health/readiness probes, queue visibility, a dashboard and redacted logs for operators to read in Docker Compose. |
| FR-O-08 | S | Document manual PostgreSQL and MinIO backup/restore with synthetic data; restore must preserve lineage and linked metadata. |
| FR-O-09 | M | Grant Parent/Teacher roles from the server. The Teacher role and organization require an authorized provisioning/assignment process; the client must not attach Teacher/admin claims itself. Before launch, a description is needed for account recovery, Teacher invitation/activation and organization bootstrap. |
| FR-O-10 | M | Separate business `run_state` from the `job_state`/`job_stage` of ASR, VLM, story and video, and also return a separate VideoGeneration admission status. The Status API returns both states along with revision/version, deadline, update timestamp, safe error code, retryability and human action required so the UI can distinguish admission/AI running, waiting for an adult, or finished; admission reconciliation is not adult-wait. The AI job deadline is 6 hours, and the adult-wait idle deadline is 168 hours. |

<a id="srs-8"></a>
## 8. Non-functional requirements

The baseline catalog has 33 NFRs; NFR-34–NFR-38 are added for platform v2.3, for a total of 38 NFRs counted separately by ID; the AI evaluation, cost and performance items are benchmark gates, not measured results.

These requirements are targets for a local demo using synthetic data, not measured commitments or a production SLA. Confirm load/latency values only after an approved deployment plan and synthetic benchmark exist.

| ID | Group | Priority | Requirement/target |
|---|---|---|---|
| NFR-01 | Architecture | M | Domain code must not import FastAPI, SQLAlchemy, Redis, S3/MinIO, Firebase Admin, Modal SDK, payment SDK or UI packages. Use cases depend on ports; adapters depend inward. |
| NFR-02 | Authorization | M | Every access to profile/class/run/asset/playback is authorized by the server according to the actor and the current scope/assignment. Deny by default. |
| NFR-03 | Privacy | M | The demo uses only synthetic data. Redact payloads from logs/traces/metrics. Exclude names, image/audio bytes, transcripts, prompts, signed URLs and support notes. |
| NFR-04 | Data separation | M | The Parent profile owner is separate from the Teacher organization owner; do not use a polymorphic FK that could mistakenly join the two profile types. |
| NFR-05 | Data integrity | M | Database constraints enforce one child/run and one run/current-owner. Lock by an active-owner field separate from the creator; BE1/Story reassignment uses durable idempotent transfer phases instead of a cross-module atomic transaction, fences the old permission immediately, and does not grant new permission before the lock-transfer receipt; conflicts return 409, and emergency admin force-close requires audit. Enrollment, assignment, sample, rewrite/video cap, quota and payment callback still have their own invariants/idempotency. |
| NFR-06 | Asynchronous consistency | M | Each job is idempotent, versioned and bound to an immutable input hash. A stale result must not overwrite a newer run/script/profile/lesson revision. |
| NFR-07 | Recoverability | M | Restarting local Compose still retains the PostgreSQL/MinIO volumes. Manual backup/restore guidance is provided. Do not claim cloud uptime. |
| NFR-08 | Performance | S | Initial target: authenticated read requests that do not call AI, p95 ≤ 500 ms; command/job enqueue accepted p95 ≤ 2 seconds, excluding upload/inference. Benchmark with synthetic load before deployment. No AI latency commitment before model/GPU benchmarking. |
| NFR-09 | Media | M | Check MIME by content, extension, size, page count, decode and checksum; keep the private original together with derived versions that have provenance. |
| NFR-10 | Playback | M | Require short-lived, correctly scoped playback authorization. Do not issue permanent object URLs or file listing/download endpoints. |
| NFR-11 | Observability | M | Emit redacted structured logs, traces and metrics for the API, queue, job, stage, provider latency/failure, GPU, storage, billing notice and rate-limit events. |
| NFR-12 | Rate limit | M | Return HTTP 429 with Retry-After when limits are exceeded; key the rate by account/IP/operation, not by child ID or content. |
| NFR-13 | UX clarity | M | Stages and errors are easy for non-specialist adults to understand, in Vietnamese for V1 flows; group related upfront confirmations into few accessible screens without dropping mandatory review; do not display model stack traces. |
| NFR-14 | Maintainability | M | Contracts are versioned and type-checkable; domain states are explicit; repositories convert rows to domain objects; route/worker adapters are thin; avoid huge shared models and cross-module write SQL. |
| NFR-15 | Security | M | Secrets must come only from ignored local files or a runtime secret manager; .env.example contains placeholders only. Firebase is used only for Authentication. |
| NFR-16 | Migration safety | M | Start with a non-destructive expand/backfill; only after a separately approved migration may keys/nullability/checks be changed, using dual-read/write and parity evidence. Keep rows/IDs and compatibility until this can be proven; this baseline does not drop legacy tables/rows or change the meaning of IDs. Do not claim that every change is additive. |
| NFR-17 | Provider fault tolerance | M | Timeout/provider unavailable/malformed response/retries exhausted become typed job states; there is no automatic switch to another model/provider. |
| NFR-18 | Backup privacy | M | Backups contain only aggregated demo data until a real-data policy and a secure backup design are separately approved. |
| NFR-19 | Generated media quality/safety | M | A synthetic evaluation suite measures subject recognizability, color/shape anchoring, scene coherence, narration/subtitle synchronization, output format and safety gate coverage before a model is upgraded. Technical/safety checks run before playback; an adult may still flag a video as technically passing but inappropriate. There is no commitment to perfect identity preservation or a perfect classifier. |
| NFR-20 | Accessibility | M | The main Parent/Teacher flows support screen readers/semantic labels, keyboard operation on web, visible focus, sufficient contrast, text error summaries and captions/subtitles. If camera/mic permission is denied, upload/text entry alternatives remain available. |
| NFR-21 | Language | M | The V1 adult flows, status labels, validation and safety errors are in Vietnamese. API codes are stable and language-independent; date/money formats follow locale/timezone but stored values use UTC/VND. |
| NFR-22 | Demo load | S | The first proposed synthetic benchmark configuration: 10 concurrent authenticated clients, each with at most one run; one LTX video run executes concurrently and at most five videos wait globally. The scheduler selects eligible owner scopes in round-robin order, FIFO within each scope, and limits the number of waiting jobs per scope by configuration so that one Parent account/organization cannot occupy the whole queue. Status/wait time is displayed; when the queue is full, launch is safely rejected. This is a proposed local demo size, not a production SLA. |
| NFR-23 | Child content safety | M | The safety policy is versioned and enforced server-side for input/output. It must not rely only on the model prompt or the UI; adult review is supplementary but does not replace automated validation. |
| NFR-24 | Layered security | M | A STRIDE threat model exists for the client/API, DB, queue, object store, Modal, payment and notification boundaries. MFA is mandatory for org/billing admins; Android Play Integrity goes through the backend (Firebase App Check is not used); files in quarantine are antivirus-scanned before the parser/model; PostgreSQL RLS and least-privilege roles are defense-in-depth. The runtime DB role does not own tables and does not have BYPASSRLS. |
| NFR-25 | AI cost control | M | Before accepting a full-video request, the application must reserve enough GPU-seconds, based on the benchmarked per-request ceiling and the remaining daily/owner budget, through the AI Policy port. If the reservation cannot be made, the request is rejected before Billing admission and no quota is consumed. After a request is accepted, the reservation protects it from ordinary daily-cap exhaustion; the circuit breaker closes new admission and does not cut off requests that are already reserved. Only an emergency safety/operator abort may stop a request midway; per Q128, an accepted unit is not refunded. Limits on scenes/attempts/bytes/concurrency/queue are mandatory; alerts do not replace hard caps. |
| NFR-26 | Push notification | S | The inbox is the canonical state; push is best-effort, with minimal payload and deduplication. Lost push or denied push permission does not prevent polling/foreground refresh; business state must not depend on notification delivery. |
| NFR-27 | Availability, SLO and recovery | S | Local demo baseline: containers must report readiness within 10 minutes after `docker compose up`; manual PostgreSQL/MinIO backup has a target RPO ≤24 hours and a target RTO ≤4 hours in a local drill. These are proposed targets, not yet measured. Proposed production availability target is 99.5%/month for the control-plane API, excluding provider/Modal; production RPO/RTO are not yet finalized and an ADR/owner approval is mandatory before deployment. The end-to-end AI SLO can only be finalized after the L40S benchmark; the current hard deadline is 6 hours/job. |
| NFR-28 | Storage and capacity | M | The output MP4 file must be ≤256 MiB; input/run caps follow §10.4/§24.2. Capacity must be planned using the formulas `monthly_object_bytes = accepted_runs × (source_assets + retained_intermediates + final_outputs)` and `monthly_gpu_seconds = accepted_video_requests × measured_p95_gpu_seconds/request`; request counts and concurrency must be put into the benchmark/caps before the live demo. The included quota amounts and the number of runs/month are not finalized, so there is no production storage/GPU estimate yet. |
| NFR-29 | Accessibility | S | Teacher Web aims for WCAG 2.2 AA; Android is assessed against WCAG2ICT/platform accessibility guidance. Verification includes automated accessibility scans and manual keyboard/screen reader/contrast/focus checks for the main flows; this is a design goal, not an independent certification. |
| NFR-30 | Client compatibility | M | The Android support floor is API 29 (Android 10) per the repository configuration; the current target/compile SDK must continue to follow the inventoried Gradle pin. Teacher Web supports the two latest stable versions of Chrome and Edge on desktop. The release checklist records the OS/browser versions and smoke verification is performed before the demo. |
| NFR-31 | Data retention limits | M | The synthetic demo follows Q66/Q129/Q133: COMPLETED/FAILED/ABANDONED runs and media are kept until the user actively deletes them; there is no date-based auto-purge. This is a demo choice and must not be applied to real child/production data. Before the real-data gate, the following must be decided: maximum retention per data type, backup expiry, DSR, deletion from backups or crypto-shredding, and evidence that deletion is complete. |
| NFR-32 | Network-interruption-tolerant upload | M | Uploads of images up to 20 MiB, audio up to 20 MB/2 minutes and lessons up to 25 MB must support resuming after the app/browser or connection is interrupted. Use application chunks of at most 4 MiB, each chunk being an independent PutObject object; `core.upload_sessions`/`core.upload_parts` store the durable session/receipts. Resume sends only the missing parts; an exact retry does not add to the unique-byte budget. The backend checks order, size, and SHA-256 of each part/the whole file, assembles a bounded stream, then writes the complete asset with a single PutObject of known size (demo files up to 25 MB, below the 128 MiB single-PUT threshold stated in the MinIO Go SDK documentation; a POC on the exact client version is needed). This is not the S3 Multipart Upload API. |
| NFR-33 | Teacher presentation capability | M | The Teacher Sketch Deck displays stably on a 16:9 viewport of 1280×720 or larger, scales to the projector, keeps high-contrast line-art on a white background and uses short text. The Back/Next/skip/select activity controls have semantic labels/focus; content does not depend on color alone; text/controls meet WCAG 2.2 AA contrast. Verified by render review on a 16:9 viewport, keyboard-only testing and a trial projection check. |

<a id="srs-8-delivery"></a>
### 8.1 Priority, verification and phasing

Priority `M` (Must) and `S` (Should) indicates necessity in the target phase, not approval status. The functional catalogue (§7) and the NFR catalogue (§8) record the priority for each ID. The §30.3 matrix is the traceability source by ID for source/rationale, UC/scope, entity owner, verification method, phase and status. "Verification" is the intended acceptance method, not evidence that tests have been run. Requirement status is `DRAFT` until the owner approves the document; a Q/BR source does not imply implementation acceptance.

| Proposed technical phase | Verification outcome | Typical requirements | Boundary |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0 — Foundation/contract | Modular monolith, auth/scope, owner schema, outbox/job, Docker, synthetic fixtures, security basics, quota admission, hard cost reservation and resumable upload contract | FR-C, FR-O, NFR-01–18, NFR-24–28, NFR-32 | Quota/cost reservation must not be removed from the video flow; an upload adapter POC is needed before media integration; real payment providers are not yet needed for fixtures |
| P1 — Parent vertical slice | Parent profile → photo/idea → subject/activity/context review → story review → Modal video → playback/delete, on synthetic data | FR-P, FR-AI, subset of FR-C/NFR, resumable media upload | No real child data; quota fixture/config is provided by Billing |
| P2 — Teacher vertical slice | Organization/class/roster → profile → lesson import/review → occurrence/one child sample → script confirmation → video/presentation/reassignment | FR-T, shared FR-AI/FR-C | One child/activity/run; no classroom collaboration/batch |
| P3 — Billing and notification | MoMo/VNPAY sandbox, class capacity, recurring/subscription state, quota top-up, inbox/email/Web Push, Android provider POC | FR-B, FR-O-09, FR-B-14 | Sandbox before production merchant; Android push is promoted only after a delivery POC |
| P4 — Production readiness | Hosting/region, SLO/RPO/RTO, legal/retention/DSR/DPIA, billing/tax, threat model evidence, migration rehearsal | NFR-24–32; §29.2 | Real child/production data is blocked until each gate has an owner and evidence |

All Parent and Teacher flows, product quotas, the video pipeline and the confirmed business rules remain within the V1 target. The phase table is a proposed build/verification order; it does not remove requirements or claim that users have approved a release plan.

<a id="srs-9"></a>
## 9. Target software architecture

### 9.1 Clean architecture and module boundaries

Keep the modular monolith until runtime data proves that independently deployed services are needed. Dependencies point inward:

| Layer | Responsibility | Must not depend on |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | Parent profile, organization roster/capacity, lesson occurrence, story run, review, billing policy and state machines | Framework, ORM/database, queue, cloud provider, model SDK, HTTP/UI |
| Application | Commands/queries, transactions, use case orchestration, authorization, ports, idempotency and stale-result handling | Specific client infrastructure |
| Contracts | Versioned schemas for HTTP/event/job/asset/provider request/response; validation at the boundary | ORM rows or unvalidated provider payloads |
| Interfaces | Thin FastAPI routes, queue consumers, scheduled notification command adapters | Business branching, direct SQL, direct calls to AI/payment SDKs |
| Infrastructure | SQLAlchemy/Alembic, PostgreSQL, Redis/RQ, MinIO, Firebase token verification, Modal, MoMo/VNPAY, email, OTel | Owning Domain business rules |
| Apps | Parent React Native, Teacher React web, device camera/audio adapters, screens and generated contract types | Database/provider credentials or direct access to providers/storage |

Each module owns its tables and write permissions. Module communication uses versioned commands/events with opaque references through application ports. A module must not query another module's private tables to infer permissions or modify state.

### 9.2 Proposed code structure

    apps/
      mobile/                         Parent Android (React Native + TypeScript)
      teacher-ui/                     Teacher and organization (React + Vite)
    backend/src/sketch2life/
      domain/
        identity/ organization/ roster/ parent_profiles/ profile_context/
        lesson/ story/ billing/ notifications/ assets/
        learning_content/ ai_jobs/ ai_policy/ (domain owners, proposed)
      application/
        commands/ queries/ services/ ports/ policies/
        learning_content/ ai_jobs/ ai_policy/ (owner use cases and ports, proposed)
      contracts/
        http/v1/ events/v1/ jobs/v1/ schemas/
      interfaces/
        http/routers/ dependencies/ workers/ schedules/
      infrastructure/
        persistence/ object_storage/ queue/ identity/
        learning_content/ ai_jobs/ ai_policy/ (adapters only, proposed)
        ai/modal/ payments/momo/ payments/vnpay/ email/ telemetry/
    backend/alembic/                   Versioned migrations, grouped by table owner
    infra/                             Docker Compose profiles and monitoring config

This is a proposed target structure and does not assert that the packages already exist. Each business concept belongs to exactly one domain module. A SQLAlchemy row is not a domain entity; mapping is done at the repository boundary. Worker completion is an application command that verifies the expected revision before saving.

### 9.3 Technology baseline

| Scope | Proposed baseline/target | Rationale/status |
|---|---|---|
| Parent Android | React Native 0.87 + TypeScript currently pinned in apps/mobile | Keep the repository baseline; restore the reference behavior of apps/ui-mobile. Do not describe 0.87 as the latest upstream version. |
| Teacher web | React 19 + Vite 8.3 currently pinned in apps/teacher-ui | Keep the current app, converting the collaborative product flow into a separate Teacher flow. |
| Backend | Target Python 3.13, FastAPI, Pydantic v2, SQLAlchemy 2, Alembic | Python 3.13 needs bug-fix support and compatibility with the repo's <3.14 limit; evaluate Python 3.14 after dependency/Modal runtime review. Keep the 3.12 baseline for legacy workloads until the migration is approved and 3.13 is verified. |
| Database | PostgreSQL 17 in Compose/local demo | Matches the repo/migration baseline and is supported. PostgreSQL 18 is the current upstream major; migrations/extensions must be evaluated before upgrading, especially in production. |
| Queue/cache/limits | Redis 8 + RQ | PostgreSQL/outbox holds durable work; Redis/RQ is used for dispatch, backpressure and rate limiting. |
| Object store local | MinIO, private S3-compatible | Stores original and locally derived media/lessons; binaries must not be stored in PostgreSQL. |
| Authentication | Firebase Authentication only | The backend verifies the ID token and handles authorization; Firebase services that store product data are prohibited. |
| AI hosting | Modal is the inference hosting direction during the development phase | Only the backend worker/adapter may call the provider; production region, credentials, data transfer, retention and end-to-end integration remain gates. The code has a Lightning adapter from earlier slices; its presence does not change the target and does not prove that Modal has been wired into the main API. |
| VLM + story/storyboard | Qwen3.5-VL is the development direction chosen by the owner | The checkpoint/serving profile must be pinned and schema, safety, quality, license, latency and cost must be verified; the NO_GO of Qwen3-VL must not be extended to this family. |
| ASR | Whisper is the development direction chosen by the owner | The implementation/checkpoint must be pinned, Vietnamese/latency measured and retention finalized; an adult still confirms the transcript before the story is created. |
| Line-sketch/lesson plan images | SVG catalog + deterministic vector engine is the target; Qwen3.5-VL is the direction for generating structured vector drafts when the catalog is lacking | FLUX is not used for the SVG guide/engine; the only exception is the optional Teacher Sketch Deck picture (§31.21). The model does not return free-form SVG/HTML; the backend checks schema/safety/license/age suitability and then serializes/renders. Each capability, checkpoint, serving, quality, latency/cost and license needs its own POC/promotion gate. |
| Image segmentation | SAM 2.1 optional | Enabled only after benchmarking mask/crop fallback with synthetic data; not required to continue the flow if segmentation does not pass. |
| Video model | Official self-hosted pipeline LTX-2.5 per Q144; Modal L40S is the target requiring a POC | No end-to-end verification yet. The WAN worker is a separate development candidate; it does not replace LTX-2.5 without a new owner decision/ADR. |
| Smaller AI stages | Modal L4 is a candidate | Used for lighter ASR/VLM/LLM/TTS if benchmarks are suitable. |
| Media | FFmpeg | Assembles scenes, narration/subtitles and performs MP4 technical checks. |
| Monitoring | JSON structlog, OpenTelemetry SDK/Collector, Prometheus, Grafana | Proposed for local Docker Compose; the production alert destination has not been chosen. |
| Email local | Mailpit | Captures local payment reminder emails and does not send them externally. |
| Container | Docker Compose | API, worker, local stateful dependencies/monitoring; the mobile emulator runs on the host; Teacher web uses a dev server or a Compose profile. |

React Native 0.88 was released upstream on 2026-10-19. This is an upgrade candidate and does not change the baseline automatically: first review compatibility of apps/mobile, React/Metro, native modules and Android/iOS tooling. Vite 8.3 is the current pin in teacher-ui. Compose pins PostgreSQL 17 for repository compatibility; PostgreSQL 18 is evaluated at the migration/deployment gate. Official sources and detailed version status are recorded in §25.6.

**Current platform state in the repository:** apps/mobile is currently mostly a fixture/contract foundation; apps/ui-mobile is a behavior reference source, not a production Parent flow to be restored. Camera capture, audio recording and voice upload must be implemented together with device permission handling. The SRS does not assume that all screens/integrations already exist. When planning Parent, both apps must be checked and behavior mapped to supported React Native APIs before the flow is closed.

The versions stated above reflect the repository baseline or the proposed target. Patch versions, model revisions, CUDA/runtime images and licenses must be pinned in an approved implementation plan. Provider docs/GPU capabilities may change; re-check before deploying.

### 9.4 Local deployment and secrets

Target Docker Compose services:

- api: HTTP interface FastAPI.
- worker: RQ worker together with the Modal/payment/email adapters.
- PostgreSQL: persistent volume and health check.
- Redis: queue/rate-limit state.
- MinIO: private local object store and persistent volume.
- OTel Collector: receives application traces/metrics/logs and exports locally.
- Prometheus/Grafana: scrape and display local metrics.
- Mailpit: local SMTP sink for billing notifications.
- Optional Teacher web profile: build/dev server.

Android runs using local development tools/emulator and calls the API; the Android production build contains no private key/endpoint. Compose uses named volumes, readiness checks and health endpoints. Real .env files/provider credentials must stay local and be ignored; .env.example contains only placeholders. Secrets must not be printed in terminal evidence.

Modal does not access local MinIO directly. The synthetic demo uses the backend SDK to move files into a separate per-attempt scratch prefix on a Modal Volume, passes only path/hash refs, downloads the output via the SDK for checking, stores it in MinIO if it is safe/playable, and then purges the scratch. Detailed limits/cleanup are in §24.3; a synthetic file transfer POC is needed before video integration.

The local demo architecture has not yet chosen the production host/region, production object storage, email delivery, alert destination, disaster recovery target or production scale.

### 9.5 Versioned boundary contracts

| Contract | Purpose | Mandatory invariants |
|---|---|---|
| OwnerScopeRefV1 | Resource with an owner scope | kind is ORGANIZATION, PARENT_ACCOUNT or GLOBAL; GLOBAL is used only for platform catalog/policy; IDs are opaque; the backend derives the actor for authentication |
| OwnerResourceRefV1 | Reference between two modules | `owner_module`, `resource_kind`, opaque `resource_id`, `owner_scope`, `contract_version`; add a revision/hash pin when the resource is immutable. The target owner verifies the ref via a port at admission/completion/reconciliation; do not create a reference registry table or cross-schema FKs. |
| ProfileRefV1 | Reference to a Parent/Teacher child profile | Profile type matches the owner; cross-role read needs an active ShareGrantRefV1, purpose/field scope/version check and revoke |
| StoryRunCommandV1 | Create/update a run | Exactly one scalar profile ref, an adult actor and an input revision; array/group refs are rejected |
| LessonOccurrenceRefV1 | A Teacher teaching session | Class, current responsible Teacher and one approved plan revision |
| VideoQuotaAdmissionV1 / VideoQuotaSummaryV1 | Admit one full-video request and display the current quota | Scope is the Parent account or school organization (Teacher based on occurrence), derived by the server; one unit/request is accepted; has a grant ref and idempotency receipt; contains no child data/media |
| ClassProjectionConfirmationV1 | Confirm the audience/purpose of projecting a sample video for an occurrence | Current responsible Teacher, occurrence ref, safety-passed generation ref, confirmation timestamp and assignment revision; grants no reusable capability to class members |
| AssetRefV1 | Original/derived media or temporary guide | Opaque asset ID, immutable version/hash, media type, source link, access policy and expiry when the asset has a temporary lifecycle |
| AiJobCommandV1 / AiJobStatusV1 | Asynchronous AI stage for Story/video, lesson extraction, Teacher Deck or Parent guide | Exactly one target kind/ref pair: `STORY_RUN`, `VIDEO_GENERATION`, `VIDEO_GENERATION_SCENE`, `LESSON_SOURCE_DOCUMENT`, `LESSON_LIBRARY_VERSION`, `LESSON_VERSION_VISUAL_ASSET`, `LESSON_SKETCH_DECK_REVISION` or `PARENT_SKETCH_GUIDE`; expected target revision/input hash, stage, attempt and safe result/error. A StoryRun target resolves exactly one profile from Story; a video scene target resolves the scene/generation/run via the Story port; both Lesson targets have no child profile; a Parent guide has only generic topic/difficulty. `correlation_id` links jobs belonging to the same generation/run but does not replace the target. |
| StoryRunDetailV1 | One aggregated response for the client | Current run/input/script/video pointers, safe stage, scene/job statuses, authorized asset/playback refs and quota summary via owner ports. Does not return prompts, raw transcripts, hidden story revisions, foreign-scope existence or provider error detail. |
| ScriptRevisionV1 | Story/script/storyboard | Immutable revision/hash, 5–15 scenes, target length 30–90 seconds |
| SketchDeckV1 / SketchDeckStepV1 | Line-sketch deck per occurrence plan | Occurrence ID, approved plan revision/hash, target age band, 16:9 template version, activity ref, stable instruction-group key, adaptive 4–8 ordered progressive drawing-step records per guide, bounded layout hint, concise child-facing prompt/explanation, asset ref/hash/lineage, model/schema/template version; no profile/child or video quota ref |
| PaymentEventV1 | Provider callback | Provider/event/order ref, signature verification result, idempotency key, amount/currency |
| NotificationCommandV1 | Scheduled/event-based notification | Recipient role, template/version, trigger ID, dedupe key; contains no child content |

The client polls a versioned job status, starting every 2 seconds with exponential backoff up to 10 seconds; it stops at a terminal state and reduces/pauses polling when the app runs in the background. ETag/job version avoids unnecessary fetches. SSE/WebSocket is added only if data proves it is needed and with a separate ADR. The contract is the authoritative source; mobile/web types are generated or derived from the contract.

### 9.6 Proposed target API

The routes below are resource groups, not the final OpenAPI. Outside local development, every route uses HTTPS and authorization, except auth bootstrap/health and provider callbacks.

| Route group | Main operations |
|---|---|
| /v1/me | Resolve the verified identity, role and granted scope |
| /v1/parent/profiles | List/create/revise/delete the Parent's own profiles |
| /v1/organizations/{org}/classes | List/create classes, assign Teachers, manage rosters within the entitlement |
| /v1/teacher/profiles | Create/edit profiles via form and profile context |
| /v1/lessons and /v1/lesson-occurrences | Import, extraction status, edit revisions, plan occurrences and approve |
| /v1/lesson-occurrences/{id}/sketch-decks | Generate a deck from an approved plan revision, read the current status/revision, get step/asset refs and regenerate exactly one sketch step. Each command checks occurrence assignment, plan hash, idempotency key and sketch-specific rate/cost admission. |
| Parent drawing-guide resources (version/route TBD) | Read approved guides from the SVG catalog; request a draft guide by topic when the capability is enabled; view preview, accept/discard, read stage/render; catalog/AI errors are returned clearly and do not block capture. |
