| Teacher lesson presentation/slide/media resources (version/route TBD) | Read/edit draft presentations; create/edit/delete/reorder slides, text and media placement; upload images or reference YouTube/Vimeo/SVG; use revision/concurrency guards and keep the published version immutable. |
| Teacher SVG stroke-guide resources (version/route TBD) | Select the exact source asset/version; read/classify the source; create a manual or AI draft guide, edit metadata/order within limits, preview stage, review/accept; keep source geometry immutable and check organization/lesson permission. |
| /v1/activities | Proposed route to read the published/approved Parent catalog; exposure to Parent runs without a preset and the response/filter contract remain OPEN at §31.10; Teacher selects an activity according to permissions |
| /v1/story-runs | Create a single-child run, submit drawing/idea, review stage, request rewrite, confirm script, complete/delete |
| /v1/story-runs/{id} | Read the aggregated StoryRunDetailV1 of current state and safe timeline; the backend composes from Story, AI Jobs, Core Asset, Lesson/Billing ports depending on the run, with no cross-schema SQL or multiple endpoints forcing the frontend to stitch them together |
| /v1/jobs/{job_id} | Poll versioned AI job/stage status and safe errors |
| /v1/story-runs/{id}/playback-token | Issue short-lived playback permission after authorization |
| /v1/lesson-occurrences/{id}/projection-confirmation | Separately confirm projecting a sample video for the occurrence |
| `/v1/video-quota` and `/v1/video-quota/purchases` (TO-BE; active offer/provider configuration required) | Read quota by scope; a Parent purchases for their own account; a School Manager purchases for the assigned organization. The backend takes the actor/scope from the verified session and grants only after verified settlement |
| /v1/organizations/{org}/subscription | Read/update plan, capacity, payment/renewal and downgrade requests |
| /v1/payments/{provider}/callback | Verify MoMo/VNPAY callbacks and discard duplicate events |
| /v1/notifications | Read the in-app inbox, cursor pagination, and acknowledge as read |
| /v1/notifications/push-endpoints | Register a Web Push endpoint or Android provider adapter after authenticating the actor/device proof; revoke the current actor's endpoint |
| /health/live, /health/ready, /metrics | Operational probes/metrics, appropriately protected |

Use pagination for rosters/history; verify request size/content type; require an idempotency key for commands that change state or relate to payment. Do not return storage URLs, provider prompts, or internal tracebacks.

The new resources above are target requirements, not frozen routes. The exact API version, path, request/response schema, error enum, idempotency/concurrency contract, and persistence owner must be reconciled in M0 against the current OpenAPI and DTR; do not treat the API V2 proposal in the plan as an implemented API.

### 9.7 Authoritative data sources

Each business concept has one canonical data owner. Other modules hold only versioned references/events and do not maintain competing mutable copies.

| Business fact | Canonical source | What other modules may hold |
|---|---|---|
| Signed-in adult identity and exclusive Parent/Teacher scope | Firebase Authentication proves the issuer/UID; the BE1 identity registry owns the exclusive account-scope assignment | Typed, opaque actor/scope reference and assignment version; a Firebase claim is not the sole authority for authorization |
| Teacher organization, class, assignment, and roster | BE1 | Opaque reference and assignment version in the lesson/story contract |
| Parent's child profile | Parent profile module | Opaque Parent profile ref and approved run snapshot |
| Teacher profile revision | be1.profile_revisions; be1_managed_profiles is the current head projection | Story holds an immutable revision ref/hash and the approved values |
| Current class membership | BE1 enrollment row | be1_managed_profiles.class_id is only a current-class projection; updated in the same application transaction as the enrollment |
| Revision of adult-entered profile/context | Profile owner (Parent or BE1) | Story holds a minimal, immutable snapshot ref/hash and the approved items; in-app history and out-of-app experience remain two separate sources |
| Permission/consent to process real data | BE1/Parent authorization record, only for the real-data scope | A synthetic profile has data_origin=SYNTHETIC_TEST and does not fake consent; legacy data of unknown origin must not be treated as having consent |
| Published Parent activity | Learning Content | Story/lesson holds the selected version together with an immutable activity snapshot/hash |
| Teacher lesson plan and occurrence plan | Lesson | Story holds the exact approved plan revision/hash and a snapshot of one activity |
| Teacher Sketch Deck revision and order of presentation content | Lesson | A sketch job holds only versioned occurrence/plan/deck revision refs; Core holds assets/lineage; the deck holds no child/profile ref |
| Entitlement/grant and video usage quota | Billing `video_quota_grants` + `video_quota_usage` | Story holds only an opaque admission/usage ref via a versioned contract; AI Policy receives no product-quota ref or balance |
| GPU capacity/cost and platform compute | AI Policy `resource_scope`, `budget_bucket`, `resource_reservation`, `resource_event` | One append-only resource event holds the idempotency/fingerprint/result snapshot and delivery state; the legacy `resource_receipt` source is kept read-only. Billing does not use AI Policy to issue grants, deduct product quota, or compute balance |
| Story/run lifecycle, child, script, and request limits | Story; the revision/generation rows are canonical, the run-head pointer is only a projection | A Story command verifies owner-local targets, then changes the pointer, aggregate version, and run-state projection in the same transaction; the generation lifecycle state remains canonical |
| Dispatch job, attempt, and provider completion | AI Jobs; the attempt row is the sole retry/transfer ledger, the job row is the current state | One job has one target kind/ref; a scene records the source job ref to the correct generation scene. Do not keep a separate retry counter; job events are append-only; do not write new legacy JSON history/result-array |
| Model policy/profile and provider-copy evidence | AI Policy owns the policy/model profile; the old provider-copy registry is legacy/read-only | The job holds the profile/version ref/hash; synthetic Modal workspace/cleanup metadata lives in the attempt/event. A new provider-copy registry only after the real-data gate |
| Asset bytes, hash, media type, and byte count | Separate object store via the Core Asset port; core_asset_metadata is the canonical metadata, core.asset_lineage owns the provenance edge | Story/job holds only the asset ref/version, and does not copy the hash/output metadata |
| Subscription, invoice, payment, and organization class capacity | Billing; subscription_events owns per-class capacity changes, append-only | The BE1 class state is a fast-read projection of the effective capacity/suspension events; class/run creation queries a versioned entitlement port |
| Inbox, push endpoint, and in-app/email/push delivery state | Notifications | Producers emit a typed notification request and dedupe key; the inbox is canonical, push is best-effort |

Authoritative data sources avoid duplicate owners and a fact living in two places. `core_outbox_events` emits domain events owned by Core; `ai_jobs.outbox` emits AI dispatch commands; they do not share the same responsibility. The data-table register records each owner's write model and the retained source columns. The StoryRun detail API is an application-level read composition through the owner ports; do not write mutable copies into a separate cache/read-model table.

### 9.8 Standard error contract

Every API error returns a safe, stable envelope:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| code | Stable, machine-readable domain/error code |
| message | Safe explanation, localized into Vietnamese; does not reveal internal providers or resources in another scope |
| correlation_id | ID linking the request/job to the trace |
| retryable | Whether the client/operator may retry |
| retry_after_seconds | Optional; returned when the rate/backpressure limit allows the time to be determined |
| field_errors | Field error codes for validation; do not echo upload content |

| HTTP | Error code/example |
|---|---|
| 400 | INVALID_REQUEST, INVALID_FILE_CONTENT, INVALID_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY |
| 401 | AUTH_REQUIRED, INVALID_TOKEN, TOKEN_EXPIRED |
| 403 | ACTION_NOT_ALLOWED (only when the resource has been determined to be in the same scope but the actor lacks permission to perform the action) |
| 404 | RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND (the same shape for a nonexistent ID and an out-of-scope resource; does not reveal membership, assignment, or existence) |
| 409 | ACTIVE_RUN_EXISTS, TEACHER_ACTIVE_RUN_CONFLICT, TEACHER_ASSIGNMENT_REQUIRED, ACCOUNT_SETUP_UNAVAILABLE, CLASS_CAPACITY_REACHED, INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION, STALE_REVISION, SKETCH_DECK_STALE, SKETCH_DECK_NOT_READY, SAMPLE_VIDEO_ALREADY_EXISTS, STORY_REWRITE_LIMIT_REACHED, VIDEO_REGENERATION_LIMIT_REACHED, SUBSCRIPTION_RESTRICTED, VIDEO_QUOTA_EXCEEDED |
| 409 | UPLOAD_PART_CONFLICT, UPLOAD_SESSION_STATE_CONFLICT (same part number/state with a different digest or invalid lifecycle transition) |
| 410 | UPLOAD_SESSION_EXPIRED |
| 413 | LESSON_FILE_TOO_LARGE, AUDIO_TOO_LARGE, MEDIA_TOO_LARGE (byte limit) |
| 415 | UNSUPPORTED_LESSON_TYPE, UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE |
| 422 | IMAGE_DIMENSIONS_EXCEEDED, MULTI_CHILD_NOT_SUPPORTED, OCR_NOT_SUPPORTED, TRANSCRIPT_NOT_CONFIRMED, LESSON_PLAN_NOT_APPROVED, SUBJECT_NOT_CONFIRMED, PROFILE_CONTEXT_NOT_CONFIRMED, UPLOAD_INCOMPLETE, UPLOAD_CHECKSUM_MISMATCH, UPLOAD_CONTENT_MISMATCH |
| 429 | RATE_LIMITED with Retry-After; applies a separate bucket for Teacher Sketch and does not deduct video quota |
| 502/503 | PROVIDER_BAD_RESPONSE, PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE, STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE, QUEUE_UNAVAILABLE, AI_CAPACITY_BUSY, AI_COST_LIMIT_REACHED, MODAL_TRANSFER_FAILED, MODAL_RESULT_INVALID, SKETCH_OUTPUT_INVALID |

Asynchronous AI errors also appear on the versioned job resource, including a safe code, stage, retryability, and finished/unfinished state. VIDEO_SAFETY_BLOCKED and AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED are final, safe outcomes for the job/run; the former outcome never grants playback. Do not return stack traces, local filesystem paths, Modal secrets, model prompts, or uploaded text.

### 9.9 Entities, aggregates, and code conventions

Use domain entities/aggregates for concepts with state transitions and business invariants; do not create one god entity for the whole application.

| Aggregate/entity | Owns | Key state transitions/invariants |
|---|---|---|
| ParentChildProfile | Parent profile head and immutable profile revisions | Only the owner may edit; a change creates a new revision; no Teacher/organization reference |
| ManagedChildProfile | Teacher profile identity and enrollment rights via the BE1 port | Belongs to an organization; at most one active enrollment; changing class closes the old enrollment |
| OrganizationCapacity | Class-slot entitlement and right to create classes | Capacity = 10 included slots + purchased slots; a transaction prevents exceeding the limit |
| LessonOccurrence | Source document revision, approved plan, occurrence activities, and current deck pointer | The plan must be approved; may have multiple activities; the deck covers the approved activities; a run selects one activity; at most one sample is playable/passes safety |
| SketchTeachingDeck | Presentation deck/step revisions per occurrence | Pins one plan revision/hash; a deck is READY only when it covers every activity and each instruction has adaptive 4–8 steps; if the plan changes it becomes STALE; carries no child profile; separate compute, does not use video quota |
| StoryRun | One adult, one child; lesson version optional; Parent selects and pins one main activity before the story; Teacher run pins an approved activity | One active run per adult; typed/versioned lesson ref if used; the main activity is the story context. After the video, no-pin returns the run-pinned activity; a lesson pin returns all activities of the exact version and marks the main activity. `last_meaningful_activity_at` drives the idle deadline; VIDEO_READY idle→COMPLETED, the adult-wait states idle→ABANDONED; both release the lock and keep the content; cancel moves to DELETE_PENDING; XOR profile kind; immutable revision; rewrite/video limits |
| VideoQuotaGrant | Monthly/purchased quota grant per owner scope | Grants are immutable; the current month's included quota expires at month end; purchased quota does not expire and is FIFO; admission excludes expired grants |
| VideoQuotaUsage | One immutable usage unit per grant in Billing | Idempotent; one unit per accepted full-video request; no refund; independent of scene retry; no mutable balance |
| AiJob | One classified target kind/ref (`STORY_RUN`, `VIDEO_GENERATION`, `VIDEO_GENERATION_SCENE`, `LESSON_SOURCE_DOCUMENT`, `LESSON_LIBRARY_VERSION`, `LESSON_VERSION_VISUAL_ASSET`, `LESSON_SKETCH_DECK_REVISION` or `PARENT_SKETCH_GUIDE`) and one AI stage | Idempotent dispatch, bounded attempts, expected hash/version, final completion or stale; Story profile resolves only via the canonical StoryRun; lesson-sketch/Parent guide has no child subject |
| OrganizationSubscription | Billing state, period, grace, auto-renew, and class capacity | Only a verified callback changes payment state; downgrade selects classes; 7-day grace |
| Notification | Send lifecycle of one notice/recipient/trigger/channel | Dedupe by trigger; bounded retry; contains no child content |

Value objects include OwnerScopeRef, ProfileRef, AssetRef, ActivitySnapshot, RevisionHash, MoneyVnd, BillingPeriod, QuotaGrantRef, QuotaDecision, and SafeError. Use explicit enums for state/reason codes; do not use free-text status. Domain entities validate state transitions themselves; do not expose arbitrary setters for state, owner, approval, or revision.

Code rules:

1. A route/worker parses and validates transport data, creates a versioned command, calls one application use case, and maps the typed result to HTTP/job output.
2. An application use case reads the aggregate via a repository port, authorizes the actor/resource, opens a transaction, calls domain behavior, writes the outbox/job command, and returns a typed result.
3. The domain owns invariants/state transitions and does not import frameworks, ORM, providers, or UI.
4. An infrastructure repository maps SQLAlchemy rows to domain objects. A provider adapter validates untrusted JSON/media before returning a typed contract. Do not let a provider response or ORM model leak into the domain/API contract.
5. A worker does not directly modify the story row after inference. It calls the completion command with the job ID, expected run revision, input/context/lesson hash, and contract version. The application accepts the result or records stale.
6. Every state-changing command has idempotency/dedupe. A payment callback uses the provider event identity. AI uses the job/semantic request receipt. The outbox event commits in the same transaction as the aggregate.
7. A schema change belongs to the owner's Alembic migration. Use a compatible expand/backfill, reconcile, then enable the feature flag/cutover. Do not rename/drop tables as a shortcut; changing a key/structure may need a separate compatible migration.
8. UI state is only a projection from the API/job contract. A screen does not infer permission, approval, billing capacity, or job completion from local state.
9. Original and derived assets have separate identities. Each transform creates a new asset version/lineage; do not overwrite the source drawing/lesson plan.

### 9.10 Implementation sequence under repository governance

Each later implementation task must comply with the governance workflow:

1. Create/update the feature folder; read the relevant context and source register.
2. Record the specific scope, dependencies, acceptance criteria, owner module, and rollback plan.
3. Resolve product questions; record architecture decisions in an ADR before finalizing the stack/schema.
4. Define domain entities and versioned contracts first; add fixtures/contract review before UI/backend integration.
5. Implement domain behavior, the application use case/port, the infrastructure adapter, thin routes/workers, and then the client screen, following the direction of dependency.
6. Use non-destructive, staged Alembic changes; store migration evidence, row/hash reconciliation, and rollback behavior.
7. Store evidence in the owning feature; update context/decision/status after each significant implementation step.
8. Explicit approval is required for each implementation plan before changing product code/data. Approval of this document does not permit runtime changes.
9. Before commit/push, run the repository security validator. Do not commit secrets, credentials, or real child data.

Sprint 1 remains four independent fixture/contract workstreams per ADR-0006. The dependency-based roadmap and the parallel work assignment are two separate views; integration requires a separately approved allocation. By default, do not assign backend/infra/E2E to Person 4 or the entire Android app to Person 3.

<a id="srs-10"></a>
## 10. Security, monitoring, and rate limits

### 10.1 Security and privacy controls

- Firebase Authentication verifies adult identity; the backend performs its own authorization by profile owner, organization membership, class assignment, run actor, and subscription state. Firebase does not store product data.
- Parent and Teacher accounts are separate; profiles may be linked through a consented, scoped, and revocable grant. Account/profile owners must not be merged; every cross-role access is verified by the backend.
- Names/nicknames and support-needs information are excluded from the AI prompt context. Each run's snapshot is explicit, minimal, immutable, and approved by an adult.
- Treat lesson file content, typed/spoken idea input, and model responses as untrusted data. Separate extracted lesson text from the system policy; content must not replace system instructions, invoke tools, or exceed the approved plan/safety policy.
- Validate story/script/scene/narration against the active child-safety policy before display or video generation. Unsafe output is blocked, a safe recovery path is returned, and only audit metadata is recorded.
- Use private object storage, at-rest encryption if the service supports it, short-lived playback scope, server-side content-type/size checks, and hash/provenance for original and derived assets.
- Browsers/mobile clients do not receive Modal, MoMo, VNPAY, PostgreSQL, Redis, or MinIO credentials. Provider adapters run only in the backend worker.
- Verify the payment callback signature; a unique provider-event ref and an in-transaction receipt make repeated webhooks idempotent.
- Enable MFA for organization admins and billing admins. The Android client sends the Play Integrity token through a backend adapter; this is not identity proof replacing Firebase Auth, and Firebase App Check is not used.
- The database implements PostgreSQL RLS by owner/org scope as defense-in-depth; runtime DB roles are not table owners, do not have `BYPASSRLS`, and are granted only minimal privileges. Use cases still check explicit owner/assignment; RLS does not replace application authorization.
- File uploads go into quarantine. ClamAV or a compatible scanner must return `CLEAN` before any parser, ASR, VLM, or model; if the scanner times out, is unavailable, or errors, the file stays in quarantine and the system fails closed. Scanning does not write file content to logs.
- A drawing may contain identifying text such as a name/school/address: warn/preview to the adult before provider transfer, perform bounded text checks, require crop/mask if PII is detected, and send only the derived masked asset. The original is always private and lineage is recorded; this is a risk-reduction layer and does not guarantee recognition of all handwriting.
- Do not log request bodies, Authorization headers, audio, drawings, transcripts, story text, prompts, signed URLs, payment credentials, or child names.
- Demo fixtures use synthetic data only. Exclude real child data from the demo until its purpose, rights, consent, provider transfer, retention/deletion, access, and privacy are separately approved.
- Do not commit .env, provider tokens, service-account keys, signing keys, seed account credentials, or real child data.

### 10.2 Monitoring

Use structured JSON logs with a correlation ID and opaque resource IDs. Metrics are aggregated, with no child/profile identifiers or free text.

| Signal | Example metrics |
|---|---|
| API | Request count/latency by route template/status, auth errors, 4xx/5xx |
| Queue | Queue depth, age of the oldest job, enqueue/start/finish, worker heartbeat |
| AI job | Latency/errors by stage/type (story-video and Teacher Sketch reported separately), attempt count, stale results, provider timeouts, Modal GPU seconds/concurrency |
| Teacher Sketch | Deck/step generation, coverage/schema/safety rejections, stale/regeneration, latency, and compute usage by aggregated scope; no child/profile labels, no video quota usage |
| Video/quota | Scene attempts, decode/format/safety check results, generation/regeneration, grant/admission/consumption/exhaustion by generic owner scope type, top-up/callback reconciliation; no child/profile labels |
| Storage | Bytes/objects by generic asset type, upload/download errors, deletion lifecycle backlog |
| Billing | Callback signature errors, duplicate callbacks, invoice reconciliation latency, renewal result, notification delivery |
| Security | Rate-limit events, authorization denials, abnormal authentication failures |
| Runtime | Container health, DB connection pool saturation, Redis/MinIO/OTel collector status |

Grafana has dashboards for API/job/service health, Modal usage by stage, video retries, storage lifecycle, payment reconciliation, and notifications. Alert recipients are a production decision; the local demo only displays dashboards/logs.

### 10.3 Data-safe telemetry

Permitted telemetry fields: route template, status, duration, stage, retry number, non-secret provider/model profile ID, GPU class, aggregated metrics, correlation ID, and opaque run/job ID with controlled retention. Recording child names, detailed profile context, image/audio bytes, transcripts/stories/prompts, credentials, signed playback URLs, and health/support-needs information is prohibited. Sampling must not skip the redaction step.

### 10.4 Initial limits for the local demo

| Scope | Initial limit | Response |
|---|---:|---|
| Authenticated API | 60 requests/minute/account | 429 + Retry-After |
| Unauthenticated endpoints | 20 requests/minute/IP | 429 + Retry-After |
| Create story run | 2 requests/minute/account | 429 + Retry-After; the one-active-run limit is checked separately |
| Full-video admission attempt | 2 requests/minute/account | 429 + Retry-After; the limit also counts rejected admissions to prevent intent spam; a retry with the same idempotency key returns the same result and does not create a new intent |
| Upload session control (create/complete/abort) | 10 requests/minute/account | 429 + Retry-After; separate rate bucket from chunk/status |
| Upload chunk PUT | 60 requests/minute/account; at most 40 MiB of new bytes/minute/account; at most 3 active sessions/account | 429 + Retry-After; chunk at most 4 MiB; an exact-digest retry still counts as a request but does not count again against the unique-byte budget |
| Upload status/list-parts | 30 requests/minute/session | 429 + Retry-After; returns only state/receipts within the owner scope |
| Teacher AI Sketch | Independent rate limit and compute cost cap per organization/current responsible Teacher/occurrence; specific values after synthetic benchmark | 429 `RATE_LIMITED` or 503 `AI_COST_LIMIT_REACHED`; does not write Billing video quota |
| Active story run | 1 per adult account | 409 ACTIVE_RUN_EXISTS |
| Monthly video quota | Total grants of the Parent account or Teacher organization | 409 VIDEO_QUOTA_EXCEEDED; playback/past history remains accessible |
| Audio | 2 minutes and 20 MB | 413 when size/duration is exceeded |
| Drawing image | At most 20 MiB encoded, 40 megapixels decoded; JPEG/PNG/WebP only | 413 when bytes are exceeded; 422 IMAGE_DIMENSIONS_EXCEEDED or UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE |
| Lesson file | 25 MB and 100 pages/slides | 413/422 with a safe error code |
| Scene AI | 1 initial attempt + 2 automatic retries | When retries are exhausted, the job fails safely in a terminal state |
| Maximum scene inferences | 15 scenes × 3 attempts = 45 attempts/request; at most 3 accepted requests/run = 135 attempts/run structurally | Hard GPU-second/request and daily caps stop before the theoretical limit if the benchmark/cost ceiling is lower |
| LTX video on Modal | 1 run in progress; at most 5 videos waiting | 503 AI_CAPACITY_BUSY + Retry-After when the queue is full |
| Modal scene stage timeout | 900 seconds per attempt; hard generation deadline `accepted_at + 6 hours`, including queue, retry wait, model stages, composition, validation, and reconciliation | Stop at the deadline, return a safe timeout; quota already accepted is still consumed; the deadline is not a completion SLO |
| GPU cost cap | Hard cap on GPU-seconds per accepted request and per day, plus a circuit breaker | Configuration is mandatory before live video is opened; values are set after a synthetic benchmark, not warnings only |
| Fair queue | Round-robin by owner scope; FIFO within a Parent account/Teacher organization; bounded waiting per scope | One scope does not occupy the entire queue; when the queue is full, new requests are rejected with AI_CAPACITY_BUSY |
| Modal kill switch | AI_VIDEO_ENABLED=false stops accepting new videos; sends cancellation requests for work in progress | New requests are rejected safely; stale/late output is not published |

The limits are configured server-side and are a starting point for the synthetic POC; upload buckets are separate from the generic API bucket so that resume is not blocked by the 10 requests/minute limit. The upload/image/timeout/queue thresholds are not measured capacity or a production SLO. The level of 15 scenes × 3 attempts × 900 seconds corresponds to at most 11.25 aggregate GPU-hours if run sequentially; independent scenes run in parallel within bounded concurrency. Before quota admission, the GPU-second reservation and a feasible deadline/capacity envelope must be confirmed; if not, reject before Billing usage. The benchmark must confirm 6 hours wall-clock from accepted_at, including queue and all stages; 6 hours is a hard timeout, not a p95 claim. Hard GPU caps are a mandatory release configuration; specific values are fixed only after a synthetic benchmark. All limits must remain finite and non-zero. Do not key rate limits by child ID/content.

Payment callbacks have their own per-provider anti-abuse/replay policy, signature verification, and idempotent receipts; valid callback retries are not rejected merely because the generic unauthenticated-browser bucket is full. Health probes and metrics scrapes must be protected internally, separate from the public throttle.

<a id="srs-11"></a>
## 11. Data architecture and invariants

The canonical list of owner, key, main fields, relationships, constraints, retention, and source/target disposition is in [DATA_TABLE_REGISTER.md](DATA_TABLE_REGISTER.md). The target model divides responsibilities as follows:

| Owner/schema | Data responsibility |
|---|---|
| core | Shared audit, aggregate revision, outbox, asset metadata/lineage, upload sessions, and part receipts |
| be1 | Organizations, adult identity projection, organization/class assignments, classes, Teacher profiles, and enrollment |
| parent | Parent child profiles, revisions, context, support needs, and data authorization |
| ai_jobs | Durable provider jobs, stage attempts/events, and command/completion receipts; Story/Lesson aggregates own output refs |
| ai_policy | Policy/model governance, activation, and platform compute/resource reservation ledger; the source provider-copy registry is kept read-only outside the target graph |
| learning_content | Global, approved, versioned Parent activity catalog |
| lesson | Source files, extracted-text revisions, occurrences, approved plans, and activities |
| story | Single-child run; `run_inputs` holds the drawing/idea revision, the pinned profile revision, and the approved context snapshot; transcript/understanding, story/review revisions, scenes, and video generation |
| billing | Versioned rates, organization subscriptions, capacity, invoices/payments, provider receipts/events, video-quota grants, and an immutable usage ledger |
| notifications | In-app items, delivery attempts, and push endpoints |
| Legacy schema | Historical collaboration/canvas/video/activity/preset tables plus 13 deferred AI Policy tables kept read-only; not in the target ERD |

### 11.1 Mandatory database constraints

1. A story run carries exactly one profile ref, Parent or Teacher (exclusive-or check); role/scope must match.
2. A story run has one activity and one scalar lesson-occurrence ref if it is a Teacher run.
3. Partial unique constraint for at most one open run per current owner: the Parent account or the currently assigned Teacher, via `active_owner_parent_account_id`/`active_owner_teacher_id`. These fields are separate from the immutable `created_by_actor_ref`. `DRAFT`, `VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING` and every open run hold the lock; `COMPLETED`, `FAILED`, `ABANDONED`, `DELETE_PENDING`, `DELETED` do not. Reassignment updates the owner/lock via a versioned, idempotent Story command; BE1 and Story use separate transactions/receipts within the saga, with no cross-module SQL/transaction. Cancellation and idle-closeout release the lock in the Story transaction; the timer does not close a run while an AI job/admission reconciliation is still active.
4. Partial unique constraint for at most one active class enrollment per managed profile.
5. Partial unique constraint for at most one active responsible Teacher per class.
6. Unique partial constraint limiting each occurrence to one playable/safety-passed sample video. A run that fails/is safety-blocked before the first claim does not count. After the claim, the slot is not reopened by video rejection, regeneration failure/exhaustion, delete/cancel or Teacher reassignment.
7. A run receives one initial full-video request and at most two quality regeneration requests. Each accepted request consumes one request cap and one quota unit. ai_jobs.attempt limits technical retries to two after the first call of each scene and does not deduct additional quota.
8. Story revisions allow the initial version and at most three rewrites per run. Revisions are immutable; a stale job cannot approve a newer revision.
9. Each AI job has exactly one target kind/ref/revision. `STORY_RUN` resolves exactly one child via Story; `VIDEO_GENERATION` resolves exactly one generation/run; `VIDEO_GENERATION_SCENE` resolves exactly one scene/generation/run; `LESSON_SOURCE_DOCUMENT` resolves the document via Lesson; `LESSON_LIBRARY_VERSION` resolves the lesson draft; `LESSON_VERSION_VISUAL_ASSET` resolves the visual asset; `LESSON_SKETCH_DECK_REVISION` resolves the Teacher deck; `PARENT_SKETCH_GUIDE` accepts only a generic topic/difficulty and returns a temporary guide asset. Both sketch targets have no child/profile. No subject-ref or job-scene helper table is created.
10. The number of active classes does not exceed the organization's effective capacity. Capacity changes/class creation run in a transaction with a lock or serializable enforcement.
11. The provider event key is unique per provider; webhook receipt and invoice/subscription updates are idempotent within a transaction.
12. A profile revision/context snapshot is a versioned opaque ref between modules. Cross-module validation uses a contract/port; no cross-module FK is created.
13. Only the profile owner/responsible Teacher may read profile content; an admin managing the roster does not implicitly view media.
14. Billing is the source of entitlement and the immutable usage ledger for video quota. Parent scope is the Parent account; Teacher scope is the organization. Before admission/dispatch, the application reserves compute budget via the AI Policy port, then Billing checks scope/grant and records usage idempotently; if there is no budget/quota, the request is not accepted and no usage is recorded. Once accepted, the unit is not refunded even if provider/safety/emergency abort fails or the run is deleted. The AI Policy resource ledger only measures/reserves platform resources; Story holds an opaque usage ref. No second mutable balance is created and there is no cross-module SQL/join.
15. Each accepted full-video request consumes one quota unit and one of the three request slots per run, including technical/provider/safety failures. Billing records usage before dispatch; scene retries do not create new rows; no refund. The period is the calendar month in `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`; the current month's included grant is used first, then top-ups FIFO. Unused included grant expires at the end of the month; purchased additional quota carries forward (Q117–Q118, Q124–Q126, Q128, Q132).
16. Deleting a run does not refund the quota unit already consumed (Q119); at minimum the usage ledger is retained for audit/metering, and linked content is deleted according to the lifecycle.
17. A top-up is a one-time purchase and does not auto-renew (Q120). Only a verified/idempotent payment event issues the grant.
18. An account/organization starting mid-month receives the full included allowance for the current calendar month, with no proration (Q121).
19. Each showing of a sample video to a class requires per-occurrence confirmation by the responsible Teacher before a showing playback token is issued. This is recorded separately from script/video review; no reusable playback capability is created for class members.
20. `VIDEO_READY` is finalized/regenerated by an adult or automatically moves to `COMPLETED` after 168 hours of adult-wait idle. Other adult-wait states move to `ABANDONED` at the same deadline; a reminder is sent at hour 144. Both states close the run and release the lock but keep content until deletion. Canceling an incomplete run moves it to `DELETE_PENDING`, releases the lock immediately, cancels jobs and deletes per the lifecycle. The consumed quota and the successful sample claim are retained (Q122–Q123, Q129, Q133).
21. A generation-scene cannot be attached to a scene from a different revision: `story.video_generations` has unique `(video_generation_id, story_revision_id)`; `story.story_scenes` has unique `(story_revision_id, scene_id)`; `story.video_generation_scenes` carries an immutable `story_revision_id` and has composite FKs to both the generation and the scene.
22. The current pointer belongs to the correct run: the Story command locks/version-checks the run, validates input/story/video pointer ownership and the parent revision, then updates the pointer + aggregate revision + run state in one Story transaction; a stale expected revision is rejected.
23. Run/video state does not drift on its own: `video_generations.state` is the canonical generation lifecycle; `runs.state` is the business projection. Story's idempotent completion updates both simultaneously; `VIDEO_READY` requires the current generation to be READY, assets complete, and technical/consistency/safety checks all passing.
24. An AI Job connects to exactly one target: a job has exactly one target kind/ref/revision — `STORY_RUN`, `VIDEO_GENERATION`, `VIDEO_GENERATION_SCENE`, `LESSON_SOURCE_DOCUMENT`, `LESSON_LIBRARY_VERSION`, `LESSON_VERSION_VISUAL_ASSET`, `LESSON_SKETCH_DECK_REVISION` or `PARENT_SKETCH_GUIDE`. A scene holds a reciprocal source job ref verified by the Story owner; attempt is the single retry/transfer ledger.
25. Cross-module references have a contract: a ref carries owner/resource kind, opaque ID, scope, contract version and a revision/hash pin where needed; the target owner validates at admission/completion/reconciliation. No cross-schema FK or shared ref registry table is used.

A PostgreSQL partial unique index is proposed for state-dependent uniqueness; it must be verified against the actual schema/state during the implementation phase. [PostgreSQL 17 index documentation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/indexes.html).

### 11.2 Owner scope

OwnerScopeRefV1 supports ORGANIZATION, PARENT_ACCOUNT and GLOBAL. GLOBAL is only for platform catalog/policy; it is not the owner of a child profile, run, video quota or media asset. The backend derives the adult actor for authentication and does not trust the value sent by the client. Parent profiles and Parent quota use the Parent account scope; Teacher profiles belong to the organization; Teacher video quota resolves by the occurrence's organization, with no separate Teacher-account quota. A Parent is not represented by a fake organization. Parent/Teacher profile IDs are opaque and unrelated to each other. Each model payload is built from exactly one accessible profile and an approved snapshot.

### 11.3 Retention and deletion

- Original drawings/audio/lesson plans and each derived artifact have an immutable asset identity and source lineage where needed.
- Canceling an incomplete run uses the same purge lifecycle as content deletion: revoke access and release the active lock as soon as the command is recorded, cancel jobs on a best-effort basis, delete all eligible objects/metadata; retain minimal quota usage and the consumed Teacher sample-slot claim.
- Deleting a run creates a lifecycle request for each piece of private content and each artifact generated by the run. If an asset is still referenced by another valid object, it is deleted only when no valid stored reference remains under the owner lifecycle.
- For a Teacher sample video, after the run is deleted only sample_slot_state and the occurrence's claim time are retained; run/profile/media pointers are deleted. A consumed slot is still used per the owner's confirmation. This metadata does not identify the child and grants no playback.
- Audit retains actor/action/resource type/time/result and the deletion receipt; it does not retain media, transcripts, prompts or profile details.
- Deleting a profile follows the owner's lifecycle, and dependent context/history is deleted/hidden per policy. The legally required retention period is not finalized in the demo SRS; a real data policy is needed before enabling real child data.
- No new retention is implicitly applied to read-only legacy data. Archive migration/purge requires a separately approved mapping, backup and restore plan.
- Q119 is decided: deleting a run/content does not refund consumed quota; minimal quota-usage ledger metadata is retained.

| Data type | Retention in the synthetic demo | Deletion/backup | Applicability status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Parent/Teacher profiles and revisions | Until the authorized subject requests deletion per the lifecycle; no auto-expiry | Revoke access immediately, purge dependent content; retain minimal classified audit/roster/legal metadata | Synthetic demo; maximum period for real data not finalized |
| Runs, drawings/audio, transcripts, story revisions, scenes, videos of COMPLETED, FAILED or ABANDONED runs | Until the user actively deletes; Q66/Q129/Q133 set no date-based auto-purge | Deleted via lifecycle; backup per the approved demo backup policy, currently no real-data backup policy | Synthetic demo only; production gate requires a maximum retention |
| Original lesson plans and extraction revisions | Until the Teacher/admin deletes the lesson per the lifecycle | Purge original + derived text/assets, retain minimal audit | Synthetic lessons only; production schedule TBD |
| Modal scratch/input-output working copy | Deleted after completion/failure/cancel; orphan scratch is cleaned up/alerted after 24 hours | Job-scoped prefix, checksum and deletion receipt | Contains no real child data in the demo |
| Billing invoice/payment events and quota grants/usage | Per financial/metering retention to be set with legal/accounting; quota usage contains no child content | Run content is deleted independently; append-only billing/audit metadata is not deleted with the run | Production period TBD |
| Logs/traces/metrics | Finite retention is configured; only redacted data/opaque IDs | Deleted per operational retention; contains no payload/media/PII | Specific period must be filled in the deploy profile before the pilot |
| Backups | Manual local backups contain only synthetic data | Production must have expiry, access controls, and a reviewed deletion propagation or cryptographic-erasure design | Production gate; no backup SLA yet |

"Until the user deletes" cannot be interpreted as a retention policy suitable for real children. Any production/pilot with real data is blocked until OI-05–OI-08 are approved, with a maximum period per record type, a deletion approach within backups and an accountable owner.

<a id="srs-12"></a>
## 12. Source inventory, migration and conversion

### 12.1 Source table inventory

Before Rev31, the repository declared 86 production source mappings, including core (8), BE1 (22), durable AI Jobs (7), AI Policy (25), Learning Content (6), Knowledge Video (2), Video Review (6), Video Failure (3), Activity Execution (5) and Preset Automation (2). Rev31 adds three Lesson support mappings, so the current source crosswalk has 89 mappings; this is not an inventory of the running database. Five fixture mappings used for tests are not included in the total.

### 12.2 Target disposition

- Keep/normalize the 38 existing mappings as owners for organization, roster, AI Jobs, AI policy/version/activation/resource budget, active catalog, Core provenance/audit and transaction infrastructure.
- Add 54 current target tables: 40 initial platform tables, 11 logical platform tables and three Rev31 Lesson support tables; remove four helper/snapshot tables that duplicate the aggregate owner.
- Keep 48 legacy mappings read-only in place: 35 legacy classroom/canvas/video/activity/preset mappings plus 12 `ai_policy.copy_*` registries and `ai_policy.resource_receipt`. The full provider-copy registry is deferred to the production gate using real child data. New resource commands use `resource_event` as the event + idempotency/result ledger, without writing to two tables.
- Current Rev31 crosswalk: 38 retained/refactored + 54 new = 92 active; +48 legacy/read-only = 140 projected physical. The current source crosswalk has 89 mappings: 86 pre-Rev31 + 3 Lesson support, comprising 38 retained/refactored + 48 legacy + 3 Lesson support. The historical v2.3 design baseline is 89 active + 48 legacy = 137. The five fixtures are not counted; these mappings/targets are not an inventory of the running DB.
- This design does not copy tables to an archive schema and does not drop/truncate tables. Existing rows/IDs are retained; some keys/nullability/checks of active tables change only after expand/backfill/reconcile and a separately approved migration plan.
- Do not interpret old collaboration session IDs as single-child story runs. Retain the session IDs and their historical meaning unchanged.

#### Existing BE1 constraints that the target model must handle

The declarations in backend/src/sketch2life/infrastructure/persistence/be1_models.py are stricter than the proposed target; do not assume the new flow is already compatible:

- be1_managed_profiles.class_id is currently NOT NULL and references class. In the target, be1_enrollments is the canonical source of current/historical membership; managed_profiles.class_id is only a normalized projection of the current class. Create/move the profile and enrollment atomically, and reconcile the projection. A profile missing an enrollment, or with an unclear enrollment, must be quarantined for review and must not be enrolled automatically.
- V2.3 accepts ages 36–120 months for Parent and Teacher profiles; the current source schema/validation declares the old range and must be changed in one approved migration. The specific age-band boundaries remain open.
- consent_id, consent_purpose_ref are currently NOT NULL. The target adds data_origin (SYNTHETIC_TEST, REAL, LEGACY_UNCLASSIFIED) and requires consent only by origin: synthetic fixtures need no fake consent row/ID; real-data rows need verified valid authorization; rows lacking evidence remain LEGACY_UNCLASSIFIED, and backfill must not automatically upgrade them to real-data eligibility.
- be1_consent_records.state currently accepts only pending and revoked, which is not enough for the authorization lifecycle. The demo has no real child data. Real-data processing must define the states valid/granted/expired/withdrawn and verify evidence before provider transfer is enabled.
- The current unique constraint of be1_enrollments only guarantees the organization/class/child triple; it does not guarantee that a child is in only one active class. Add a partial unique constraint for one active enrollment and idempotent reconciliation before enabling writes.
- be1_adult_identities currently has a subject key and issuer, but no exclusive Parent/Teacher account scope. Add a server-managed scope and uniqueness; keep the old identity as Teacher/legacy until separately verified, and do not infer Parent eligibility merely because an organization assignment is missing.

### 12.3 Proposed migration sequence

1. **Capture baseline and backup:** count tables/columns/indexes/rows in each deployment environment; verify PostgreSQL/MinIO backup/restore using synthetic data.
2. **Expand schema:** create the target schemas/tables/indexes and add versioned scope/revision/origin fields via Alembic. Keep existing columns, keys and rows unchanged in the initial expand step.
3. **Compatibility mapping:** reuse organization/class/profile records through the owning module's repository. Add idempotent backfill for enrollment/identity scope/data origin for verification; use LEGACY_UNCLASSIFIED if evidence is missing; record count/hash. Do not create fake consent or synthetic organizations.
4. **Reconciliation:** compare source/target row counts, opaque IDs, owner profile/class, enrollment state, asset hash and audit reference. Stop cutover if there is a mismatch.
5. **Disable the old flow:** use a feature flag to turn off legacy classroom/canvas writes; keep table permissions/owner and read-only history for authorized migration/operators. Do not display old data in new single-child runs without an approved mapping.
6. **Target flow cutover:** enable target-side Parent/Teacher after the fixture/contract/integration meet the criteria. Verify current role assignment and that there are no empty child/run refs.
7. **Constraint switch:** after parity and owner approval, add/validate partial uniqueness and switch the receipt key/nullable owner scope through a compatible dual-read/write step. Keep original column/row values and the compatibility path until the new reader is proven.
8. **Monitoring and rollback:** monitor errors, reconciliation, queues and data parity. Roll back via feature flag and compatible reader; do not delete migrated data or legacy columns for rollback.
9. **Later contract cleanup:** deleting old tables, moving to archive or changing retention requires a separately approved plan, a restore test and data/legal owner review.
10. **Move provider-copy tables to legacy:** before cutover, prove that the synthetic Modal path has an attempt-scoped workspace ref, manifest hash, cleanup receipt and orphan sweep; after historical reconciliation, lock write permission on `resource_receipt` and the 12 `copy_*` tables. Do not drop/copy; keep rows/IDs unchanged and read-only.
11. **Merge the resource command ledger:** before new writes, ensure `resource_event` has a unique idempotency key, fingerprint, operation/result snapshot and delivery state; reconcile old receipts. If one command can produce multiple events, use event-kind in the key or keep the receipt table until safe mapping is proven; do not enable an ambiguous migration.
12. **Validate Story composite constraints:** backfill `story_revision_id` for generation-scene links from the generation parent; quarantine scenes not belonging to that revision; reconcile mismatches/orphans and only then validate the composite FK. Do not assign scenes automatically.
13. **AI Job target migration:** backfill one target kind/ref/revision from the verified aggregate mapping; map scene jobs to `generation_scene_id` and check the reciprocal `source_job_ref`. Do not infer a single-child job target from a collaboration session ID.
14. **Read-model parity:** compare the StoryRun detail response against each owner source and the permission matrix; do not create a second projection table or return sensitive prompt/transcript/profile data.

This section only describes the proposed safe sequence. Do not perform migrations or access the database when drafting the document.

<a id="srs-13"></a>
## 13. Foundation architecture acceptance criteria

| ID | Given | When | Then | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AC-01 | Parent and Teacher have independent accounts/scopes | The actor tries to access a profile in the other scope | The backend rejects without revealing that the account/profile exists; there is no cross-link | BR-03; FR-C-01/04 |
| AC-02 | An adult already has an active run tied to exactly one profile | Submits a second run or multiple profiles | The database/API rejects; VIDEO_READY still holds the lock until a valid complete/cancel/idle closeout | BR-01/02; FR-C-02/03 |
| AC-03 | A Teacher already has a class, roster and occurrence | Looks for canvas/QR/lobby/batch action | There is no runtime collaboration, shared canvas, QR, lobby or multiple children/runs | BR-01/05; FR-T-01–10 |
| AC-04 | An occurrence has no sample claim yet | A video passes safety and becomes playable, is later rejected, and regenerations fail | The claim is retained permanently; there is no recovery run for the occurrence | BR-10/17; Q127 |
| AC-05 | A Parent uses a lesson with an activity list, or a Teacher prepares an occurrence | The Parent video completes or the Teacher creates a sample | Parent activities are shown after the video; the Teacher sample still pins one approved activity; a custom activity is only within the occurrence and requires a checklist | BR-08/09; FR-PL-16 |
| AC-06 | A story/video run is active | Rewrite or full-video request is called | Story allows at most 1 initial + 3 rewrites; video allows at most 1 initial + 2 regenerations; each video request requires script confirmation | BR-11/13–15 |
| AC-07 | A video has passed automatic safety | The system plays the video | There is no final video approval, PixiJS motion, download/export or public URL | BR-16/25/36 |
| AC-08 | The Parent/Teacher has confirmed the transcript, subject and role-appropriate context/lesson direction; the Teacher has an approved activity | The adult continues the pipeline | The Parent selects and pins one main activity before the story; that activity is the story context and takes place after the video. The Teacher pins an approved activity before the video; the final script revision requires adult confirmation before admission | BR-14/15/55; FR-P-05/FR-PL-16; Rev66 |
| AC-09 | The current post-Rev31 source crosswalk has 89 production mappings; there are 5 test fixtures | Compare the register with the target | Distinguish the current source 89 = 38 retained/refactored +48 legacy +3 Lesson support from the historical v2.3 design baseline of 89 active/137 physical. The current target is 38 retained/refactored +54 new =92 active; +48 legacy =140 projected physical; fixtures are not counted and no live DB count is claimed | §1.1; §12; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER §§1/11/14.4 |
| AC-10 | The module owns a profile, lesson, story, billing, notification or asset | Check the write path | Each fact has one owner; projection/reference/event is clearly specified; the register has a dictionary and requirement-based retention | §9.7; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER |
| AC-11 | Domain/use case/adapter are organized | Check import/dependency direction | Domain does not depend on framework/provider/storage/queue/UI; modules communicate through versioned ports/contracts | NFR-01/14 |
| AC-12 | The Docker local synthetic stack is running | Start/observe/backup/restore | Readiness, rate-limit, error envelope, redacted monitoring, and manual backup/restore with instructions | NFR-07/08/11/12/27; §25–27 |
| AC-13 | The demo creates a profile/media/run | Seeds or uploads a fixture | Only synthetic data is used; real-child data is blocked until the privacy/retention/provider gates have evidence | BR-27; NFR-03/18/31 |
| AC-14 | An organization buys/upgrades/downgrades capacity or renews | A MoMo/VNPAY callback is received | Payment/capacity/grace/downgrade/proration/notice are consistent; a redirect is not treated as payment | BR-28–33 |
| AC-15 | Two Markdown documents are awaiting owner review | There is a request for a diagram/PDF | No final artifact is created before the owner approves both the SRS and the data register | Owner sequencing requirement |
| AC-16 | Use cases UC-01…UC-17 are reviewed | The specification is checked | Each UC has an actor, prerequisite, trigger, main/alternate flow, postcondition and data/requirement refs | §19 |
| AC-17 | A Parent-account or school-organization quota scope has a grant/usage; a Teacher uses the occurrence's organization quota and the School Manager may allocate internal limits | View quota, buy a package/top-up as the proper actor, allocate/revoke unused Teacher quota, the Teacher leaves the assignment, generates or runs out of quota | Billing ledger is idempotent; scopes are separate; an allocation does not exceed the school balance; unused portions are revoked manually or automatically returned to the school when the Teacher leaves the organization; consumed usage still belongs to the school; included is consumed before top-up FIFO; included expires at month end, top-up does not expire until used up; a purchase is granted only after verified settlement, no refund after a successful purchase; old playback remains open | BR-38; FR-B-10–13; §31.20; Rev71 |
| AC-18 | The Teacher's video is ready | The Teacher wants to show it to the class | The responsible Teacher confirms the correct occurrence; a short-lived playback token; no reusable account/capability is granted to students | BR-37; FR-T-16 |
| AC-19 | An upload, callback, async job or playback is invoked | A duplicate/stale command is sent or the provider outcome is unknown | The contract has auth/idempotency/version; a stale result does not overwrite; an unknown state goes to reconciliation; errors use the error envelope | FR-O-02/05/10; §21–22 |
| AC-20 | A user runs the main Parent/Teacher flow | Uses only keyboard/screen reader or refuses camera/mic | The flow has semantic labels, focus, text error/caption; upload/text fallback is usable; Vietnamese UI | NFR-13/20/21/29 |
| AC-21 | A lesson, adult input, model output or media is processed | The safety policy runs | Content is untrusted; versioned validation blocks unsafe/private/medical claims before script display or playback | BR-36; FR-AI-12; NFR-23 |
| AC-22 | A DRAFT or a run awaiting adult action, with no AI job/admission processing | Reaches 144h/168h idle | The inbox creates one reminder; push is best-effort; email is attempted only if the current owner has a verified email; +168h VIDEO_READY→COMPLETED, other waiting states including DRAFT→ABANDONED; releases the owner lock but keeps content; AI deadline is a separate 6h from accepted_at | BR-39/40; FR-C-11; AT-11 |
| AC-23 | A video request has passed admission | A provider/safety/technical failure or run deletion occurs afterwards | Every accepted request that has consumed quota is not refunded; a request rejected before admission does not consume | BR-11/38; Q128 |
| AC-24 | Story requires quota admission | Application coordinates modules | Billing is the sole owner of grants/usage; AI Policy handles GPU/compute only; Story keeps an opaque ref; no joins/cross-writes to private tables | Q132; §9.7 |
| AC-25 | Admin requests reassignment; StoryRun belongs to the class and has an active owner | Transfer saga starts/completes or encounters a conflict | Old access/token is fenced immediately; pending state does not grant content; Story lock is transferred before BE1 finalizes; valid AI job continues; normal conflict returns 409; emergency force-close has audit metadata, does not view/delete media, quota/sample claim is preserved | BR-06/41; FR-T-14/17; AT-12 |
| AC-26 | Actor enables push or endpoint is invalid | Worker sends notice | Inbox is always authoritative; Web Push is best effort; Android adapter is enabled only after POC; payload contains no child data; Firebase Messaging is not used | FR-B-14; NFR-26; Q131 |
| AC-27 | Unknown/out-of-scope ID or actor lacks action | API authorization | Unknown and out-of-scope IDs both return 404; 403 only for a resource that is known and in the same scope but the action is not permitted; account-scope conflict does not expose UID | §9.8; FR-O-05 |
| AC-28 | System receives many upload/job/provider requests | Load exceeds rate/cost/queue or threat boundary | Hard caps, fair queue, quarantine AV, RLS/least-privilege/MFA/Play Integrity are release gates; caps must not be exceeded | NFR-12/24/25; §10/26 |
| AC-29 | Adult uploads photo/audio/lesson over an unstable network | Network lost, then session resumes | `core.upload_sessions`/`core.upload_parts` hold state/receipts; app sends 4 MiB chunks via separate object PutObject calls; backend verifies contiguous parts + per-part/whole SHA-256 + bytes, bounded assemble and final PutObject; S3 Multipart Upload API is not called; exact retry does not recount byte budget; limits allow valid resume | FR-C-12; NFR-32; AT-13 |
| AC-30 | Run is `DRAFT`, in a review state or `VIDEO_ADMISSION_PENDING` with no ambiguous result remaining | Idle scheduler/admission callback runs | DRAFT and review states start the adult-wait timer; at +144h there is an inbox notice, best-effort push, and email if verified; at +168h closeout and lock release. Admission pending is not idle-closed; rejected returns to SCRIPT_REVIEW and starts a new timer | BR-39/40; FR-C-11; FR-O-10; AT-11 |
| AC-31 | Teacher assignment changes while run/job is active or new Teacher already has a run | Run normal transfer or emergency force-close | Transfer has durable BE1/Story receipts; old authorization is revoked, no-content during pending, new authorization only after finalize; valid output is not cancelled; ordinary conflict returns 409; force-close is metadata only and grants no media to admin | BR-06/41; FR-T-14/17; NFR-05; AT-12 |
| AC-32 | File upload is within demo limit and network is interrupted | App resumes, server finalizes | 4 MiB application chunks are separate object PUTs; session/parts tables store receipts; verify contiguous part numbers, size/SHA-256, scan/format; final asset is written with a low-level single PutObject; idempotent duplicate does not double-count bytes. This AC verifies upload and does not decide the target table count; see §1.1/§12 for the current data counting | FR-C-12; NFR-09/12/32; AT-13 |
| AC-33 | Parent/Teacher is in a drawing session; Parent may choose topic/lesson and enable guide | Adult chooses guide or skips, then child draws and Parent takes a photo | Parent/Teacher guide uses adaptive 4–8 steps progressing from basic strokes to a recognizable figure; guide is optional and error/skip does not block capture. Parent does not choose the photo subject before capture; VLM proposes after capture for the adult to confirm/correct | BR-42/43/46/54; FR-P-02/04/11; FR-T-18; UC-02/17; AC-44/45/53 |
| AC-34 | Teacher approves a plan revision with multiple activities | Requests creation of Teacher Sketch Deck | One deck revision is tied to the exact plan hash, covers every activity, and each guide has adaptive 4–8 progressive steps to a recognizable figure, layout hint and child-friendly prompt; contains no child profile/drawing | BR-43; FR-T-18/20; FR-AI-14; UC-17 |
| AC-35 | Deck/presentation is READY on the current lesson/plan revision | Teacher previews, edits slides/media, presents or skips the guide | Teacher can edit text, add/delete/reorder slides, place uploaded image/SVG/YouTube-Vimeo embed on the correct slide and preview; guide has Back/Next/select/skip/regenerate; edits create a draft revision, source change marks dependent assets STALE; no freehand drawing/PPTX; AI SVG has its own cap and does not use video quota | BR-43/44/53; FR-T-19/20; FR-AI-14; FR-T-22; NFR-12/25/33; UC-17; Rev66 |
| AC-36 | A run has a photo of exactly one child, an approved idea and a valid activity | Run story/video pipeline through playback | VLM creates visual anchors → LLM creates structured story → 5–15 scene storyboard → adult confirms script → Character Bible/reference images → LTX-2.5 on Modal L40S per scene → consistency check → FFmpeg joins scenes → Vietnamese TTS/subtitles, no music → automatic safety → private playback; stale/unsafe/invalid output must not be played | BR-45; FR-AI-01–13; UC-02/05/16 |
| AC-37 | Video output completes technically and safety | System plays or adult requests regeneration | Video is playable immediately, without waiting for final approval; unsuitable output is hidden when a replacement is accepted, returning to script review; at most 1 initial + 2 video regens, each scene 1 + 2 retries; an accepted request consumes quota even on failure; no public gallery/download | BR-10/11/16/17/24/25/45; FR-AI-06/07/09/13; AT-17 |
| AC-38 | Generation G pins story revision R1; scene S belongs to R2 | Writer creates a generation-scene link between G and S | Composite FKs from generation+revision and scene+revision reject the record; repeated `story_revision_id` is an immutable constraint key; no application-only check or new link table is needed | §11.1 #21; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER §5.6–§7 |
| AC-39 | There is an ASR, VLM, LTX scene, composition or Sketch step job | System dispatches and receives duplicate/stale results | Each job has exactly one target kind/ref/revision; scene job targets exactly generation_scene_id, correlation groups siblings, Story checks reciprocal source_job_ref; `ai_jobs.attempt` is the sole retry ledger | FR-AI-01/06/13/14; §22; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER §4.1/§6 |
| AC-40 | StoryRun has current input/script/generation pointers and one aggregate revision | Concurrent commands/jobs complete and update state/pointers | Story transaction checks expected version, ownership chain and hashes; `video_generations.state` is canonical, `runs.state` is a concurrent projection; stale command is rejected and `VIDEO_READY` cannot stand alone when generation/asset/safety are not READY | §6.1.1; §11.1 #22–23; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER §6.3 |
| AC-41 | Client is allowed to read a StoryRun; some resources may belong to another owner/module | Client calls `GET /v1/story-runs/{id}` | One StoryRunDetailV1 aggregates status, timeline, scenes, job-safe errors, permitted assets and quota via owner ports; no cross-schema SQL, no frontend fan-out, no leak of prompt/transcript/profile support/foreign-scope existence, no read-model table | §9.5–§9.7; §22.4; NFR-05/10 |
| AC-42 | Compare data source inventory with target register | Review AI Policy and every source table disposition | Current crosswalk is 89 source mappings =38 retained/refactored +48 legacy +3 Lesson support; target =38 retained/refactored +54 new =92 active; +48 legacy =140 projected physical; 5 fixtures excluded. 12 copy registry and `resource_receipt` are read-only; 12 AI Policy active; legacy rows/IDs are not dropped/copied. Baseline 89 active/137 physical is v2.3 history, not the Rev31 target | §1.1; §12; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER §§1–4/9/11/14.4 |
| AC-43 | Resource command reserve/consume/release is sent repeatedly with the same key and fingerprint | AI Policy processes the command and emits a resource event | One event ledger row is the new receipt/idempotency source with response snapshot/delivery state; replay returns the same outcome; uniqueness distinguishes event kind if needed; source `resource_receipt` is kept read-only, with no history lost | §11.1 #14; §12.3; DATA_TABLE_REGISTER §4.1/§6 |
| AC-44 | Parent is in a drawing session while the child draws | Adult enables line-sketch guide or skips | When enabled, the request returns a progressive 4–8 step SVG guide from the approved catalog or a draft via a permitted capability; when disabled/skipped no job is created. Only topic/difficulty/guide contract is sent to the model; no child/profile/photo/audio/artwork refs. Output is private, temporary, with provenance/expiry; not attached to profile/StoryRun, does not use video quota. Any source/worker/timeout/quality failure does not block capture; after drawing the Parent takes a photo then VLM subject review | BR-42/46/54; FR-P-02/04/11; FR-AI-15; Q146/Q167/Rev66 |
| AC-45 | Parent guide request is rejected by timeout/limit or output is invalid/unsafe | Parent chooses to continue, retry within the cap, or exit the guide | No unvalidated output is displayed; safety errors do not expose provider detail; guide is cleaned up/not attached to profile/run; adult still reaches the drawing/capture flow; resource accounting uses the separate sketch cap and does not record video quota | BR-36/44/46; FR-P-11; FR-AI-12/15; NFR-23/25; Q146 |

### 13.1 Acceptance examples Given/When/Then

| ID | Given | When | Then | Requirement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AT-01 | Teacher occurrence has no sample claim; first video has played and been claimed | Teacher marks the video unsuitable; all remaining regeneration requests fail or are exhausted | Old video is not played back; occurrence remains `SAMPLE_USED`; API does not allow opening a second run; run ends in failure and the slot is not returned | BR-10, BR-16–17, Q127 |
| AT-02 | A video creation request passes admission and Billing has recorded usage | Provider, technical or safety gate fails; then adult deletes the run | Usage unit still exists, no refund; asset/run content is deleted per lifecycle; scene retry does not create another unit | BR-11, BR-26, BR-38, Q128 |
| AT-03 | Run is in `INPUT_REVIEW`, `SCRIPT_REVIEW` or `VIDEO_READY`, has no active AI job and adult-wait has reached 144 hours/168 hours | Scheduler reaches the reminder deadline then the idle deadline | Inbox notice and best-effort push at hour 144; at hour 168 `VIDEO_READY` becomes `COMPLETED`, other review states become `ABANDONED`; lock is released; content is not automatically deleted | FR-C-11, BR-39–40, Q129/Q133 |
| AT-04 | Class has runs/media; admin chooses a new Teacher; new Teacher has no active run | Assignment transaction commits | Old Teacher loses access and old playback tokens expire; new Teacher has access per assignment; creator actor is not rewritten; audit metadata records the transfer; admin still cannot view media | FR-T-14, BR-06, Q130 |
| AT-05 | Actor has registered a valid push endpoint | There is an idle/billing/job notice or provider reports endpoint invalid | Notice enters inbox regardless of push; best-effort push contains only an opaque reference; invalid endpoint is revoked; raw token/subscription does not appear in logs | FR-B-09, FR-B-14, NFR-26, Q131 |
| AT-06 | Parent/Teacher scope has a valid grant, no usage yet for the idempotency key | Story requests admission via the Billing port | Billing selects the grant by FIFO, writes one usage row in a transaction before dispatch; retry with the key returns the same receipt; AI Policy does not hold product-quota balance | FR-B-10–12, BR-38, Q132 |
| AT-07 | Run has received photo/idea; ASR and VLM jobs are running or retrying | Parent/Teacher loads run status | `StoryRun=INPUT_PROCESSING`; API returns stage/job state/deadline/time updated; no adult-wait timer is set while jobs remain; once jobs are terminal it moves to a review state | FR-O-10, FR-AI-01, NFR-06, Q133 |
| AT-08 | Budget reservation or quota admission is insufficient before a full-video request | Adult confirms the video creation request | No accepted generation/job/quota usage; returns `AI_COST_LIMIT_REACHED` or `VIDEO_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` as appropriate; if both admissions succeed, the reservation covers the request cap before dispatch | FR-B-10/11, NFR-25, BR-38 |
| AT-09 | Owner scope has exactly one quota unit left; two different requests attempt admission concurrently | Billing processes the competing requests | At most one request is ACCEPTED and has one usage row; the other request is rejected with no usage/job/dispatch; a retry of the winning request with the same idempotency key returns the old receipt | FR-B-10/11; BR-38; NFR-02 |
| AT-10 | Upload session has already received some app chunks; the app/browser loses network and then reopens | The actor resumes the same session, sends an exact-digest duplicate and then completes; afterwards attempts a wrong hash/state or the session expires | Each 4 MiB app chunk is stored as a separate object using PutObject; an exact duplicate is idempotent; part/hash conflicts are rejected; a partial asset does not reach the parser/model; the backend assembles a bounded stream, verifies total size + per-part/whole SHA-256 + format + AV, then performs a single PutObject of the final asset; byte/request/session limits allow a correct retry without double-counting unique bytes; expiry purges both temp objects and metadata | FR-C-12; NFR-32; AT-13 |
| AT-11 | A `DRAFT` run has no AI job yet; a video request has just been rejected at admission | The Scheduler runs at 144h/168h or Billing returns a rejected receipt | The DRAFT/review state is reminded and closed out on time; ADMISSION_PENDING has no timer; rejection returns to SCRIPT_REVIEW, does not consume quota/cap and starts a new wait; email is sent only when the active owner's email is verified | FR-C-11; FR-O-10; BR-39/40; AC-30 |
| AT-12 | Teacher A has an active run for the class; Teacher B is the replacement and does or does not have an active run of their own | Admin reassignment or emergency force-close + retry | Teacher A's access/token is fenced immediately; pending items do not expose content; the Story owner lock is transferred before BE1 finalize; a normal conflict returns 409; emergency close is metadata-only and does not grant the admin media access; a valid AI job continues and the quota/sample claim is retained | FR-T-14/17; BR-06/41; AC-31 |
| AT-13 | An upload session has 4 MiB app chunks that have been partially acknowledged | The client retries a valid chunk, completes, and sends a wrong hash | The session/part tables have receipts; each chunk is a separate PutObject; the backend verifies continuous part IDs, bytes, per-part + whole SHA-256, AV/format; final low-level PutObject; no S3 Multipart API; the unique-byte bucket does not increase on an exact retry; 429 limits do not block the configured maximum file flow | FR-C-12; NFR-09/12/32; AC-32 |
| AT-14 | The Parent has captured the drawing; no subject was chosen in advance | The VLM proposes a subject for the adult to confirm or edit | Editing the subject creates a new revision/VLM job on the same image hash; the Parent does not pre-select the subject of the child's drawing; an optional generic guide does not replace capture-first; the run is still tied to one child | BR-42; FR-P-02/04; AC-33 |
| AT-15 | The Teacher approves a plan with multiple activities | Create a Sketch Deck | The deck covers every activity; each instruction group has adaptive 4–8 progressive steps, with age/layout hints; the job has no child/profile/drawing ref; the deck pins the approved plan hash | BR-43; FR-T-18; FR-AI-14; AC-34 |
| AT-16 | The deck is READY; the Teacher requests regeneration of one sketch or the plan changes | Complete regeneration and request projection | Regeneration produces an immutable deck revision and keeps the assets of unchanged steps; a changed plan hash makes the deck stale; a separate bucket/cost cap does not create video quota usage; a stale deck is blocked | BR-43/44; FR-T-20; AC-35 |
| AT-17 | A run has exactly one child, an approved script, and starts a video request | Execute the entire pipeline with both failing and succeeding consistency/safety branches | Each scene has one first attempt and at most two automatic retries; LTX generates the scene → consistency check → FFmpeg assembly → Vietnamese TTS/subtitles, no music → safety; output that fails is not played, output that passes is played privately immediately without waiting for final video approval; quota and request limits apply to every accepted request | BR-45; FR-AI-01/06/07/09/13; AC-36/37 |
| AT-18 | The Parent is about to have the child draw and has no idea yet | The adult selects a topic/difficulty and then requests a guide or skips | The guide returns 4–8 progressive SVG steps; catalog-first, AI draft only when missing and the capability is enabled. Only topic/difficulty are sent; no profile/child/artwork ref. Skip/source/worker/timeout/quality failure does not block capture; the Parent still captures the photo and then does VLM subject review | BR-42/46; FR-P-11/FR-AI-15; AC-44; Rev66 |
| AT-19 | A Parent guide job is running or has just completed | Timeout, limit refusal, unsafe/invalid output, the adult skips/closes the guide, or the TTL expires | The capture flow remains usable; invalid/unsafe output is not displayed; the accepted guide asset is deleted when the session ends/the TTL arrives; the lineage/expiry receipt contains no child content; the resource event records sketch usage separately, with no video quota usage | BR-36/44/46; FR-AI-12/15; NFR-23/25; AC-45 |

<a id="srs-14"></a>
## 14. Decisions and deployment gates

Q01–Q146 have been answered and reconciled by the owner. Q145 was a temporary text-only form and was superseded by Q146 for the Parent pre-drawing guide.

### 14.0 Answered clarifications

| ID | Question | Current recommendation | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q146 | Should the Parent be allowed to use an AI-generated suggested line-sketch, for example a guide for drawing a butterfly in 3–5 steps, as a separate option before the child draws on their own? | Historical answer: the optional Parent guide used 3–5 steps at that time. Rev66 §31.17 supersedes its step count with adaptive 4–8 progressive SVG steps and replaces FLUX image generation with SVG catalog + deterministic engine. Generic input/privacy, separate compute and nonblocking mandatory capture remain. | ANSWERED — owner clarification, 2026-10-28; included in SRS v2.2-draft, not a sign-off of the whole SRS |

The following list consolidates the technical/operational/commercial gates that have an owner/evidence to be closed. The product decisions Q01–Q146 have been recorded; they do not by themselves close the deployment gates below:

### 14.1 List of open issues/gates

Q01–Q146 resolve the foundational product decisions. The following table collects the technical, legal, commercial and rollout decisions that still require an owner/evidence; the SRS/Data Table Register content is still a draft and these gates have not been closed. `Milestone to close` is a milestone condition, not a calendar date, because there is no approved release plan.

| ID | Issue/gate | Owner role | Milestone to close | Impact if not closed | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OI-01 | Android push provider other than Firebase; check distributor, opt-in, invalidation, battery/background behavior | Mobile + Architecture | Before P3/mobile push demo | Inbox/email still work; Android push cannot yet be committed, Q131 does not yet have sufficient operational evidence | Open POC; FCM excluded by repo rule |
| OI-02 | Video cost benchmark by L40S, GPU seconds, 45 scene attempts/request, storage/output and quota price | AI + Commercial + Billing | Before enabling live full-video requests | Default allowance, top-up price or economic unit not yet fixed | Not benchmarked |
| OI-03 | Hard cost reservation per request/day; confirm sufficient budget before Billing admission; prioritize completing accepted requests | AI + Billing + Operations | Before P1 video live | A mid-job cost cap may cause quota to be lost without a video; per Q128 no refund | Mandatory design, values need benchmark |
| OI-04 | Compare PostgreSQL outbox/RQ/Modal poller with Temporal for long sagas | Architecture + Operations | Before workflow implementation | Crash recovery/timers/cancellation may be complex or increase operations burden | No comparison ADR yet |
| OI-05 | Controller/processor, DPA, authorization/consent, privacy notice, DSR, DPIA/assessment and breach escalation | Privacy/Legal + Product | Before any real child data | Real-child data/provider use and production launch are prohibited | Awaiting expert |
| OI-06 | Max retention per profile/run/media/transcript/log/audit/payment; backup expiry and deletion proof | Privacy/Legal + Data owner | Before the real-data gate | Q66/Q129/Q133 are suitable only for a synthetic demo; indefinite retention must not be carried into production | Not fixed; demo retains until user-delete |
| OI-07 | Modal/provider region, cross-border transfer, object storage/hosting region and scratch deletion evidence | Privacy/Legal + Infra | Before real-child data/provider transfer | Only synthetic data may be sent | Not reviewed |
| OI-08 | Production host/network/region, availability SLO, RPO/RTO, alert destination, offsite backup/restore | Operations + Architecture | Before production deployment | No SLA/recovery commitment; the local target is only a draft goal | Not selected |
| OI-09 | Merchant MoMo/VNPAY sandbox, recurring contract, callback signature/freshness, cancel/retry and failure reconciliation | Billing + Legal/Commercial | Before P3 payment integration | Payment cannot be enabled; browser redirect must not be used as evidence | Adapter contract draft; sandbox not yet run |
| OI-10 | Electronic invoice/VAT, rounding/proration, consumer refund/support policy | Legal/Accounting + Commercial | Before production charging | Risk of incorrect invoices/pricing and customer obligations | Not reviewed; price not fixed |
| OI-11 | Production PostgreSQL schema/row/index inventory and source-to-target migration rehearsal/restore | Data owner + Backend | Before migration | Rev31 crosswalk 92/140 and v2.3 design 89/137 are source-derived; local DB snapshot in §32 is not production and does not prove target migration | Local development DB was read-only inspected in Rev43; production inventory/rehearsal remains open |
| OI-12 | Google Play Families/target-audience, child safety, privacy listing and app review requirements | Product + Mobile + Legal | Before Play publication | Onboarding/permissions/store listing may need to change | Not reviewed against the actual app listing |
| OI-13 | Teacher invitation/provisioning, account recovery, organization bootstrap and role support runbook | Identity + Product + Support | Before external pilot | Teacher/admin cannot be onboarded/recovered with support | Requirement exists; detailed UX/ops not approved |
