## 22. Asynchronous jobs and event processing

### 22.1 Job envelope

Each durable AI job carries:

| Field | Purpose |
|---|---|
| contract_name / contract_version | Typed and versioned request/response boundary |
| job_id / idempotency_key | Durable identifier and command deduplication |
| stage | LESSON_EXTRACT, SKETCH_DECK_PLAN, SKETCH_STEP_GENERATION, ASR, VLM_IMAGE_UNDERSTANDING, STORY_PLAN, STORY_REWRITE, STORYBOARD, SCENE_REFERENCE, VIDEO_SCENE, CONSISTENCY_VALIDATION, TTS, SUBTITLE, COMPOSITION, OUTPUT_SAFETY or VALIDATION |
| owner_scope_ref / actor_role | Server-granted ownership and policy context |
| target_kind / target_ref / expected_target_revision | Exactly one target kind/ref pair in each job: `STORY_RUN`, `VIDEO_GENERATION`, `VIDEO_GENERATION_SCENE`, `LESSON_SOURCE_DOCUMENT`, `LESSON_LIBRARY_VERSION`, `LESSON_VERSION_VISUAL_ASSET`, `LESSON_SKETCH_DECK_REVISION` or `PARENT_SKETCH_GUIDE`; optimistic concurrency per target |
| correlation_id / input_hash | Links stages/jobs within the same run/generation and pins the exact input set; correlation does not replace the target or the owner-local FK |
| source_asset_refs / source_hashes | Only minimal refs/hashes for input artifacts; do not copy binaries, prompts or profile fields into the job |
| pinned_lesson/activity/context refs | Only when the stage needs them; the lesson/story owner verifies scope and revision via a port, and does not store a mutable profile/context snapshot again in the job |
| model_profile_ref / config_hash | Reproducible runtime selection, containing no secrets |
| attempt / deadline / cancellation_state | Bounded processing; the attempt stores an opaque workspace ref, manifest hash and cleanup state/receipt for Modal synthetic |
| result_refs or safe_error | Typed output or a safe error; no raw stack trace |

The job contract rejects lists of child refs and requires exactly one target kind/ref. ASR/VLM/story planning/rewrite target `STORY_RUN`; reference preparation, consistency, TTS, subtitle, composition and safety target `VIDEO_GENERATION`; each LTX inference targets exactly one `VIDEO_GENERATION_SCENE`; lesson extraction targets `LESSON_SOURCE_DOCUMENT`; the Teacher deck targets `LESSON_SKETCH_DECK_REVISION`; the Parent guide targets `PARENT_SKETCH_GUIDE`, with the request ID existing only on the AI Job and no separate Parent aggregate/table. A video scene row links back to the job via a typed versioned ref, and the Story owner verifies that the scene and generation match. A StoryRun target resolves exactly one child from Story canonical, without repeating the profile ref in AI Jobs. Neither sketch target has a child/profile/child drawing; the Parent guide contains only topic/difficulty and output-ref/lifecycle metadata. All job types share the same dispatch/attempt/outbox; no new history is written into legacy JSON arrays.

### 22.2 Dispatch and completion

1. The application command verifies domain state and writes the aggregate revision together with the outbox event in a single PostgreSQL transaction.
2. The outbox dispatcher sends a minimal job ref to Redis/RQ. The queue is used only for delivery; PostgreSQL remains the canonical source for the job record.
3. The worker receives the job via lease/heartbeat, loads versioned and authorized input, verifies the hash, then calls only its own provider port.
4. The provider adapter maps the provider's own response/errors to a typed internal result. The adapter does not modify story tables.
5. The worker sends a completion command to the application layer. Completion verifies the exact target kind/ref/revision/hash; a StoryRun target checks run/input/lesson/activity; a generation target checks run/script/generation; a scene target checks that the scene belongs to the correct generation/revision; a Lesson document/deck target checks occurrence assignment/plan hash/deck schema; a Parent guide target checks the generic topic contract, safe output, owner scope and expiry policy.
6. A valid result is written by the corresponding owner. Story scene job completion updates scene state/output ref; overall generation completion updates canonical `video_generations.state`, then the same Story transaction projects `story.runs.state` and current pointers. `VIDEO_READY` only after current generation READY, validation gates, and output asset exist. Lesson owner handles deck outputs; Parent guide returns a private temporary Core Asset ref with provenance/expiry and no profile/StoryRun attachment. AI job event/completion receipt stores execution disposition/hash only. Stale/rejected/expired outputs are cleaned through Core asset lifecycle. No worker writes across owner tables.
7. A terminal error stores a safe code and correlation ID for the operator. The client receives a localized stage status and does not see a stack trace.

Long Modal inference does not occupy an RQ worker while the GPU runs. The adapter calls `Function.spawn()` and persists the Modal FunctionCall `object_id` together with the attempt before the worker finishes; the poller reloads it with `FunctionCall.from_id()` and checks the result non-blockingly. If a submit times out but it is unclear whether Modal received the job, keep `AWAITING_RECONCILIATION`, reconcile FunctionCall/outbox, and only then decide; do not automatically submit a duplicate GPU job. Cancellation is best-effort and does not change quota already consumed.

### 22.3 Retry, cancellation and queue recovery

- `ai_jobs.attempt` is the single provider-attempt/retry ledger. Scene creation allows exactly one initial inference plus two automatic technical retries; `story.video_generation_scenes` stores the current scene/job state and asset pointer, and does not store a second retry counter. Record the attempt before calling the provider the next time.
- The script confirmation command creates a `VideoGeneration=ADMISSION_PENDING` intent with a durable idempotency key before any external admission. The orchestrator first reserves compute budget in AI Policy, then Billing records usage in a separate transaction under the same request key. Only after both receipts are confirmed does Story change the intent to `ACCEPTED`, assign the generation number in the Story transaction and write the outbox for dispatch; request cap/run and quota are counted only from the `ACCEPTED` state. If budget/quota is rejected, the intent becomes `ADMISSION_REJECTED`, and quota usage and the AI job must not be created. If the process dies midway or a timeout makes the result ambiguous, the reconciler reads both receipts under the same key; a reservation not yet attached to Billing usage is released/expired, while Billing usage already recorded is completed idempotently into an accepted intent and then dispatched exactly once. A reservation attached to an accepted request is held/settled according to the actual job and does not expire while inference is still within its deadline. There is no SQL transaction across modules; retrying with the same key does not create a second request/usage/inference.
- Each accepted full video-creation request atomically consumes one request-cap and one Billing quota unit before inference is dispatched. The request is still counted if a technical failure or safety block occurs afterwards. Only a technical/safety failure before any playable video claims the occurrence leaves the sample slot free and allows a replacement run; once the sample claim has been committed there is no other recovery run for the occurrence, even if all quality regenerations fail. If the owner scope's monthly quota is exhausted, reject the request and do not consume quota.
- Temporary transport errors may be redelivered with configured bounded backoff; idempotency prevents duplicate state transitions. This delivery retry does not increase the scene attempt counter unless inference is actually called again.
- When the provider supports it, use a provider request key deterministically derived from the job ID and stage attempt. If a timeout leaves the inference result unknown and the provider does not guarantee status query/idempotency, mark it as requiring reconciliation; do not automatically launch unbounded duplicate GPU jobs.
- Do not retry invalid input, authorization denial, unsupported files, OCR_NOT_SUPPORTED, malformed adult data, or policy rejection.
- Other AI stages use a bounded operational retry policy, defined per adapter at implementation time; do not silently turn a provider error into a story rewrite or a change of provider model.
- Cancelling an incomplete run moves the run to DELETE_PENDING and releases the active lock in the transaction that records the command. Write `StoryRunCancellationRequestedV1`, create a lifecycle request, revoke playback/upload, and request cancellation of pending/running jobs. The worker checks cancellation before resource-intensive stages and before accepting results; if the provider cannot be interrupted, late-arriving output is stale, not playable, and is deleted through the lifecycle. Keep the quota usage already consumed and any successful Teacher sample claim.
- Deleting a run in COMPLETED, FAILED or ABANDONED also creates a lifecycle request; the delete command does not create a refund or delete the quota ledger/sample claim metadata.
- A durable scheduler sets `adult_wait_started_at` when no AI job is active and the run needs an adult, sends a reminder at the +144 hour mark, then closes at +168 hours. The inbox uses a dedupe key containing run/state/deadline version; a valid adult command resets the mark and makes the old schedule stale. Background polling, push receipt and app heartbeat do not reset idle. Closeout verifies the expected state/deadline/version in the transaction; VIDEO_READY→COMPLETED, adult-wait review state→ABANDONED, keeping the content and releasing the lock. An AI job has an independent 6-hour deadline; timestamps are stored in UTC and do not rely on the device clock.
- When a class is reassigned, the current BE1 assignment is the canonical source of authority: the workflow is a durable saga, each owner writes in its own transaction and hands over a versioned receipt. BE1 moves the class to transfer-pending/read-only and fences the old Teacher's token/permissions; the Story command checks uniqueness, moves `active_owner_teacher_id`/the active lock and increments the owner epoch; BE1 finalizes the new assignment only after the Story receipt. If Story rejects because the new Teacher has an active run, the saga compensates BE1 state back to the old assignment; if the compensation result is ambiguous, keep transfer-pending/read-only and reconcile, granting content to no one. Emergency force-close is a separate metadata-only command before retrying. A valid job is not cancelled merely because of the transfer: completion checks the run/input/script/activity/occurrence hashes, that the organization/class is still valid, that it is not cancelled/deleted, and the current transfer receipt; the original creator/assignment version does not grant permission. If the input/occurrence/class binding has changed, the result is stale. Only the current owner has permission to view/play back.
- Loss of Redis must not delete pending job records. On recovery, the dispatcher scans the unconsumed outbox/job state in PostgreSQL and then republishes idempotently.
- A job that cannot be processed after multiple attempts remains in the terminal FAILED state in PostgreSQL. An Operator may view safe metadata and start a new permitted run; video scenes in a failed run must not be manually retried beyond the configured limit.
- Worker concurrency and Modal concurrency may be configured separately. A child's runs must not run in parallel; scenes within the same run may run in parallel only within the configured GPU/resource limits and are assembled in storyboard order.

### 22.4 Status and progress on the client

The client receives a stage code and a user-friendly label, for example: “Đang nhận nội dung”, “Đang đọc tranh”, “Đang tạo kịch bản”, “Đang tạo từng cảnh”, “Đang ghép video”, “Đang kiểm tra video”, “Sẵn sàng phát”, “Cần chỉnh lại kịch bản”, “Không tạo được lần này”. A safely blocked artifact must never be granted playback permission.

StoryRun status includes a `run_state` separate from `job_state`/`job_stage` for ASR, VLM, story and video; SketchDeck status is a separate Lesson resource with a job stage for extraction/deck/step generation. Each status returns the target revision, version, progress only when measurable, start/update times, retryability and a safe error code. This distinguishes model processing from an adult reviewing and prevents a run/deck from appearing stuck. Exact percentages must not be fabricated for opaque model tasks. The client polls with the backoff in §9.5, stops at terminal states and reduces frequency when running in the background; push only signals that an update exists, and the client fetches the new status through an authenticated API.

`GET /v1/story-runs/{id}` returns an aggregated StoryRunDetailV1 so the frontend does not call multiple endpoints per module. The application read composer reads Story as the root, calls AI Jobs for stage/job status by target, Core for asset/playback readiness, Lesson to check occurrence/assignment when the caller is a Teacher, and Billing to return a quota summary when permitted. The response includes scene/generation status, current input/script/generation refs, a safe error code, current authorization and a short-lived playback ref if permissions suffice. It must not return prompts/provider payloads, raw transcripts, profile support notes, hidden revisions, or the existence of resources outside scope. The endpoint must not store a read-model table or duplicate mutable state; it uses a cursor/version for safe caching and ETag.

### 22.5 Domain event catalog

These are proposed versioned internal event names. They are envelope metadata, not a mechanism for sharing raw profile/media content between modules.

| Event | Owner | Consumer/purpose | Minimum payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| OrganizationCreatedV1 | BE1 | Initialize Billing/entitlement lookup | organization ref, actor ref, version |
| ClassCreatedV1 | BE1 | Reconcile/audit the capacity ledger | organization/class refs, assignment revision |
| ManagedProfileRevisionCreatedV1 | BE1 | Determine eligibility to create a Story context snapshot | profile ref, revision ref/hash, scope |
| LessonDocumentAcceptedV1 | Lesson | Dispatch the extraction worker | occurrence/document refs, asset hash, parser contract |
| LessonPlanApprovedV1 | Lesson | Determine eligibility to create a Teacher run and Sketch Deck | occurrence/class/teacher refs, plan revision/hash |
| SketchDeckRequestedV1 | Lesson | Orchestrate the AI Job that generates the deck for the occurrence | occurrence/plan revision/hash, target age band, assignment version, idempotency ref; no child/profile ref or child's drawing |
| SketchDeckReadyV1 | Lesson | Allow presentation of the validated deck | occurrence/deck revision ref, current plan hash, step count, schema version; no sensitive content |
| SketchDeckGenerationFailedV1 | Lesson | Show a safe error and let the Teacher retry within limits | occurrence/deck revision ref, safe error code, retryable, correlation ref |
| StoryRunCreatedV1 | Story | Orchestrate jobs/audit | run ref, actor role, one profile ref, run revision |
| StoryInputAcceptedV1 | Story | Plan ASR/VLM jobs | run/input refs, source hash; no binary/text |
| TranscriptConfirmedV1 | Story | Precondition for the Story planner | run/transcript revision/hash |
| SubjectConfirmedV1 | Story | Precondition for the Story planner | run/understanding revision/hash |
| ContextSnapshotApprovedV1 | Story | Precondition for the Story planner | run/snapshot ref/hash and allowlist version |
| StoryRevisionCreatedV1 | Story | UI review/job readiness | run/story revision/hash, rewrite index |
| ScriptConfirmedV1 | Story | Determine eligibility to create a video job | run/revision/hash, confirming adult ref |
| VideoGenerationRequestedV1 | Story | Dispatch AI Jobs | run/generation ref, confirmed story hash |
| VideoOutputSafetyPassedV1 | Story | Allow transition to playback | run/generation refs, policy version, safe decision code, coverage summary |
| VideoOutputSafetyBlockedV1 | Story | Block playback and return to script review | run/generation refs, policy version, safe finding codes |
| VideoGenerationCompletedV1 | Story | Ready for playback/ensure sample uniqueness | run/generation/artifact refs; output hash remains canonical in Core Asset metadata |
| VideoGenerationFailedV1 | Story | Safe run status/failure metrics | run/job ref, safe failure code |
| VideoQuotaGrantIssuedV1 | Billing | Update available quota after verifying a purchase or issuing the monthly quota | owner-scope ref, quota-grant ref, units, period/expiry and event ref; no child/run/media data |
| VideoQuotaUnitConsumedV1 | Billing | Immutably record one unit for an accepted full-video request before dispatch | owner-scope ref, grant ref, usage ref, idempotency fingerprint and units=1; no child/run/media data or unknown outcome |
| ClassProjectionConfirmedV1 | Lesson | Issue a short-lived token so the Teacher can control the presentation | occurrence ref, generation ref, responsible-Teacher ref, assignment revision and confirmation time |
| TeacherAssignmentChangedV1 | BE1 | Transfer run/media access to the currently assigned Teacher; revoke the previous access and fence stale jobs/tokens | class ref, old/new Teacher refs opaque, assignment version, effective time; metadata only |
| StoryRunIdleReminderRequestedV1 | Story | Send an in-app/push reminder one day before closeout | run ref opaque, expected state/deadline version, dedupe key; no content |
| StoryRunIdleClosedV1 | Story | Close the run under the 7-day inactivity rule and release the active lock | run ref opaque, prior state, COMPLETED/ABANDONED result, expected version, occurred_at |
| StoryRunCancellationRequestedV1 | Story | Release the active lock and start the cancel/delete lifecycle for an unfinished run | run ref, actor ref, expected revision, lifecycle request ref, safe reason code; no media/content |
| StoryRunDeletedV1 | Story | Delete lifecycle/object | run ref, lifecycle request ref, scope |
| PaymentVerifiedV1 | Billing | Activate capacity/notification | invoice/subscription ref, amount/currency, provider event ref |
| SubscriptionRestrictedV1 | Billing | Block commands that create new classes/runs | organization ref, effective capacity period, state |
| CapacityClassSuspensionScheduledV1 | Billing | Put the read-only state into BE1 at the effective time | change-group ref, one class ref, effective time, capacity period |
| BillingNoticeRequestedV1 | Billing | Notification outbox | organization/billing recipient ref, notice template and dedupe key |

Each event envelope has an event ID, event name/version, occurred-at UTC, producer module, aggregate ref/version, correlation ID and an idempotency/dedupe reference. The consumer stores a receipt before creating side effects. Events are retried at-least-once; handlers must be idempotent. Display names, transcripts, story text, provider secrets or media bytes must not be put into events.

<a id="srs-23"></a>
## 23. Billing lifecycle details

### 23.1 Capacity and pricing model

- Each organization has 10 class slots in the basic plan; the basic plan fee and the fee per additional slot are configured and versioned; the billing cycle is monthly or yearly, with no tiers.
- Prices are stored as immutable versions in VND. A new price applies to new quotes/periods by effective date; an issued invoice keeps its price/amount snapshot.
- Effective class capacity equals the 10 basic slots plus the add-on slots paid for in the period, minus the classes chosen for suspension when a downgrade takes effect. Only active classes within capacity may be written to; suspended classes can still be read/managed in a read-only state.

### 23.2 Capacity upgrade and proration

When an admin buys additional slots mid-period:

1. The backend builds a quote/invoice snapshot from the price version, the remaining time and the number of added slots.
2. MoMo/VNPAY checkout starts through the payment adapter. A browser redirect is not evidence of payment.
3. The verified successful payment event is deduplicated; within a transaction, update the payment attempt/invoice and create the effective capacity period/add-on.
4. Slots are activated immediately after successful verification.
5. The prorated fee is based on the remaining time of the provider period and does not change the renewal date. The invoice stores the base price, the increased quantity, period start/end, the remaining-time ratio, the rounded VND amount and the price version. The following formula is a proposed deterministic rule and must appear in the quote before payment.

Proposed formula: prorated add-on fee = number of added slots × price per slot for the full period × (remaining seconds / total seconds of the current provider period), rounded to the nearest whole VND using half-up. Use UTC timestamps; store the ratio and the rounded amount in the invoice snapshot. Tax/discounts are a separate commercial configuration.

If the callback is delayed, the UI shows that it is pending; do not activate slots before verification. A failed payment does not change capacity. Duplicate/out-of-order callbacks must not charge or activate twice.

### 23.3 Renewal, grace period and restriction

| Event | Subscription/capacity result | User permissions |
|---|---|---|
| 7 days until due | Schedule a reminder | Current permissions |
| 1 day until due | Schedule a reminder | Current permissions |
| Verified successful renewal | Start a new paid period | Sufficient capacity |
| Renewal failed | GRACE for 7 days; in-app/email notification | Existing classes, roster, history, playback and payment updates remain usable; new classes/runs can still be created during the grace period |
| Payment recovered within the grace period | ACTIVE, invoice reconciliation | Fully restored |
| Grace period ended unpaid | RESTRICTED; send notification | Must not create new classes/runs; can still read/manage existing classes, roster, history and payments |
| Auto-renew turned off | CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END | Use paid features until the end of the period |
| Period ended without renewal | RESTRICTED or CANCELED depending on the provider | Keep data; restrict new creation |

Restriction starts only after the full 7-day grace period, per the owner's decision.

### 23.4 Capacity downgrade, cancellation and notification

- The admin reduces the number of add-on slots and chooses the classes to suspend before confirming.
- Billing writes one subscription_events row for each selected class, with a shared capacity_change_group_ref, class ref, capacity before/after, capacity period and effective timestamp. Commit the entire selection atomically before confirming the downgrade.
- Paid capacity is kept until the period boundary. In the next period, the versioned event CapacityClassSuspensionScheduledV1 projects the selected classes as read-only in BE1; retries are idempotent, and the event group is reconciled with the class refs. Class/roster/run history must not be deleted. Transferring a class/reactivating it requires capacity.
- Turning off auto-renew prevents the next charge; the used portion of the period is not automatically refunded. V1 exceptions are handled manually outside automated billing.
- The dedupe key for in-app/email notifications is organization + trigger/event + period + recipient. Remind 7 days and 1 day before the due date; success/failure/end-of-grace notifications are sent per event.
- Send failures are retried with bounded backoff and recorded; verified payment/capacity must not be reversed. The admin views delivery status, not child content.

### 23.5 Video quota grant, usage and top-up

- Parent quota belongs to one Parent account; a video initiated by a Teacher is recorded against the quota of the organization that owns the occurrence. There is no Teacher-account pool. The backend derives the owner scope from the principal and the validated assignment; the client does not choose the owner ID/quota.
- Billing owns `billing.video_quota_grants` and `billing.video_quota_usage`. A grant is an append-only entitlement; usage is an append-only ledger, where each accepted full-video request records exactly one unit and references the grant used. AI Policy `resource_reservation`/`resource_event` only measure platform GPU/compute capacity and resource cost; a resource event is the idempotency ledger for new commands, while the historical `resource_receipt` table receives no further writes and does not participate in product quota.
- A monthly included grant is attached to a Parent-account or school-organization scope; a Teacher uses the school grant of the occurrence, and there is no Teacher-account grant/pool. The included amount is fixed after the synthetic cost/latency benchmark. A scope that starts mid-month receives the full included allowance for that month, with no proration (Q115, Q121). The included grant expires at the end of the calendar month in `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`; any remainder does not carry over to the next month. A top-up is a one-time purchase that does not auto-renew and does not expire; the current included grant is used first, then top-up in FIFO order (Q114, Q117–Q120, Q126).
- Quota admission is a Billing use case exposed through a versioned port. Story records an `ADMISSION_PENDING` intent; the application saga obtains a compute-reservation receipt from AI Policy, then calls Billing to check the scope/grant and record usage in a separate transaction owned by Billing. Only when both receipts have a confirmed status does Story move the generation intent to `ACCEPTED` in the Story transaction and write the outbox; there is no single PostgreSQL transaction spanning the modules. If the budget/quota is rejected, the saga records the intent as `ADMISSION_REJECTED`, releases the reservation if any, and does not dispatch. If the outcome is unclear after a crash/timeout, reconcile using the same idempotency key before dispatch; usage already committed in Billing must be re-linked to the Story intent exactly once, and must not be recreated or refunded. `billing_quota_usage_ref` in `story.video_generations` is an opaque receipt reference, not an FK/shared ledger column. The balance is computed only within Billing from grants minus usage.
- Only a request rejected before admission commit does not consume quota. Once an accepted request/usage has been committed before dispatch, the unit must not be refunded in any case: queue/provider ambiguity, technical failure, safety block, cancellation, or run deletion (Q110, Q119, Q128). Retrying with the same idempotency key returns the same generation/usage; a technical scene retry is not a new request. Each run has at most one initial request plus two quality regeneration requests; every received request counts toward this cap, even if it fails.
- If the scope has no units left, reject with 409 `VIDEO_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, and do not create an accepted generation, usage row, or AI dispatch. Running out of quota does not block viewing saved videos/data or deleting a run. Deleting a run purges content according to the lifecycle but retains minimal usage metadata for reconciliation; the consumed grant is not refunded.
- When an offer is configured/activated by a Platform Admin, quote prices using a server-controlled price version, create a typed invoice per owner scope, and issue a grant only after a MoMo/VNPAY payment event has been verified, deduplicated, and recorded idempotently. A Parent purchases for their own account; a School Manager purchases for the assigned organization and allocates/revokes the unused portion of Teacher allocation. An Admin manages offer/price versions. A browser redirect/pending state is not settlement. A quota purchase is not refunded after successful settlement; consumed usage must not be deleted or refunded.

Product quota, rate limit, GPU concurrency, and cost cap are three separate mechanisms: quota limits the customer's entitlement; rate limit prevents API abuse; capacity/circuit breaker blocks overload or exceeding the operating cost ceiling.
<a id="srs-24"></a>
## 24. Data classification and media handling

### 24.1 Data classification

| Type | Examples | Handling |
|---|---|---|
| Public | Published activity descriptions, catalog age groups | Versioned, reviewed; no private run refs |
| Account/organization | Firebase UID ref, organization/class ID, role assignment, billing contact | Server-side authorization, least privilege, not public |
| Child profile | Display name, age in months/group, interests, observations, achievements | Entered by an adult; per owner scope; name is not sent to the model |
| Sensitive support | Support-need group, encrypted note | Narrow profile permission; excluded from AI context by default; no diagnosis |
| Child-created media | Drawing, voice idea, story, video | Private object storage; demo uses synthetic data only; no logging/public link |
| Lesson | Original PDF/PPTX/DOCX and extracted/edited text | Teacher responsible; keep source/revision; no OCR of scanned pages |
| Payment | Provider refs, invoice, event IDs, amounts | Store only necessary merchant refs; do not store card/bank secrets |
| Operations | Correlation ID, run/job ID, status, stage duration | Redacted, limited retention, opaque IDs; no free text/content |
| Secrets | Firebase verifier config, Modal key, payment signing secrets, SMTP | Ignored file/runtime secret manager; not in source control/client |

The activity catalog uses a typed/versioned contract and adds no table. The target contract ActivityCatalogItemV2 has these fields: supported ages/month ranges; subject/objectives; theme; setting/context; season; outdoor location; duration; materials/equipment; accessibility; safety constraints; status/review version. This is structured metadata, not a free-form prompt. The current catalog contract does not yet have all of these fields; filter only by fields provided by the current version. Enable filtering based on the full metadata only after upgrading the contract version, fixtures, and client/server adapters in sync. Do not add tables: the existing learning_content.record/version remains the authoritative source. Moderation is unchanged: a Parent selects from the approved global catalog; a Teacher selects from the catalog or a custom activity within an occurrence confirmed by a safety checklist.

### 24.2 Upload validation and document analysis

- Cross-check the extension, declared MIME, and file signature; do not trust the extension alone.
- Quarantine uploads until a ClamAV/scanner-compatible verdict of `CLEAN`; a file not yet scanned must not enter any ASR/VLM/parser/model job. `INFECTED`, `ERROR`, or timeout fail closed; keep in quarantine or reject according to policy.
- Binaries are sent through a resumable backend API upload session; `core.upload_sessions` holds principal/scope/purpose, expected media/size/hash, expiry, and session state; `core.upload_parts` holds part number, size/hash, and an opaque object ref for each application chunk. Each chunk (at most 4 MiB) is a separate object via PutObject, without using the S3 Multipart Upload API. The backend assembles only with contiguous part numbers, verifies per-part/whole SHA-256 and total bytes, runs media validation/AV, then creates the final asset with a PutObject of known size. Partial bytes cannot be attached or passed to a model; expired/aborted sessions and chunk objects are purged idempotently by TTL. The client receives only an opaque ref/progress, not the storage endpoint/credentials.
- Store the verdict, scanner/version, checksum, and scan time in asset-validation metadata/event; do not log file bytes, display name, or content signature.
- Lesson limit is 25 MB and 100 pages/slides before extraction. Reject encrypted or password-protected PDF/Office files. Mark scanned pages as unsupported; no OCR fallback.
- PPTX/DOCX are ZIP containers: limit decompressed size, entry count, nesting, and processing time to reduce archive-bomb risk. Do not run macros, embedded scripts, or external links.
- The parser runs in a resource-limited worker with no arbitrary outbound network. Extract text only; do not execute/render embedded objects.
- Reject faulty files with a stable safe code; store parser name/version, page coverage, warnings, and source hash.
- Audio is at most 20 MB/2 minutes. The list of accepted MIME/container/codec is an implementation gate; validate by decoding, not by file name.
- Proposed limits for synthetic demo images: 20 MiB encoded, 40 megapixels decoded, JPEG/PNG/WebP. Decode in an isolated, resource-limited worker; normalize EXIF orientation/color into a derived asset, keeping the original unchanged. An oversized image returns IMAGE_DIMENSIONS_EXCEEDED; do not resize before recording the source hash/provenance.
- Limit decompression and decoded pixels to prevent image bombs. Compute SHA-256 while streaming the upload; do not trust MIME, filename, or dimensions declared by the client.

### 24.3 Storage, lineage, and lifecycle

- PostgreSQL stores business metadata/references; MinIO/private object store stores binaries.
- The object key is a random opaque ID and does not contain name, school, date of birth, or original file name.
- Each source/derived object records owner scope, media type, byte size, checksum, upload/transform time, source ref, model/config revision, and deletion state.
- The original is immutable. Crop, reference frame, scene clips, narration, subtitle file, and final MP4 are separate assets.
- The deletion lifecycle revokes playback/upload authorization, cancels jobs, then deletes eligible objects/metadata in idempotent batches. A failure keeps deletion-pending for retry; it is not marked complete until objects are fully reconciled.
- Sharing assets between runs is not a V1 feature. Delete a referenced asset only when the lifecycle confirms there is no remaining valid reference.

#### Contract for transferring synthetic data from local Docker to Modal

Local MinIO is the authoritative store for original/derived media. Modal receives only a per-job working copy in an isolated scratch prefix; it has no MinIO credentials, Docker-network route, or long-lived URL to the local store.

1. The backend validates, streams, and hashes the upload into a private MinIO key. Only validated assets and allowlisted, size-limited text/context are staged to inference.
2. The worker creates a dedicated prefix sketch2life/{environment}/{job_id}/{generation_id}/{attempt_id}/ in a Modal Volume, uploading through the Modal SDK local-to-volume batch interface. Each prefix is single-writer; jobs do not share mutable filenames/scratch paths.
3. The remote function receives only an opaque job/generation ID, a versioned contract, allowlisted path refs, SHA-256, and small metadata; it does not receive raw image/audio bytes, the local MinIO endpoint, child name, Firebase token, or payment data as arguments.
4. Input transfer is at most 48 MiB/job: one drawing of at most 20 MiB, an optional voice of at most 20 MiB, and at most 1 MiB of extracted lesson/story/context text plus manifest overhead. Do not stage the original lesson document; only reviewed text. Deadline of 300 seconds, at most two idempotent transport retries.
5. Output is written only in the attempt's output prefix. The worker retrieves it via the SDK and checks path containment, manifest, SHA-256, byte cap, codec, dimensions, and duration; the application checks sufficient narration/subtitle coverage and an automated output-safety gate. Only output that passes both gates becomes a playable private MinIO asset; verify the stored hash. A safety-blocked candidate is not published/issued a playback token; delete the scratch copy and keep only safe decision metadata.
6. The final MP4 is at most 256 MiB. A hash mismatch, unexpected/missing output, traversal path, or invalid media returns MODAL_RESULT_INVALID; transfer exhaustion returns MODAL_TRANSFER_FAILED. No result is playable.
7. After a verified return or terminal failure, delete the attempt prefix. An idempotent orphan sweeper deletes unreferenced prefixes after 24 hours. Deleting a run also schedules scratch cleanup; the MinIO lifecycle does not depend on Modal cleanup succeeding.
8. Pass job_id/storage-path refs instead of large function arguments. Modal recommends external object storage for large/async arguments and uses the default centralized storage region; Volume staging is still external processing. These limits are for the synthetic demo only. Real data requires review of provider region, transfer, retention, and deletion before activation.

48 MiB/256 MiB/timeout are proposed synthetic-demo caps, not yet measured. Verify limits and SDK/Volume support before enabling live video. [Modal local data](https://modal.com/docs/guide/local-data), [Modal data residency](https://modal.com/docs/guide/data-residency), [Modal Volumes](https://modal.com/docs/guide/volumes), [Modal cloud bucket mounts](https://modal.com/docs/guide/cloud-bucket-mounts).

<a id="srs-25"></a>
## 25. Environment and deployment requirements

### 25.1 Environment separation

| Environment | Purpose | Data/provider policy |
|---|---|---|
| Local developer | Individual development | Synthetic fixtures only; Docker Compose; Mailpit; Modal dev environment only when the implementation configures it explicitly |
| Shared demo | Reproducible walkthrough | Synthetic profile/media; isolated test organization; payment sandbox; no real child data is used |
| Staging | Integration verification | Synthetic data; provider sandbox; isolated secrets/storage; production payment is not used |
| Production | Real organizations | SRS not finalized; hosting, privacy, security, operations, budget and rollout require separate approval |

The current authorization is for documentation only; do not create/configure these environments in this work.

### 25.2 Compose service requirements

| Service | Role | Persistence/health |
|---|---|---|
| api | FastAPI routes, command/query adapters | Stateless; readiness checks the DB, queue and object-store client |
| worker | RQ jobs and scheduled tasks | Stateless process; heartbeat; graceful shutdown/cancel check |
| postgres | PostgreSQL 17, local demo source of truth | Named volume; readiness probe; backup/restore documentation |
| redis | Queue transport, rate-limit counters | Named volume optional for demo; jobs can be rebuilt from PostgreSQL |
| minio | Private S3-compatible object store | Named volume; liveness/readiness; synthetic content only |
| otel-collector | Receives traces/metrics/logs | Config-mounted; collector health |
| prometheus | Scrapes metrics | Named volume optional; scrape health |
| grafana | Local dashboard | Demo admin secret from ignored config; no default production credential |
| mailpit | Local SMTP sink | Local Web UI/SMTP; does not send external email |
| teacher-ui (optional profile) | Teacher web app | Dev server/build container; does not contain backend secrets |

Android runs on the host/emulator or an approved simulator, not as a privileged container. Compose exposes only the necessary host ports. By default, Database, Redis and MinIO are not exposed publicly to the external network. Persistent volumes are separate/named; deleting volumes is not part of a normal stop/start.

PostgreSQL 17 is the current Compose/local demo compatibility baseline, not a claim that it is the latest upstream. PostgreSQL 18 is the current major as of the review date; evaluating 18 is a separate migration/deployment gate, checking extensions, drivers, migrations, backup/restore and data compatibility.

### 25.3 Configuration and secrets

The following configuration names are examples for standardization; do not commit real values:

- Database URL, pool sizing.
- Redis URL, queue names, worker concurrency.
- MinIO internal endpoint, bucket names, runtime credentials.
- Firebase project/audience and server-side ID-token verifier.
- Modal workspace/environment, function IDs, timeout/concurrency, provider secret reference.
- Model profile IDs/revisions, prompt-template versions, content-policy versions, inference limits.
- MoMo/VNPAY merchant IDs, callback URLs, signing/verification secret references.
- SMTP adapter endpoint; local uses Mailpit.
- OTel collector endpoint, sampling/redaction flags, service name.
- Upload byte/page/duration limits, rate-limit policy, playback token lifetime, retention config.

Secrets come from ignored local files or runtime secret management; .env.example contains placeholders only. The startup validator fails closed if production-mode secrets or callback verification are missing. Do not log environment dumps.

### 25.4 Production topology is a separate ADR

Production region, API/database host, object storage, network path to Modal, key management, email vendor, alert destination, backup frequency, recovery objectives, scale and SLA have not been chosen. Do not infer that local Docker Compose is the production design.

### 25.5 CI/CD and release

CI/CD already has GitHub Actions CI at `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, running on `push` and `pull_request`. The workflow currently has repository-policy checks using Python 3.12.14, backend contract/unit/integration jobs, and Child/Teacher/Admin UI checks using Node 24.15.0 (`npm ci`, `npm run check`). This describes the source/configuration, not the result of a specific CI run. The Rev58 source snapshot in §32.8 shows no CD workflow; the deployment provider/host/region/registry has not been chosen.

**Target CI/CD flow (TO-BE):**

1. Every pull request and push runs the existing CI jobs together with the approved gates. Only a green status allows merging into `dev`; artifacts are tied to the full commit SHA and an immutable digest.
2. After merging `dev`, the pipeline packages once and then deploys exactly that artifact to staging. Staging uses only fixture/synthetic data, not real child profiles or media.
3. Staging checks health/readiness and smoke according to the contracts: a Parent completes the one-child flow when no Lesson Preset is attached and no Lesson-specific activity is added; a Teacher occurrence keeps the pinned lesson version/hash when the lesson has a new revision; a synthetic roster with no eligible child returns `SAMPLE_NOT_AVAILABLE`, does not reveal child details, does not create a story/video job, does not consume quota and does not occupy a sample slot; profile-sharing allows only allowlisted recipient/profile/version and revoke blocks new requests.
4. A release tag `vX.Y.Z` promotes the same artifact digest that passed staging, without building a different artifact; after promotion, check health, API readiness, safety gates and the smoke suite.
5. If deployment or smoke/health fails, stop promotion, keep or restore the most recent good digest, check migration compatibility/rollback, and record the commit, digest, failing step and rollback status.

The gates below are requirements for the future deployment pipeline; they do not assert that CD is configured/running:

1. Format/lint/type checks for changed modules and a consistency check of generated contracts.
2. Unit/domain/contract suites, boundary tests per the approved feature plan.
3. Dependency/secret/container-image scans and the repository security validator.
4. Migration on a disposable PostgreSQL from the source baseline; check indexes/constraints, reconciliation, rollback/feature-flag path.
5. Android/web build; verify that the client bundle contains no credentials/provider endpoints.
6. Compose smoke/readiness, restart persistence, synthetic local end-to-end when the workstream is approved.
7. Feature-flag release; check migration parity, rollback, dashboards/alerts before enabling traffic.

This is the target release flow used to design and write acceptance scenarios. It does not assert that a CI run, build, CD, deployment or migration has been performed; does not choose a production host/region/registry; and does not close the owner/policy gates.

### 25.6 Client/runtime version baselines to evaluate

- apps/mobile pins React Native 0.87: the repository baseline, not the latest upstream version.
- React Native 0.88 upstream was released on 2026-10-19. Upgrading from 0.87 requires a separate compatibility review for apps/mobile, React/Metro, native modules, Android/iOS tooling and CI; do not change the pin on your own.
- apps/teacher-ui pins Vite 8.3: the existing baseline; upgrading requires a compatibility/security review per the lockfile and dependency set.
- Compose uses PostgreSQL 17 as the local baseline; PostgreSQL 18 must be evaluated at the migration/deployment gate, and the major version must not be changed on your own in this document.

Official upstream sources: [React Native 0.88 release (2026-10-19)](https://reactnative.dev/blog/2026/10/19/react-native-0.88), [React Native release overview](https://reactnative.dev/releases/), [PostgreSQL supported versions](https://www.postgresql.org/support/versioning/), [PostgreSQL current release notes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/release.html), [Vite releases](https://vite.dev/releases). The sources confirm upstream as of the review time; the repository pin remains the baseline until a separate upgrade review.

<a id="srs-26"></a>
## 26. Monitoring, alerting and operational objectives

### 26.1 Mandatory metric catalog

| Metric name | Example indicators |
|---|---|
| API | Request count/latency by route template/status, authentication errors, 4xx/5xx rate |
| Queue | Queue depth, age of the oldest job, enqueue/start/finish, worker heartbeat |
| AI job | Latency/errors by stage, attempt count, stale result, provider timeout, Modal GPU seconds/concurrency |
| Teacher Sketch | Number of deck/step jobs, latency, coverage/schema/safety rejection, stale/regeneration and aggregate compute usage; no child/profile label and not added to the video quota |
| Video/quota | Number of scene attempts, decode/format/safety check results, generation/regeneration, grant/admission/consumption/exhaustion by generic owner scope type, top-up and callback reconciliation; child/profile is not used as a label |
| Storage | Bytes/objects by generic asset type, upload/download errors, deletion lifecycle backlog |
| Billing | Callback verification errors, duplicate callbacks, invoice reconciliation latency, renewal results, notification delivery status |
| Security | Rate-limit events, authorization denials, abnormal authentication failures |
| Runtime | Container health, DB connection pool usage, Redis/MinIO/OTel collector status |

Grafana provides separate dashboards for API/job/service health, Modal usage by stage, video retries, Sketch deck/step cost-cap, storage lifecycle, payment reconciliation and notifications. Sketch usage and video quota have separate metrics/dashboards; billing/media dashboards must not allow drill-down by child profile.

### 26.2 Log and trace fields

Record only structured JSON logs with a correlation ID and opaque resource IDs. Traces/metrics use aggregate values, with no child/profile identifier or free text. Permitted telemetry fields include route template, status, duration, stage, retry number, non-secret provider/model profile ID, GPU class, aggregate values, correlation ID and opaque run/job ID within a controlled retention period. Do not log child names, profile context details, image/audio bytes, transcript/story/prompt, credentials, signed playback URLs or health/support-needs notes. Sampling must not skip the redaction step.

### 26.3 Alert conditions for local demo

| Condition | Action |
|---|---|
| API readiness fails or 5xx errors exceed the configured threshold | Stop generating new demo traffic, check dependency health and the correlation ID |
| Worker heartbeat is lost or a job is older than the alert deadline | Check worker/Redis/outbox; do not fix business rows on your own |
| LTX queue/concurrency exceeds the configured maximum, the queue is full or a video job takes longer than the alert deadline | Stop accepting new work when saturated; safely display AI_CAPACITY_BUSY |
| Configured Modal usage threshold exceeded by GPU seconds/bytes staged/runs per day | Hard cap prevents accepting/dispatching more work; stop scenes not yet run, record a safe error and an aggregate alert; do not exceed the budget, keep the quota already admitted |
| Safety gate returns indeterminate or frame/audio/subtitle coverage is missing | Block playback; return to the script review step; emit a redacted operator event |
| A Modal scratch prefix is unreferenced for more than 24 hours | Run idempotent cleanup; alert if the backlog grows |
| Stale result rate rises suddenly compared with baseline | Check duplicate dispatch/version churn, without viewing user content |
| Payment callback has an invalid signature | Record a security event; a valid callback not reconciled within 5 minutes is a warning |
| Lifecycle deletion pending exceeds the configured retry horizon | Show the queue count to the operator, without showing content |
| Local storage volume exceeds the configured warning threshold | Block new uploads before storage runs out |
| Sustained spike in rate-limit events | Report aggregates by route/bucket class |

Alert thresholds and paging recipients must be tuned after synthetic benchmarks; the hard GPU-second cap/request and cap/day are mandatory configuration before live video. Before Billing accepts a request, the application must reserve enough maximum budget for the request through the AI Policy port; if the reservation is missing, return `AI_COST_LIMIT_REACHED` before admission, without recording quota usage. After admission, the reservation must protect the request from the normal daily cap; the circuit breaker closes new admission and the worker must not spend beyond the reservation. An emergency safety/operator abort may stop a running request; per Q128 the admitted quota is still not refunded, this is an accepted residual risk and must be explained before confirmation. The worst-case structural bound is 15 scenes × 3 attempts = 45 scene attempts/full-video request, at most 135 scene attempts/run; the reservation and cap must be computed to exactly this bound, not confused with scene retries outside the request cap. The global/owner-scope circuit breaker rejects new requests when the reserved budget is exhausted. GPU-second/USD figures are not finalized until synthetic benchmarks.

### 26.4 Operations dashboards

Grafana needs separate panels for service readiness, API latency/errors, auth denials, rate limiting, queue depth/age, jobs by stage, model/GPU seconds, scene retries, output validation, storage/lifecycle, payment reconciliation and email/in-app delivery. Separate the billing dashboard from the child media dashboard; no dashboard has drill-down by child profile.

<a id="srs-27"></a>
## 27. Operations runbook

These procedures are documentation goals; they do not permit deployment or provider operations in this task.

### 27.1 Local startup and health check

1. Confirm that Docker Engine is ready and that no production provider secrets are loaded.
2. Start the stateful dependencies and application services using the documented Compose profile.
3. Wait for PostgreSQL/Redis/MinIO readiness before starting the API/worker; do not rely only on the container status "running".
4. Run the Alembic head check and initialize the synthetic demo fixture in a separately approved implementation task.
5. Open the API health/readiness, Prometheus targets, and Grafana dashboard. Confirm that Mailpit receives only aggregated billing notices.
6. Connect only the Android emulator and the Teacher web to the local API; verify that clients do not call Modal or MinIO directly.

### 27.2 AI job is stuck or has failed

1. Look up by correlation ID/job ID and stage; do not look up by child name or prompt.
2. Check the durable state of ai_jobs, attempt count, outbox, worker heartbeat, Redis connectivity, and Modal provider health.
3. Based on the safe error code, determine whether the error is retryable, user-correctable, or terminal.
4. Do not manually edit story/job database rows. Use an application repair command with idempotency and audit.
5. If scene retries are exhausted, keep the run FAILED and explain the next safe step. A Teacher may create a new run for the occurrence only if no playable sample has passed the safety gate.
6. If an accepted video request fails or is safety-blocked, follow the bounded request cap and return to script review. If a playable video already exists but is unsuitable, do not reset the counter or invoke a scene retry outside the flow.

### 27.3 Modal/provider outage

1. Stop or delay new jobs according to the queue backpressure configuration; keep durable jobs/outbox in PostgreSQL.
2. If a usage/safety/transfer/queue alarm requires it, enable the operator kill switch to stop accepting new video jobs; do not delete durable job state.
3. Display a safe provider-unavailable status and the expected retry guidance.
4. Do not change the model/provider on your own, silently reduce quality, or expose credentials to clients.
5. On recovery, resume idempotently and check the input hashes together with the current authorization/assignment version before accepting results.
6. Reconcile and purge the Modal scratch prefix per attempt; record aggregated duration/failure/GPU class, and do not record content payloads.

### 27.4 Payment callback discrepancy

1. Verify the callback signature and provider event ID before any state transition.
2. Check the provider, event receipt, payment attempt, and invoice using opaque refs.
3. If the signature is invalid, reject and record a safe security event; do not activate capacity.
4. If the callback signature is valid but the callback is a duplicate, return the provider acknowledgement without updating the invoice/capacity again.
5. If the provider status and the internal invoice differ, keep capacity unchanged and reconcile through the approved adapter; do not mark as paid based on a redirect or a user screenshot.
6. Send success/failure notices only from a reconciled state.

### 27.5 Storage or deletion backlog

1. Check MinIO health, volume capacity, the lifecycle queue, and the safe failure code per object.
2. Stop accepting new uploads when storage is degraded or the disk is about to reach the configured threshold.
3. Keep the deletion request pending until the required object refs are reconciled; retry idempotently.
4. Do not return internal object keys in client errors.
5. Escalate cases of unclear shared references to owner review; do not purge a source that may still be retained.

### 27.6 Backup and restore

1. Back up PostgreSQL using a consistent method and back up MinIO object data/metadata as one matching set.
2. Record the backup timestamp, schema revision, object-store inventory/checksum summary, and synthetic-demo marker.
3. Restore into an isolated local environment; verify table counts, migration head, object hashes, and asset lineage.
4. Run only synthetic read/playback and job-status checks; do not send restored media to Modal on your own.
5. Record the restore time and discrepancies in the feature's own separate evidence.
6. Production recovery point/recovery time objectives, encryption keys, offsite copies, and the retention schedule require a separate operations plan.

<a id="srs-28"></a>
## 28. Verification strategy and review scenarios

This section specifies the evidence to be produced in the future; it does not assert that this documentation task has written/run tests.

### 28.1 Verification layers

| Layer | Evidence required later |
|---|---|
| Domain | Coverage of state transitions/invariants for a single child, active-run serialization, class enrollment, sample uniqueness, rewrite/video caps, billing grace, and deletion |
| Contract | Valid/invalid round-trip fixtures for OwnerScopeRefV1, StoryRunCommandV1, AiJobStatusV1, ScriptRevisionV1, PaymentEventV1, and the error envelope |
| Application | Fake-port tests for authorization, transaction/idempotency, stale job completion, deletion orchestration, and provider failure |
| Repository/migration | Temporary PostgreSQL from the source baseline; check partial indexes, row/hash reconciliation, and rollback via feature flag |
| Adapter integration | MinIO upload/hash/delete, Redis/RQ redelivery, Firebase token verification, Modal sandbox/synthetic flow, MoMo/VNPAY sandbox callbacks |
| API | Role/resource access matrix, request limits, stable error mapping, pagination, idempotency, and no content leakage |
| Client | Parent Android permissions/return flow; Teacher browser camera/upload, status polling, safe errors, accessibility, and no batch action |
| Synthetic end-to-end | Parent one-child run and Teacher occurrence sample from input to playable MP4, including script review and adult confirmation |
| AI evaluation | Vietnamese Whisper quality, VLM subject accuracy/uncertainty, story schema/safety, character/color/style anchors, subtitle sync, video decode/format, Teacher Sketch activity coverage/line steps/age fit, and GPU/latency/cost |
| Security/privacy | Secret scan, log/trace redaction, cross-scope denial, signed playback expiry, upload/archive parser bounds, and provider payload allowlist |
| Operations | Compose health/restart persistence, queue recovery, backup/restore, payment event replay, and lifecycle deletion recovery |

### 28.2 Minimum acceptance scenarios

| Scenario | Expected result |
|---|---|
| Parent submits two profile IDs in one run | 422 MULTI_CHILD_NOT_SUPPORTED; no run/job/media is created |
| Parent opens two runs concurrently | One run succeeds; the other receives 409 ACTIVE_RUN_EXISTS |
| Teacher confirmation request references a child who is not actively enrolled in the assigned class | Authorization denied; no story run is created |
| School Manager requests media of a Teacher run | Denied, without revealing private run content |
| Teacher assignment is revoked after a run is created | Subsequent profile/story/playback views are revoked; audit metadata records the event |
| Teacher imports an encrypted lesson or a scan-only PDF | The encrypted file is rejected; the scan returns OCR_NOT_SUPPORTED; no OCR provider is called |
| Teacher edits the extraction | A new immutable text revision is created; the old plan/run hash is unchanged |
| Teacher selects a custom outdoor activity but the safety checklist is missing | The plan is not approved and no run can be executed |
| AI returns output for an old profile/context/script hash | The job becomes stale; the current story head is unchanged |
| Parent has used all three rewrites | The fourth rewrite returns STORY_REWRITE_LIMIT_REACHED |
| Adult confirms script revision A and then creates revision B | A video request using revision A is rejected as stale |
| A video completes and the adult marks it as unsuitable | The same run returns to script review; a new confirmed revision is required before regeneration |
| A scene fails on the first attempt and on both retries | The run stops safely; no silent retry beyond the limit |
| A lesson has embedded instructions requesting to expose secrets or change the safety policy | Treated as lesson content; no system/provider secret is exposed, and the safety policy still takes precedence |
| Story output contains a diagnosis, harmful instructions, or private profile data | Blocked before the script/video job is displayed; only a safe policy reason is recorded |
| The composited video has unsafe frames/audio/subtitles or the validator is insufficient | No playback token/durable final asset is issued; set safety blocked, return to script review, and record only safe policy metadata |
| Provider times out after receiving the LTX request but the result is unclear | The job enters AWAITING_RECONCILIATION; no duplicate generation is created before the status is checked/the operator handles it |
| Teacher run fails before any playable video has passed safety | The run ends as a terminal failure; a new run for the same occurrence may use the remaining free sample slot |
| Teacher occurrence has a playable sample that has passed safety | Another independent run is rejected; quality regeneration must stay within the same run |
| Temporary test assumption — Teacher occurrence has an aggregated roster but no eligible child | The temporary design scenario uses `SAMPLE_NOT_AVAILABLE`; no profile/child details are returned, no story/video job is created, no quota is consumed, and no sample slot is occupied. The eligibility/consent rule, reselection, or child-selection evidence is not finalized. |
| Teacher assignment is replaced while a Modal scene is running | A valid job is not cancelled merely because the owner changed; completion is checked against the run/input/script/occurrence hash and the current transfer receipt. A result that no longer matches is marked stale and scratch is purged |
| A valid provider callback arrives as a duplicate | Acknowledged idempotently; the invoice/capacity changes only once |
| Payment callback signature is invalid | Payment stays pending/failed; capacity is not increased |
| Renewal grace expires | Creating new classes/runs is restricted; existing data is still retained and accessible |
| Deleting a run while a video job is running | A cancel request is sent; a late result is discarded/deleted; the run shows its lifecycle status |
| Parent deletes a profile | The profile is hidden immediately; the lifecycle starts for related runs/context/media; profiles in other Teacher scopes are not affected |
| Log review after a synthetic run | No child name, transcript, image, prompt, support note, token, or signed URL is found |

### 28.3 Requirement verification responsibility

Each feature implementing a module stores its evidence in its own feature folder. Cross-feature integration evidence records the contract version and fixture hash; it is not aggregated into a shared evidence store that lacks traceability. Product acceptance must not be inferred from a successful PDF render or an architecture review.

<a id="srs-29"></a>
## 29. Risk register and implementation gates

**Qualitative rating scale:** Probability `L/M/H` = low/medium/high; impact `M/H/C` = medium/high/critical. This is the documentation team's initial assessment and must be confirmed by the functional owner before implementation; it is not a statistical probability.

| ID | Risk | Probability | Impact | Role owner | Mitigation / status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-01 | LTX does not maintain character consistency across multiple scenes | M | H | AI lead | Synthetic POC with a fixed evaluation set, character anchors, retry limits; adults can return to script review |
| R-02 | Modal cannot access local MinIO or the transfer/region is unsuitable | M | H | Infra + Privacy reviewer | Bounded transfer via backend/isolated scratch, checksum, cleanup; synthetic only until region review |
| R-03 | LTX runtime/model revision/license changes or is incompatible | M | H | AI lead | Pin model/runtime/CUDA/license and provenance before promotion |
| R-04 | ASR/VLM/LLM/TTS models do not meet Vietnamese quality | M | H | AI lead | Synthetic benchmark against a rubric; provider ports; no silent fallback |
| R-05 | A snapshot containing sensitive notes is sent to an external model | M | C | Privacy + Data owner | Minimal allowlist, support-needs excluded by default, preflight/redaction evidence |
| R-06 | Reassignment races with a running run/job, the current-owner lock, and old permissions | M | H | Backend/BE1/Story owner | Durable two-owner transfer state, immediate old-access fence, no-content during pending, Story lock receipt before finalize; do not cancel valid jobs; admin emergency force-close metadata-only if conflict; reconcile transfer idempotently |
| R-07 | Two class-creation requests at the same time exceed capacity | L | H | Billing + BE1 owner | Entitlement transaction, lock/serializable constraint, idempotency |
| R-08 | Payment callback is duplicated, forged, or out of order | M | H | Billing owner | Verify signature/freshness, unique provider event, state machine, reconciliation; a redirect is not evidence |
| R-09 | Lesson file/parser suffers zip bomb/malware/resource exhaustion | M | H | Security + Backend | Quarantine AV, parser sandbox, byte/page/entry/ratio/time limits, fail closed |
| R-10 | Legacy ID is misinterpreted as a new run/profile | L | H | Data owner | Legacy read-only; do not map a legacy session ID to a story run; migration crosswalk |
| R-11 | Source-derived table target count is mistaken for the live database table count | M | H | Data owner | Distinguish v2.3 baseline 89/137 from Rev31 implementation crosswalk 92/140; both are source-declared; inventory the live schema before migration |
| R-12 | Real child data enters the demo/provider before a policy exists | L (due to gate) | C | Product + Privacy/legal | Synthetic-only; block real release until purpose/authorization/transfer/retention/DSR is approved |
| R-13 | Email/push provider outage slows billing/run notices | M | M | Operations | Inbox is authoritative, outbox + retry are bounded, dashboard; payment/run state does not depend on delivery |
| R-14 | Schema/table ownership drift during implementation | M | H | Data owner + Architect | Every change must update the SRS/register/ADR and migration plan before merge |
| R-15 | Production cost, availability or SLA is inferred from the local demo | H | H | Product + Commercial + Operations | No price/SLA commitment from the demo; separate benchmark, host selection, ADR and approval |
| R-16 | Lesson/output model causes dangerous content or information disclosure | M | C | Safety/AI lead | Untrusted input, versioned safety gate, structured output, script confirmation, synthetic adversarial corpus |
| R-17 | Q127 retains the sample slot after the first video, but every regeneration fails and no usable video remains for the occurrence | M | H | Product owner + Teacher workflow owner | Decision Q127 is final; warn before claim, show the non-recovery policy, monitor the rate; do not open the slot automatically |
| R-18 | Purchased/granted quota is lost even though provider/safety/cost cap does not produce a video (Q128) | M | H | Product + Billing + AI lead | The no-refund decision is retained; before admission, sufficient worst-case GPU budget must be reserved; display the units to be charged before confirmation; emergency abort remains an accepted risk |
| R-19 | Runs that are `COMPLETED`/`FAILED`/`ABANDONED` and media retained until the user deletes them may be stored indefinitely | H | C | Privacy/legal + Product | Accepted only for the synthetic demo; production is blocked until maximum retention, backup expiry, deletion evidence and DSR policy exist; idle closeout retains content per Q133 |
| R-20 | Android push does not work on most devices or requires an uncommon distributor/provider | H | M | Mobile + Architecture | Inbox/email is the standard fallback; the Android provider is only a POC gate; do not use FCM per the repo rule; do not promote delivery before it is proven |
| R-21 | A self-built long saga with outbox + RQ + poller is harder to operate than a workflow engine | M | H | Architect + Operations | An ADR comparing Temporal before implementation; evaluate crash recovery, timers, cancel/compensate, cost and burden |
| R-22 | Cost per accepted video does not match the quota/allowance price | H | H | Commercial + AI lead | Benchmark GPU seconds/output/storage; configure included/top-up; hard admission reservation and daily circuit breaker before the live demo |
| R-23 | E-invoicing, VAT, consumer policy or provider recurring differ from assumptions | M | H | Commercial + Legal + Billing | No production payment collection before legal/accounting review and merchant sandbox/approval |
| R-24 | A sample video of one child shown to the whole class creates a perception of favoritism or exposes out-of-scope data | M | H | Product + Education reviewer | Separate confirmation per occurrence, no reuse for members, no identity added to the payload; review the display scenario before production |
| R-25 | Large uploads are interrupted, app retries are blocked, or a partial asset is used by mistake | M | M | Mobile + Backend + Infra | Durable `core.upload_sessions`/`core.upload_parts`, 4 MiB object PutObject chunks, byte/request/session caps, checksum/AV gate, final low-level PutObject; OI-18 POC before integration |
| R-26 | Teacher Deck misses an activity, or the SVG Guide draft is unclear/not age-appropriate, unsafe, or retained longer than expected | M | H | AI lead + Teacher/Parent Product/Design + Privacy | Catalog/structured-vector contract, adaptive 4–8 ordered steps, line-art/media/malware/age-safety checks, adult/Teacher review, automated safety, private temporary asset expiry/cleanup, separate cost/rate caps, synthetic quality/license/render/cost POC OI-19; do not use child profile/artwork as input |

### 29.1 Gate before live synthetic end-to-end

- Pin a compatible LTX inference workflow, the GPU image and the current dependency versions.
- Benchmark the Modal L40S video job and the small model stages on L4 using self-created/test-only assets.
- Benchmark the Teacher Sketch provider/model on synthetic lesson/activity fixtures; check coverage, line-step quality, age fit, safety, license, latency and compute cost before enabling deck generation.
- Verify bounded upload/result transfer between the local backend and Modal.
- Create profile, image, audio and lesson fixtures that are synthetic only.
- Verify there is no provider/API credential in the client bundle or source control.
- Verify domain constraints, the run state machine, the script approval gate and sample uniqueness before enabling the UI flow.

### 29.2 Gate before using real data or production

- Determine in writing which role the school/organization and the platform hold for each purpose; sign an appropriate data processing agreement, determine the lawful purpose/authorization/guardian role, privacy notices and who receives data subject requests (DSR).
- Design the process for receiving, verifying, tracking and fulfilling rights of access/correction/deletion/copy where applicable; do not infer statutory deadlines in the SRS, and leave the legal owner to finalize them under current law and the controller/processor role.
- Have an incident/breach runbook covering detection, containment, evidence, escalation, communications and notification milestones confirmed by counsel; do not hard-code legal deadlines unless they have been reviewed.
- Prepare a data-flow/processing inventory for Firebase Auth, Modal, payment, email, push, object store, logs/backups; assess cross-border data transfer, processing region, provider contracts and the copy deletion mechanism before transmitting real data.
- Finalize maximum retention per record class (profile, media, transcript, lesson, run, billing, audit/log, backup); determine backup expiry, purge/crypto-shredding, deletion receipt and exception/legal hold before real-data enablement.
- Perform the DPIA/impact assessment/record process as required by applicable law and counsel's decision; the SRS does not itself conclude which type of organization is exempt or must perform it.
- Review Law No. 91/2025/QH15, related child protection documents and the latest implementing guidance with legal experts. Official sources are listed in §15; the links are only verification sources, not legal opinion.
- Review Google Play Families/target-audience, privacy disclosures, child-safety content, permissions, SDK/provider policy and the listing before Play publication; approve the store-specific implementation separately.
- Choose production hosting/region/network/object store, secret manager, observability/alert path, email provider and backup/restore objective.
- Obtain merchant sandbox/production approval and verify the exact MoMo/VNPAY recurring callback/signature contract.
- Finalize commercial prices, e-invoicing/VAT, tax/invoice handling, rounding/day-count proration rules, cancellation/recovery/refund disclosures and customer support operations with legal/accounting.
- Review the proposed half-up VND proration formula before real charging; configure tax/discount separately if applicable.
- Define Parent registration/account recovery, Teacher invitation/activation, organization bootstrap and the exact role-granting UX.
- Perform a live DB schema/row inventory, staged migration rehearsal, backup/restore, reconciliation and rollback evidence.
- Approve a separate implementation plan, with a named owner and a clear integration allocation.

The v2.3 target total, as in §1.1 and DATA_TABLE_REGISTER.md, is 89 active tables (38 retained/refactored + 51 new) and 48 legacy/read-only source tables, for an expected physical total of 137 according to the source declarations. The six retained baseline business tables are `billing.video_quota_usage`, `notifications.push_endpoints`, `core.upload_sessions`, `core.upload_parts`, `lesson.sketch_deck_revisions` and `lesson.sketch_deck_steps`; the 11 logical entities of the foundation layer are added in §14.1 of DATA_TABLE_REGISTER.md. 13 AI Policy source tables are deferred: 12 provider-copy registries and `resource_receipt`; the resource event contains the idempotency receipt for new writes. Four helper/snapshot tables are removed: job subject refs already have the target StoryRun; job result refs belong to the Story/Lesson aggregate; the context/item snapshot is stored compactly, with schema/hash/limits, in `story.run_inputs`. No diagram or PDF is included in this extended version.
